# Negi Web And Admin Architecture

This document describes how the web shell, admin APIs, and browser runtime fit together for day-to-day maintenance.

## Responsibilities And Boundaries

- `g3ku/shells/web.py` owns web runtime startup and binds the backend service into HTTP routes.
- `g3ku/web/frontend/*` owns browser-side rendering, interaction logic, and shell state presentation.
- `main/api/*` and `g3ku/runtime/api/*` own backend contracts consumed by the frontend.
- The browser shell should present backend state; authoritative project/runtime state remains backend-owned.

When debugging behavior, first identify which side owns the state transition:

- If the issue is display text, interaction wiring, or DOM updates, start in `g3ku/web/frontend/*`.
- If the issue is data shape, status lifecycle, or permissions, start in API/runtime services.

## Frontend Script Layout And Test Loading

The console is a classic-script page: no bundler, no ES modules. `org_graph.html` is the single load order, and every script shares the global lexical scope, so a module can only see a binding another script declared before its first call, not before its own load.

- `org_graph_app.js` still owns the `S`/`U` registries and the top-level `bind()`, which only calls the per-surface binders; the wiring itself lives in those binder functions. It consumes the modules below; it does not redefine their entry points.
- Shared modules, in page order: `ui/toast.js` (`showToast` / `closeToast`), `ui/confirm.js` (`openConfirm` / `closeConfirm` / `acceptConfirm` / `requestInlineConfirm`), `ui/drawer.js` (`setDrawerOpen`: open state, `aria-hidden`, focus handoff and return), `ui/media-viewer.js` (`openMediaViewer` / `closeMediaViewer` / `triggerMediaDownload` / `bindMediaViewer`: the in-app media surface behind「In-App Media Viewer And Download Lane」), `ceo/model-panel.js` (composer model catalog, chain draft/drag, mode panel, chain saving), `ceo/composer.js` (draft cache, usage estimate, pending follow-up queue, attachment list, voice capture), `ceo/compression.js` (marker normalization, one placement resolver, live divider, manual poll, terminal transition), `ceo/feed-model.js` (message keys, render signature, turn id/usage and reasoning normalization), `ceo/feed-view.js` (upward paging, stick-to-bottom follow, scroll snapshots, rebuild view state), `ceo/sessions-panel.js` (visibility, unread exemptions, bulk actions, drag ordering, card rendering), `ceo/session-store.js` (snapshot normalization, cache persistence, cache render entry), `ceo/ws-router.js` (CEO frame table; each entry keeps its own session guard, while connection lifecycle stays in `initCeoWs`), `memory/store.js` (three-column load and load-more, polling, failure alerts), `memory/actions.js` (failed-record retry/discard, note save, browser uncompress/delete/save) and `memory/view.js` (column cards, note and detail previews, the memory browser and its dialogs). The model page keeps its role-chain state in `models/role-store.js` (scope resolution, alias remapping, catalog signature dedupe, `route_entries` ordering and drafts), its renderers in `models/role-view.js` and its load/edit/drag/group actions in `models/actions.js`; `org_graph_llm.js` stays a self-contained IIFE for the connection/binding editor. The resource lane keeps its state in `resources/store.js` (`ResourcesState`: skill/tool catalogs, selection, edit drafts, file cache and paging, plus `RESOURCE_PAGE_SIZES`); `org_graph_resources.js` is its view/action lane and reads no shared `S` keys. The store loads before `org_graph_app.js`, because the app's `S` literal and its pagination helpers use `RESOURCE_PAGE_SIZES` and its resource actions read `ResourcesState`. The task lane splits into four files that share the global scope: `tasks/tree-store.js` (snapshot node normalization, round selection, status labels and the execution-tree model), `tasks/tree-view.js` (the whole-tree render entry), `tasks/node-detail-store.js` (execution-trace normalization, stage/round status and live-frame projection), `tasks/node-detail.js` (the trace, message-delivery, spawn-review, context, error-history and model-retry renderers), `tasks/hall-store.js` (list patching, deletion, terminal reconcile and detail event dispatch) and `tasks/hall-view.js` (hall grid and token-reading render). They load before `org_graph_task_view.js` / `org_graph_tasks.js`, which keep the remaining DOM behavior; the view reads the store through plain function calls rather than an import.
- The remaining standalone lanes keep their own state and consume only the shared primitives: `org_graph_ceo_governance.js` owns the approval flow, governance mode and exec whitelist state (`S.ceoApprovalFlow` / `S.ceoGovernanceMode` / `S.execWhitelist`) and uses `showToast` plus `setDrawerOpen`; its whitelist dialog keeps its own polling and submit lifecycle. `org_graph_external.js` has no shared `S`/`U` references and uses `showToast` / `openConfirm`. `org_graph_bootstrap.js` is an IIFE with a local `U` registry and local state, resolves its DOM refs on `DOMContentLoaded`, and is deliberately absent from the `app` bundle.
- `client_api/core.js` owns the only request path (URL, timeout, abort, error-code normalization). `client_api/<domain>.js` declares endpoints and response normalization; `api_client.js` is the facade that extends `ApiCore` and mixes the domains in with `Object.assign`. The facade's method names and signatures are a contract for every call site.
- The frontend module directory is `client_api/`, never `api/`: `g3ku/web/main.py::serve_frontend_file` rejects every path under `/api/` for the backend namespace, so a module served from `api/` is a 404 and the page dies with `ApiClient is not defined`.
- Adding a frontend module takes two edits: put its `<script>` in `org_graph.html` at the call-order position, and list it in the matching bundle of `tests/resources/support/frontend_sources.js`. Bundles are subsettable and mirror the page order; `tests/resources/frontend_manifest.test.js` fails when a page script is unregistered, missing on disk, or out of order.
- Tests read source through `readFrontendSources([...]).join("\n")`, not by reading `org_graph_app.js` directly. Shared DOM stubs live in `tests/resources/support/frontend_stub.js`; suites keep their local overrides until their module lands.

## External Agent API Assembly

The channel-agnostic headless surface for third-party bridges is mounted alongside the web/admin routers:

- `g3ku/web/main.py` mounts `g3ku/runtime/api/external_v1.py` at `/api/v1`; the global bootstrap-lock middleware (423) applies to it like every `/api/*` route. Bearer auth and bridge isolation are owned by `require_external_api` (`g3ku/runtime/api/external_auth.py`).
- Turn execution reuses `SessionRuntimeBridge` (same semantic base as `/ws/ceo`); per-session SSE streams come from `g3ku/runtime/external_events.py` hubs.
- The shared outbound drain started by `ensure_web_runtime_services` routes `channel == "ext"` messages to external session event hubs (heartbeat/cron/task-terminal proactive push), registering each one in the durable external outbox before the hub publish (startup replay re-injects unacked entries). Any other channel has no consumer and is skipped with a warning.
- The web CEO catalog groups `ext:` and legacy `china:` rows via `is_channel_session_key`, and the row shape keeps two separate axes: `is_readonly` (history cannot be truncated/forked/renamed, and REST answers `channel_session_readonly`) and `can_message` (the composer may submit into the session). A registered `ext:` row is `is_readonly=true` + `can_message=true`: the web lane hands the input to the external turn service, which is what lets the reply reach the channel — gate and turn contract are owned by `external-agent-api.md`「回合契约」. `china:*` archives and registry-less ext rows stay `can_message=false`; ext rows are registry-driven, so an external session appears in the catalog as soon as its bridge registers it, before any transcript file exists.
- Channel rows additionally carry the delete-lane reading the session rail renders: `can_delete=true` enables the per-card「删除」, and `hides_on_delete` says whether that button only hides the card or also retires the external identity. Both come from one predicate (`web_ceo_sessions.channel_session_hides_on_delete`), which is also what the delete handler consults, so the confirm text cannot promise an outcome the backend does not perform. See「Channel Session Clear Contract」.
- The web UI「外部接入」page (nav `data-view="external"`, `g3ku/web/frontend/org_graph_external.js`) manages `externalApi.enabled` and bridge tokens through `admin_rest` `/api/external-api/*` endpoints; token plaintext is revealed exactly once at issue/regenerate time.

Full contract (endpoints, turn terminal invariant, event mapping, outbound routing): 详见 `external-agent-api.md`.

## Local Startup And Launcher Contract

- `negi.cmd` / `negi.ps1` / `negi.sh` are thin CLI passthrough wrappers around `negi_bootstrap.py`; with no arguments they default to `web`. `start-negi.*` remains the explicit double-click entry with its own managed-process handling.
- For the `web` command the bootstrap parent stays in the terminal as a readiness supervisor: it spawns the server child, polls `http://127.0.0.1:<port>/api/bootstrap/status` (a g3ku-specific endpoint so a foreign process squatting on the port cannot fake readiness), and prints a terminal banner: success with the clickable URL, or failure with the child exit code plus a pointer to `.g3ku/logs/console.log` (where the child's stdout/stderr are appended). A slow-start warning appears after the probe deadline instead of a false failure.
- That probe measures *the port is listening*, never *the runtime is warmed up*. uvicorn only creates the listening socket after the lifespan hands control back, so `g3ku/web/main.py::lifespan` schedules `ensure_web_runtime_services()` as a background task instead of awaiting it. Runtime progress lives in the same status payload: `runtime` (the per-service flags), `runtime_ready`, and `runtime_bootstrapping`, where `runtime_bootstrapping` means a coroutine is executing the startup body right now.
- The startup body runs once per process. Its gate is "the body completed at least once *and* the task service and heartbeat are still up" — it must never be derived from whether the runtime-services lock happens to be held, because the holder reads its own lock as held, so no caller inside the critical section can ever observe readiness. When that happens each queued caller re-runs the whole body instead of returning, the lock never gets an idle window, and `/ws/ceo` accepts connections without ever emitting the first frame: the transcript stays on `Loading …` while `/api/ceo/sessions` and `/api/tasks` keep answering normally. `/api/bootstrap/status` reporting `runtime.bootstrapping=true` together with `main_runtime_ready`/`heartbeat_ready`/`agent_ready` all true is the signature of that stall, and `console.log` shows the body's own tail (`queued follow-up boot replay: N session(s) scanned`) repeating every minute or two.
- Callers that actually need the runtime — the unlock endpoint, `/api/internal/*`, `/ws/ceo` — each await `ensure_web_runtime_services()`, so during a real warm-up they queue behind it instead of observing a half-built one. The queue must stay short: the tail of the body, `replay_queued_follow_ups`, turns every session that still holds pending user rows into a full model round (tens of seconds per session, far longer while the upstream rate-limits), so it is handed to an idempotent background one-shot (`_ensure_queued_follow_up_replay_running`) instead of being awaited inside the lock. Its per-session `await` loop stays serial by design — a fresh process firing N turns at once goes straight into provider rate limits.
- `g3ku/web/launcher.py::prepare_web_server_start` self-heals before acquiring the single-instance lock: it terminates leftover web-server processes of this workspace (cmdline `-m g3ku web` or `-c "...run_web_server_entrypoint..."`, scoped by the workspace venv python path or the process working directory, excluding itself). Starting therefore replaces a stale/zombie server of the same workspace; two web servers for one workspace still cannot coexist.
- The single-instance guard is `.g3ku/start.lock` (byte-range lock; metadata pid/port are written right after locking, so a holder killed between lock and write leaves an empty file and error messages may show `pid=unknown` — fall back to `netstat` on the web port when identifying squatters).
- The web port for banners and probes is read from `.g3ku/config.json` (`web.port`, default 18790). Non-`web` commands keep the plain passthrough behavior, and direct `python -m g3ku` still prints CLI help.

## Memory Management Page And Admin Contract

The browser shell has a top-level `记忆管理` page. It is a read-only operator surface for the queued long-term memory runtime, plus mutation surfaces (failed-batch retry/discard, current-memory edit/delete, note edit) that are guarded by in-app confirmation dialogs and `memory/admin_audit.jsonl` audit records rather than an environment flag; only the legacy `POST /api/memory/admin/retry-head` keeps the `G3KU_ENABLE_MEMORY_ADMIN_MUTATIONS` gate.

### Frontend Responsibilities

- The left rail exposes `记忆管理` as its own top-level navigation item, not a nested subsection of model configuration.
- The page shows three panels: unprocessed queue items oldest-first in real queue order (left column top), parked failed batches (left column bottom, `失败记忆`), and terminal processed batches newest-first (right column), including applied, precheck-failed and operator-discarded outcomes. The `失败记忆` panel is hidden while no parked records exist, so the normal page is the queue/processed pair. The two left-column panels split the column height 2:1 in favour of `失败记忆`: the pending queue is normally empty while parked records accumulate, and a percentage cap on the parked panel leaves its list viewport too short to hold a single card.
- Cards default to collapsed and stay compact; clicking a queue, processed or failed card opens a frontend-owned read-only detail modal, with bodies kept inside scrollable text regions. **This is where the payload-projection contract lives:** all three surfaces deliver a first screen only, and the drawer renders one block per queued request — its `request_id`, the character range actually shown, and a 取全文 control while characters remain. 取全文 appends the next screen into that same block rather than re-rendering it, so the reader's scroll position survives and every screen stays bounded. The reason is layout cost, not transfer: injecting one whole multi-megabyte body into the open drawer holds the main thread for seconds.
- A processed batch card's status slot renders one operation chip per distinct kind in that row's structured `changes`, in first-appearance order, each with its own icon and color (删除=red, 增加=green, 修改=amber, 更新 Note=violet, 记忆退场=amber, 归并记忆=teal, 摘除分类导航=red, 容量降级=crimson). 摘除分类导航 shares 删除's hue on purpose: both mean a row left the resident block, and the scope (a whole consolidation section) is carried by the label and by the detail block, not by a new color. 容量降级 is the machine's own capacity move and must not fold into 修改: nobody edited that memory, the ladder shortened how it renders, and each such change carries the characters it gave back (`freed_chars`, where `0` means the row was already at its summary length and was marked only to open the consolidation door). Only rows without parsable `changes` fall back to `op`/`source_op` and then `write_mode`, whose buckets merge deletes into rewrites and collapse several kinds into one value. A row with no effective change (`noop_reason`, `already_satisfied`, or a discarded outcome) shows the single `无变更` status badge and renders that reason in the drawer's 无变更原因 slot; a capacity park shows `已停车` instead and takes its reason from `discard_reason`, because a parked batch wrote nothing *and* is not a decision to write nothing. Chips appear only where something was actually written.
- Failed cards render red with a failure-category chip (`provider 瞬时错误` / `超窗拒发` / `协议违规` / `库容不下` / `归并未受理` / `写入形状故障`), the parked time, a right-side retry icon and a one-line error preview. The chip's tooltip states that record's retry policy, and for `超窗拒发` it names what has to change before a retry can differ (`载荷变小或模型窗口调大后手动重试`). The two capacity categories name the ladder's last rungs: `库容不下` says the batch never fit and its payload is held verbatim here, `归并未受理` says a consolidation ran clean and declined these rows - discarding that record is what re-arms consolidation for the same candidate set, so leaving it parked is a deliberate stop, not a forgotten failure. Clicking a failed card opens the same detail drawer with grouped meta (类别/停车与重试计数/累计成本/重试策略) and the full `error_history` (failures and requeue events in order) above the payloads; the drawer footer carries guarded 重试（重新入队尾） and 放弃 buttons, and 放弃 asks for a second in-app confirmation. Both actions raise a sticky progress toast with a spinning arc before the request goes out and replace it with the outcome afterwards; the arc is on the `prefers-reduced-motion` exemption list, because a frozen arc reads as a static circle and gives no progress signal on machines with that setting on. Those two POSTs use a 30 s client timeout instead of the client's default: each mutation rewrites the whole `ops.jsonl` ledger, so a shorter budget reports failure for an operation that already landed.
- In a processed batch detail modal, the eight meta fields (批次, 状态, 操作, 处理时间, 模型链, 请求数, 输入, 输出) render as one merged 基础信息 group, with 操作 listing the card's chip kinds joined in change order, and the 变更内容 section sits above the 原始请求内容 section. 模型链 reads `attempted_model_chain` — the refs this batch actually walked, last one being the model that answered — so a value with more than one entry is the degradation reading and a single entry means the chain head answered; the field's owner is `runtime-overview.md`「Chat provider 超时与重试边界」. A row carrying no traversal record answers `-`: printing the configured whole chain there would read as a walk that covered every position. The 变更内容 section renders the structured `changes` payload as one block per affected memory entry, each with its full untruncated content; rewrite entries render the original text and the modified text in a side-by-side comparison, and delete entries render the removed body. Rows whose blocks carry `original_missing` render an explicit `历史批次未保留原文`-style placeholder, and a row flagged `changes_reconstructed` shows a hint that its blocks were rebuilt from the lossy legacy preview.
- The page header's 查看记忆 button opens the memory browser drawer listing the memory rows still in the resident block (创建时间, 刷新值, 通过次数, 来源, ID, 记忆内容) — consolidated rows are not listed here at all, they are reachable from 归并记忆 instead. **The ID column is an operator handle only**: the block the model reads prints no ids, so both mutation requests translate the selected ids into `第 N 行` labels on the way into the queue (contract in `runtime-overview.md`「Memory Runtime State」), and an id that is not resident is described that way in the request instead of being silently dropped. The 记忆内容 column shows one ownership chip per row built from the `note_ref` column, so opening a note from the table never depends on parsing the memory text; historical payload views still turn legacy `ref:note_xxxx` / `见noteid:` spellings inside their stored text into chips. **The body column always shows `memory_body` in full, which is deliberately not what the model reads**: a row the capacity ladder downgraded renders only its summary line in context, so those rows carry a `memory-downgraded-chip` stating that, plus a per-row 回暖 action that posts to the un-compress endpoint. Both facts are true at once, and showing only one of them makes the table lie in the other direction. Sort toggles for 创建时间/刷新值/通过次数 and keyword search run browser-side over the fetched snapshot. The drawer header carries an 编辑 toggle next to 关闭: edit mode adds a leading selection column and a trailing 操作 column (per-row 修改 / 删除 / 回暖) plus a bulk bar (全选 checkbox, selected count, 删除选中); 修改 opens an in-drawer dialog for 记忆内容 and minimal_memory whose save asks for a second in-app confirmation. Delete (single or bulk) opens a dedicated confirm dialog. Notes appear there only when a selected row actually owns one (`note_ref`, de-duplicated across the selection): the dialog then prints the reclaim rule and lists those notes read-only, with 展开内容 fetching the note's text so the operator can review content before an irreversible delete. A selection that owns no note shows neither the rule nor an empty placeholder. The dialog holds no mutation control for notes. **The delete request carries memory ids only.** Note files are reclaimed by the ownership lane once the batch commits (contract in `runtime-overview.md`「Memory Runtime State」); unlinking one here would leave an ownership pointer at a missing note whenever the agent declines the delete. **Edit and delete are requests, not writes**: both post to the memory queue (`decision_source=user`, `trigger_source=admin_ui_edit` / `admin_ui_delete`) and the memory agent applies them, so the toasts say 已提交修改 / 已提交删除 and never promise a saved document or a synced mirror. Outside edit mode the drawer shows no mutation controls.
- The note window (opened from note chips anywhere) shows the note body and carries an 编辑 toggle: edit mode swaps the body for a textarea whose save asks for a second in-app confirmation and posts to the note update endpoint. The note-preview endpoint accepts either a real note name or the handle the resident block shows (the update endpoint still requires the real name), and chips from either spelling open the window, including inside a processed batch's 变更内容; openings from a processed (historical) batch detail pass a read-only flag that hides the edit toggle, so history views never edit notes. The note window stacks above the browser drawer and detail drawers (z-index 105/110 vs 100/96) so it is never obscured by the surface it was opened from. Note files are removed only by `reconcile-notes`: the memory-delete dialog hands checked refs to the memory agent as context and never unlinks a file itself, because a declined delete would leave the row owning a note that does not exist.
- 归并记忆浏览面的入口在「查看记忆」旁边，数据来自 `GET /api/memory/index-notes`：归并区每分类一行，列分类 note 名、常驻块里那个句柄、说明行与被归并的条目数。点开分类**不另开抽屉**——同一个 note 窗换成正文，顶部给一条面包屑（`归并记忆` 与一路点开的每一级），点任意一级回到那一级；正文里的 `(note: <句柄>)`、`note:<句柄>` 和旧标记 `ref:note_xxxx` 都渲染成下一级入口，所以嵌套关系能一路点下去。列表**保留** `unusable` 的分类（note 文件缺失、首行取不到说明文字，或首行超过常驻行的字数上限并标成"说明行 N 字，过长未进常驻块"）并标成异常行，而常驻归并区不渲染它们：两边的判据是故意不同的，浏览面上少一类比多一类更难查。这一层**整条只读**——列表与一路点开的每一级都不显示编辑开关，因为分类 note 没有管理面写路径（note 更新端点只服务 `memory/notes/`，对 `memory/index/` 回 404），留着开关就是点了必失败的假门；编辑单份普通 note 仍走上面那条从记忆行/队列点开的路径。
- Detail drawers and list panels preserve their scroll positions across the 15s auto-refresh: re-renders skip identical content and restore scroll offsets instead of resetting them, and the detail shell only re-orders its sections when the ordering actually changes (unconditional re-parenting resets the 变更内容 scrollbar on every poll).
- `ref:note_xxxx` text is a preview trigger: clicking a note ref opens the note window, which fetches and displays the note body and offers the guarded edit surface described above.
- All mutation controls read their enabled state from the API (`mutations_enabled` on `/api/memory/failed` and `/api/memory/current`, always true for these surfaces); the frontend still renders disabled states defensively if a backend reports false.

### Backend Responsibilities

- `GET /api/memory/queue` and `GET /api/memory/processed` return queue-owned runtime state and terminal batch records (applied rows plus durable discarded rows), each with pagination metadata. Both project payload bodies to a first screen (4000 characters): a queue row carries `payload_char_count` beside the projected `payload_text`, and a processed row carries `payload_char_counts` — one entry per `payload_texts` element in the same order, so "was this one cut?" is answered by `len(text) < payload_char_counts[i]` instead of a third parallel flag that could disagree with the two. Only the returned page is projected, and projection is read-side only: `memory/queue.jsonl` and `memory/ops.jsonl` keep the complete payloads, which is what requeue, snapshot rebuild and audit read. Terminal processed rows carry a structured `changes` list — one entry per add/rewrite/delete/note_upsert with `memory_id`, full `content`, and for rewrite/delete also `original_content`, plus one entry per note edit (`note_edit`, carrying `old_text`, `new_text`, `applied`, `error`) and per swept note file (`note_reaped`, carrying the reason the ownership went away). A section purge contributes one entry per retired row it removed (`category_purge`, carrying `note_ref` and that row's `original_content`) — the resident-only diff cannot show those rows, so this ledger is where the operator reads what actually left. The consolidation and category-purge contracts are owned by `runtime-overview.md`「Memory Runtime State」. Both note types get their own operation chip and their own detail block (an edit shows `原文` → `改为`, with the error text when the op did not apply; a reap shows why ownership went away; a purged row shows which category it was filed under, with its body) and never fall through to the add template — a record nobody can read is not a trace. The original bodies are fetched from the SQLite store inside the commit path before the mutation applies, so each terminal row records the before/after state of every affected memory.
- Legacy processed rows that predate the structured `changes` payload are reconstructed at read time: the read path parses their flattened `change_preview` (`新增：/修改 ID：/删除 ID/更新 note ref:` segments) back into per-memory blocks and marks the row `changes_reconstructed`. Original bodies of legacy rewrites/deletes were never captured and are not reconstructible, so those entries carry `original_missing` and the frontend shows a placeholder instead of a before-text. This enrichment is display-only; the persisted `ops.jsonl` row is never rewritten.
- `GET /api/memory/failed` returns parked failed records newest-parked-first with pagination metadata and `mutations_enabled` (always true for this surface). Each parked item's `payload_text` is projected the same way, with `payload_char_count` beside it; `memory/failed.jsonl` keeps the complete payloads, which is exactly what retry requeues. `POST /api/memory/failed/{failed_id}/retry` requeues a parked record under its original `request_id`s at the queue tail; `POST /api/memory/failed/{failed_id}/discard` writes the `operator_discarded` terminal row into `ops.jsonl` and removes the parked record plus any stale queue rows. Missing records answer `404 memory_failed_not_found`, records not in `parked` state answer `409 memory_failed_not_parked`.
- The matching body-paging routes are `GET /api/memory/failed/{failed_id}/payload`, `GET /api/memory/processed/{batch_id}/payload` and `GET /api/memory/queue/{request_id}/payload`, all served by one implementation. They take `request_id` (which queued request of that batch or parked record; empty means the first) and `start_char`, and answer one screen of 20000 characters where `start_char`/`end_char` describe the range **actually delivered**, alongside `char_count` and `truncated` — the same character addressing the content lane uses, sized for an operator surface rather than for a model reading a file. An unknown record, batch or request id answers `404 memory_payload_not_found`. A reader advances by the delivered `end_char`, never by its own request parameter: if the stored body disappeared (discarded or re-parked between screens), the next call returns no progress and the control closes instead of looping.
- `GET /api/memory/current` returns the memory rows **still living in the resident block** (memory id, body, source, refresh count, passed count, compression flag, timestamps) plus `mutations_enabled` (always true for this surface), and backs the memory browser drawer; rows that left via consolidation are excluded there — their text lives in the category note, which the 归并记忆 browser shows, while sqlite keeps the rows untouched. **`POST /api/memory/current/update` and `POST /api/memory/current/delete` are enqueue endpoints, not write endpoints**: they take the operator's ids, resolve each one to its current resident line through `memory_line_for_id()`, and post a natural-language request naming those lines through the same door the CEO tool uses (`decision_source=user`, `trigger_source=admin_ui_edit` / `admin_ui_delete`), returning `{status:"queued", request_id}`. The ids themselves never enter the payload — a reader of the block has no line-number-to-id table to consult, so handing the agent an id would be handing it a handle it cannot resolve. An id that is not resident is named as "已不在常驻块里", which the agent settles through `already_satisfied`; the memory agent still decides what actually lands, so a stale id is accepted here and refused there. No endpoint writes a memory row directly - `update_current_memory` / `delete_current_memories` and their row-level repository writers were removed, because a second door means a second set of gates (content scan, capacity ladder, terminal ledger, note ownership) and only the queue side had them. The delete payload names memory rows and nothing else; the endpoint accepts no note field, since note files are reclaimed by the ownership lane (`runtime-overview.md`「Memory Runtime State」) and unlinking one here would leave a row owning a missing note. `POST /api/memory/current/uncompress` is the one exception and it is not a content write: it clears a row's compression flag so the resident block shows its full body again, runs through the mirror's own document validation, and rolls the row back whole if the document would exceed the cap - the response carries `changed` plus `resident_chars_after` next to the trigger line and the cap. All three append audit records like the other mutation surfaces; the audit line for edit/delete records `result:"queued"`, i.e. who asked, not what changed.
- `GET /api/memory/notes/{ref}` is the note preview contract: it accepts a real note name or the handle the resident block shows — the token's prefix names the tree (`n` for `memory/notes/`, `c` for the category notes in `memory/index/`) — and `item.ref` always answers the **resolved real name**, because a preview opened by handle feeds the same ref into the update endpoint, which only accepts names. A missing note answers a clear not-found error. `POST /api/memory/notes/{ref}/update` writes a new body for an existing note under `memory/notes/` (non-empty, ref format validated) and appends an audit record; category notes in `memory/index/` have no write endpoint at all (the consolidator owns them) and answer 404 there, and there is no note delete endpoint.
- `GET /api/memory/index-notes` is the read-only consolidation list: one entry per distinct `consolidated_into` in first-appearance order, carrying `note_ref`, the resident `handle` (the `c`-prefixed token the block shows for that category), that category note's first line as `scope_line` (the resident row's text, written as `大类:关键词` and capped at 500 characters), `entry_count` (how many retired rows point at it), `scope_line_over_limit`, and `unusable` (missing category note, blank first line, or a first line over the cap — the last case is the only one the row itself can name). It shares the runtime's single derivation with the resident block and never enumerates `memory/index/` — a class whose retired rows are all gone disappears by itself. **The list carries no timestamp at all** (four columns: note name, resident handle, scope line, entry count): the date a retired memory line last really changed is stamped by the runtime onto that line inside the category note, so "when was this rule made" is answered by opening the note, not by a new column here (the date's owner and why the resident block carries exactly one of them live in `runtime-overview.md`「Memory Runtime State」). It writes no audit record and takes no mutation.
- `memory/ops.jsonl` is a rolling processed-history surface rather than an append-forever archive; the backend prunes processed rows older than 7 days, so `/api/memory/processed` is the latest 7-day operator history window.
- Guarded operator mutations on the memory surfaces append an audit record to `memory/admin_audit.jsonl`; successful calls report `audit_logged`/`audit_error` in the response. The legacy `POST /api/memory/admin/retry-head` alone treats a failed audit write as a hard error (rolls back and answers `503 memory_admin_audit_failed`).

### Maintenance Boundary

- If the memory page looks wrong but the raw JSON from `/api/memory/queue`, `/api/memory/processed` or `/api/memory/failed` is correct, debug `g3ku/web/frontend/*`.
- If a `ref:note_xxxx` chip renders but the preview drawer cannot load, compare the frontend `ApiClient.getMemoryNote(...)` request with `GET /api/memory/notes/{ref}` before debugging the memory runtime itself.
- An empty 归并记忆 list is usually the real state, not a broken lane: `GET /api/memory/index-notes` reports `total: 0` while no memory row carries `consolidated_into` (consolidation has not run yet). Rows that answer `404` when opened are missing category files — `negi memory doctor` reports the same classes under `broken_category_links`.
- If the page is missing fields, ordering, discarded statuses, structured `changes`, `change_preview`, or request-artifact links already in the API response, debug the admin endpoints or `g3ku/agent/memory_agent_runtime.py`.
- If a rewrite comparison lacks the original text, first check whether the row is flagged `changes_reconstructed`: for legacy rows the placeholder is expected because their originals were never recorded. For a row committed with structured `changes`, inspect the original-body capture in the commit path of `g3ku/agent/memory_agent_runtime.py`; originals are read before the mutation applies and are not reconstructible after the commit.
- A memory detail drawer that shows only the head of a payload with a 取全文 button is the read-side projection working, not data loss: the row's `payload_char_count` / `payload_char_counts` say so, and the complete body stays in `memory/queue.jsonl`, `memory/ops.jsonl` or `memory/failed.jsonl`. When 取全文 stops advancing, compare the response's delivered `end_char` against the requested `start_char` before debugging the frontend; no advance means the stored body changed under the reader (requeued, discarded or re-parked), not a paging bug.
- If the memory browser drawer fails to load, compare `ApiClient.getCurrentMemories()` with `GET /api/memory/current`. A `404` on that route means the running web process predates the endpoint and needs a restart; frontend files are served from disk with no-cache headers, so frontend-only edits need only a browser refresh.
- If the queue page is stuck on one `processing` batch, treat that as a backend/runtime issue first, not as a frontend pagination bug; processing failures park into `memory/failed.jsonl`, so an empty queue with entries in the failed panel is the provider-outage signature (parking and success-signal requeue semantics live in `runtime-overview.md`「队列状态机与失败停车语义」).
- Mutation controls on the memory surfaces are guarded by in-app confirmation dialogs and audit records, not by an environment flag; only the legacy retry-head endpoint answers `403 memory_admin_mutation_disabled` without `G3KU_ENABLE_MEMORY_ADMIN_MUTATIONS`. A disabled edit/retry affordance therefore points at `mutations_enabled` in the API response or the frontend defensive state, not at a missing env var.
- A 放弃/重试 toast that reads 失败 with the body `Request timeout` while the record is already gone from `memory/failed.jsonl` means the client gave up before the full-ledger rewrite finished — the mutation landed. Read the toast body before treating it as an endpoint fault: the other three failure bodies (`未找到对应的失败记忆记录…`, `该记录当前不在停车状态…`, `失败记忆操作未成功…`) each point at a different cause, and only the last one is a server-side error.

## Log Audit Page And Event Contract

The left rail carries a `日志审计` page: a read-only, single-panel surface backed by a structured event sink — one untitled terminal-style stream of every raw event, newest first, paginated at 100 events per page. The page keeps no per-subsystem classification, no 24h rollup, and no error-only surface: every event, errors included, lands in the one stream, and each row's source tag is derived from its `subsystem` field as it is rendered. `level` stays an API-side filter rather than a page control.

### Frontend Responsibilities

- The `日志审计` nav item carries the first in-nav unread badge (`#audit-nav-badge`, class `.nav-badge`): a red pill capped at `99+`, hidden at zero. The badge is always-on, not view-gated: a 30s poll of `GET /api/audit/events?limit=1&since=<lastSeen>` renders `total` as the unread count, so the badge is visible from every view.
- The lastSeen marker lives in sessionStorage under `g3ku.audit.last-seen.v1` (tab-scoped, no flush on unload). First visit (no stored marker) anchors it to the newest existing event timestamp, so historical events never light the badge. Opening or in-view-refreshing the audit page marks everything read (marker = latest known event timestamp, badge cleared). `since` is a strict string-greater comparison on server-side ISO timestamps, so events stamped at the exact read moment are not double-counted.
- The stream panel renders every event newest-first as one terminal-style line (system-local time | level | source | summary, monospace), with JSON-detail expansion per line. Timestamps are rendered from the stored offset-aware ISO value through `Date` into the operator machine's system-local `YYYY-MM-DD HH:mm:ss`; the raw ISO string stays in state untouched because the badge's `since` comparison is a server-side string comparison.
- The stream itself is the view's scroll container (`.audit-event-list` with `overflow-y: auto`), while the pager footer stays pinned; the panel takes the remaining view height (`flex: 1 1 auto; min-height: 0`), so a 100-row page is reachable with the wheel instead of being clipped. Quiet 15s polls preserve the list scroll offset so reading older rows is not interrupted; paging and the explicit `刷新` reset the list to the top. Each row carries its `event_id` as `data-audit-event-id`, and the poll repaint re-opens the JSON-detail `<details>` of exactly those ids: the repaint replaces the whole list through `innerHTML`, so both offsets and expansions are re-derived from the outgoing DOM rather than left to it.
- The panel is paged, not load-more: `AUDIT_PAGE_SIZE` is 100, the footer shows `第 X/Y 页 · 显示 A-B / 共 N 条`, and `上一页`/`下一页` drive `offset = (page - 1) * 100` and disable at the first/last page. The view refreshes every 15s while active — quiet polls reload the current page so paging is not reset under the operator, while the explicit `刷新` button and the first entry into the view reset to page 1 (newest). Out-of-range pages clamp back to the last page, so a trimmed ledger never leaves a blank page. When a quiet poll lands on a page other than the first, the newest-event timestamp is fetched separately so the read marker still tracks the true head.
- Memory failures are read-only here: their events appear inside the stream, while retry/discard lives in 记忆管理.

### Backend Responsibilities

- The sink is `g3ku/audit_events.py` writing one JSON object per line to `<workspace>/.g3ku/audit.jsonl`. Record fields: `event_id`, `timestamp`, `subsystem`, `level` (`info`/`warning`/`error`), `event_type`, `summary` (≤240 chars), `detail`. Retention: a trim pass kicks in once the file exceeds ~2MB and keeps the newest 2000 events via pid-suffixed temp write + `os.replace`; reads cap at the newest 4000 events and skip malformed lines. Appends are small single-line writes shared by the web process and the managed worker, so cross-process semantics are best-effort (at worst a few events drop during a rare trim, never corruption).
- `emit_audit_event(subsystem, level, event_type, summary, *, detail=None)` is the one-call emitter recipe: fire-and-forget, never raises, returns False when unconfigured. The sink is configured once in `make_agent_loop` (`g3ku/runtime/bootstrap_factory.py`), which covers the web process, CLI and managed worker; an unconfigured sink writes nothing (no cwd fallback, so provider-factory unit tests never pollute a workspace).
- Signal sources: `provider_chain_exhausted` (model-chain exhaustion, emitted once inside the `exhausted_model_chain_error` factory in `g3ku/providers/fallback.py`, covering both chain loops in `fallback.py` and `main/runtime/chat_backend.py`); `task_node_error` (from `TaskLogService.append_task_error_log`, the single chokepoint for all five task-node error writers); `task_terminal_failed` (from the terminal publish path, only when the final status is `failed`; success terminals stay out of the audit feed); `web_api_5xx` (an HTTP middleware in `g3ku/web/main.py` that captures ≥500 responses plus unhandled exceptions on `/api/` paths, with a `/api/audit` skip prefix so audit-endpoint failures never feed back into the pool); `memory_batch_parked` (from `MemoryManager._park_failed_batch`, carrying the failed_id, category and last error text). Memory events are read-only here — retry/discard keeps living in the memory view's 失败记忆 panel. `node_final_result_repaired` (`info`, from `ReActToolLoop._emit_final_result_repair_audit`) fires once per `submit_final_result` whose payload the runtime completed on the model's behalf, carrying the tally under `detail.repairs` with a bounded vocabulary: `enum_case:status` / `enum_case:delivery_status`, `delivery_from_status`, `line_to_int`, `line_null_dropped`. Aborted streams are not an audit source: they are settled inside one provider call by advancing the model chain (`main-task-runtime.md`「Node-Level Pause and Recovery」), so their count lives on the worker log's chain-fallback lines, and a `task_model_calls` row carries `stream_incomplete` only when the whole chain aborted. It exists because the normalized payload overwrites both the transcript copy and `task_node_tool_results.arguments_text`: with no event, the repair rate is unmeasurable after the fact, and the class it covers runs at ~3% of acceptance submissions, so hours of traffic are needed before "no error rows" means anything. All emitters swallow their own exceptions; a broken sink never disturbs the host flow.
- `GET /api/audit/events` takes `limit` (1–200, default 50), `offset`, `level`, `subsystem`, `since` and returns `{ok, items, total, has_more}` newest-first; unknown filter values simply match nothing. Read failures answer `503` with `detail.code` `audit_events_read_failed` (Chinese message in `api_client.js`).

### Maintenance Boundary

- Badge stuck or zero while events exist: check the frontend path first — the sessionStorage marker under `g3ku.audit.last-seen.v1` and the 30s badge poll (`refreshAuditBadge`), not the sink.
- The stream is empty or stuck on a blank page: compare `GET /api/audit/events?offset=<n>&limit=100` against the rendered page. The frontend clamps an out-of-range page back to the last page, so an empty list means the endpoint answered `total: 0` or the ledger was never written.
- The log list cannot be scrolled by wheel while the panel still shows a clipped page: the scrolling owner is `.audit-event-list`, not the panel (`.resource-detail-panel` is `overflow: hidden`, so programmatic scrolling still works and hides the mistake) — check that the panel keeps `flex: 1 1 auto; min-height: 0` and the list keeps `overflow-y: auto`.
- Rows slide upward while the numeric scroll offset is unchanged: a repaint that loses a row's expansion collapses one `detail` block, and a single JSON detail is tall enough to move everything below it by a screen. The scroll number is therefore not the evidence — check that the expanded row's `data-audit-event-id` is still matched by an open `<details>` after the poll.
- Displayed times look shifted: the row renders system-local time derived from the stored offset-aware ISO timestamp; compare the raw `timestamp` values from `GET /api/audit/events` before suspecting the sink.
- No `audit.jsonl` at all: the process never went through `configure_audit_sink` (bootstrap wiring in `make_agent_loop`) or the workspace root differs; restart the web process and check `.g3ku/`.
- A failure class exists in the system but has no audit events: no emitter covers it yet — add one `emit_audit_event(...)` call at the failure's chokepoint (the recipe above); the source tag renders from the record's `subsystem` string with no fixed registry change needed unless it deserves a Chinese label (add it to `auditSubsystemLabel`).
- `/api/audit/*` returns 503: the sink read path cannot resolve a workspace root (`audit_sink_unconfigured`) or the file read failed.

## Model Config Page And Admin Contract

The top-level `模型配置` page manages `llm-config` provider records and model bindings. Config source-of-truth, binding resolution, and secret handling are owned by `config-and-models.md`「llm_config 子系统」; this section covers the admin surface and the add/edit model workflow.

### Model-Role Route Specificity

- The browser's session brain-chain reorder action and the model-configuration page both persist role chains through the role-specific endpoint `PUT /api/models/roles/{scope}` (for example, `scope=ceo`).
- In `main/api/admin_rest.py`, register that route before the generic `PUT /api/models/{model_key:path}` binding-update route. Starlette matches routes in declaration order; if the generic path comes first, `roles/ceo` is treated as a model key and the binding facade returns `Unknown model key: roles/ceo` instead of updating the CEO chain.
- When debugging a failed chain save, inspect the browser request URL first, then confirm the route declaration order before changing model-key validation or frontend drag payloads.
- **The model page is rendered and fed by `org_graph_llm.js`, not by `org_graph_app.js`.** That module overwrites the globals owning this page at load: `renderModelCatalog`, `renderModelRoleEditors`, `renderModelList`, `renderModelDetail`, `openModel`, `startCreateModel`, `loadModels` and `handleModelRoleEditorAction` (the 保存 button). Its data comes from `GET /api/llm/bindings` and `PUT /api/llm/routes`, not from `GET /api/models` / `PUT /api/models/roles/{scope}`. Two traps follow. An app-side renderer added for this page is never invoked — it must be called from llm's `renderAll`, and its payload must flow through llm's save, otherwise the UI renders nothing while every JS test still passes. And `routes` on that endpoint is the flattened candidate view, so chain order and group membership come only from `route_entries` + `load_balance_groups`: llm converts them once in `llmRouteChains`, keeps the group map in its own state, and `projectRoutes` projects both into `S.modelCatalog` (edit-session drafts survive a refresh). Reading a chain from `routes` would silently turn one group slot into its individual members and write that back on the next save.
- `GET /api/models` carries two views of each role chain: `roles` is the flattened candidate view (load-balance group members expanded), `route_entries` is the ordered fallback chain, and `load_balance_groups` holds the group definitions (`model_keys` plus `max_retry_rounds`). The model page is three columns: load-balance groups | model list | role chains. **The four role chains stay one row of four — never wrapped**, so the page uses its own `max-width: min(100%, 1760px)` instead of the 1440px page cap instead of letting the new column squeeze the chain cards. Groups are configured in that left column as first-class objects — 新建组 opens a dialog (组名, member checkboxes, free-entry 每成员重试次数（默认 3，只挡负数，不设上限）) and a group row is dragged into a role chain the same way a model row is, which places one `group:<key>` slot in the chain. Both the column card and the chain card open that dialog when clicked anywhere on the card, so the group tile carries no drag handle: live model tiles have none either, and adding a 40px handle collapses the card's main area to zero width. Whole-card clicking never swallows a control nested in the card: in both containers the delegated handler tests the inner action (`[data-model-chain-action]` on a chain tile, `[data-group-delete]` on a column card) before the `[data-group-edit]` card target, because the remove/discard buttons live inside the element that also carries `data-group-edit` — testing the card first makes 移出链 and 删除 unclickable and pops the config dialog instead. A group is a single chain slot whose members are peers (the tile states that order inside a group carries no priority and that a node stays bound to one member for its lifetime), while a direct-only chain keeps the existing drag/remove UI unchanged. Member counts and per-member round budgets are deliberately not printed on either card. The group's compression-window alignment is the one reading this UI prints, and it lives on a single surface: inside the group dialog, as a live line **above** the member checkbox list, printed as `组内最大上下文对齐: <window>`, where that window is the smallest member's. The line switches to the warning tone when members disagree, and the group's largest window is hover text rather than a second number. Neither the chain tile nor the column card prints it — that coupling is only actionable while a member is being ticked. The value is derived client-side from the catalog `contextWindowTokens` — the admin API gains no field for it — because that smallest member is what the send-time compression ruler binds to, so adding one small-window binding to a group lowers the whole group's compression target and raising it lifts the group again (contract: `config-and-models.md`「Frontdoor Context Window Contract」).
- Group editing belongs to the same chain-edit session as the role chains: confirming the dialog enters that session, and one 保存 submits both. Renaming through the dialog rewrites the `group:<key>` tokens in every draft chain in the same pass, so a rename can never leave a chain pointing at a dead key; deleting a group removes its chain slots too. A dialog left unconfirmed touches nothing, and 取消丢弃 edits rolls the group drafts back with the chain drafts.
- A chain that contains a group is saved with `route_entries` plus the group definitions in the same request and never with `model_keys`: the backend treats the two as mutually exclusive (`route_entries wins`) and validates both together, so a group cannot be persisted while its referencing chain fails. The submit payload carries the **whole** group set (minus groups with no members, which the backend would reject and no chain can point at), not only the referenced ones — `models.loadBalanceGroups` is replaced per scope on save, so a subset payload would delete a group that was just created and not yet dragged into a chain, or one referenced only by a scope outside this request. When no chain uses a group at all, the set rides on the first group-capable scope (`execution`, else `inspection`).
- Group validation errors are surfaced verbatim: duplicate members inside a group, duplicate chain entries, a negative `max_retry_rounds`, a group key colliding with a binding key, and a chain referencing an unknown or disabled group all return a readable `400`. Deleting a binding that is the only member of a group is refused with the group and chains named, because silently emptying a group would remove load balancing from every chain that references it.
- `GET /api/models/load-balance/status` answers the worker's runtime view of the balancer (per-member running/waiting/reserved, rolling 60-second request count, decayed 429 penalty, score, node binding count, config revision). The only failure the balancer remembers across requests is upstream rate limiting: auth errors and disabled keys are reported nowhere here, because the model chain handles them in the request that hit them. It counts buckets, never bucket identity: endpoint/API-key material is only ever hashed inside the worker, and a non-zero `unresolved_bucket_count` means that process cannot resolve the key material at all, so the quota distribution is unknown rather than duplicate-free.
### Frontend Responsibilities

- The add/edit model modal keeps one provider-config JSON draft as its source of truth. Dedicated `请求地址` (`base_url`) and `Apikey` inputs stay two-way synced with that JSON draft. The form is three fields per row with no field owning a row of its own: `请求地址` / `Apikey` / `上下文窗口Token`, then the retry pair with `最大并发数`, then `深度思考（Reasoning Effort）` / `最大输出TOKEN` / `请求超时时间(秒)`. A new config opens with `上下文窗口Token` prefilled `200K` (= 200000) and the JSON draft seeded with the same 200000, so the two readings agree from the first render; the provider template's suggested window is deliberately **not** the form default (the backend and `add_model` signatures still carry no default — 详见 `config-and-models.md`「Model Request Parameter Defaults」). There is no manual binding-key (`模型ID`) field in create mode: the binding key is derived by the backend from the draft's `default_model`, so picking the model is what establishes its identity. Create mode opens with a required `配置名称` input (placeholder `请输入配置名称`) in place of the modal title; the name must not duplicate an existing config name (case-insensitive) and is saved into the binding's `name` field (see `config-and-models.md`「模型系统不是只靠 config.json」for the display-title priority).
- The protocol select is a header control in both create and detail modes and lists the provider templates, defaulting to `OpenAI Chat`. Its `协议` label is an invisible spacer, the same mechanism the `是否为图像多模态` field and the `获取模型列表` button use, so those boxes share one line and one control band without duplicated copy; the modal pins that band to the input height instead of the global select height. Choosing an entry rewrites only the draft's `provider_id`; `protocol_adapter` is derived from that template during normalization (详见 `config-and-models.md`「llm_config 子系统」), so there is no independently writable protocol field. Entered request address, Apikey and parameters survive the switch, while the draft's `display_name` and `default_model` do not follow the template — a `default_model` outside the new template's suggested list is tagged `custom_model` in `extra_options`. In detail mode the rewrite makes the draft differ from its loaded snapshot, so 保存修改 sends the config update — and saving sends no request to the provider at all.
- The `是否为图像多模态` checkbox is a control in that same row, not a separate widget size: it matches its neighbours' box (38px tall, `--ui-radius-control`, `--ui-border`, surface fill, 13px label) so the header grid reads as one line of fields. It still carries the checkbox on its right edge and keeps a smaller 34px variant where it is placed inline in the model row.
- The JSON region renders collapsed by default and auto-expands when draft validation or the connection probe fails.
- `获取模型列表` renders the provider catalog returned by the backend as a filterable list. Selecting an entry writes it into the draft's `default_model`, which is the fallback display title when no `name` is set. The button carries no printed caption — its label is the invisible spacer described above — and it fills the third slot of the create-mode header row (`协议` / `是否为图像多模态` / `获取模型列表`); in detail mode it stays on the row it shares with `是否为图像多模态` and `上下文窗口Token`.
- The model list left rail renders each binding as a compact card showing the display title (`name` > `default_model`) and the request address (`base_url`); the per-model `Chat`, `Enabled`, and role-chain-membership chips are not rendered. Editing a binding and changing `default_model` updates the stored record, so the fallback title follows the model; the binding key stays a stable identity and is not renamed.
- 模型页两列在 ≥1024px 按窗口高度铺到底：`.llm-page-layout` 取 `flex: 1 1 auto; min-height: 0` 吃掉头部之外的剩余高度，`.model-shared-list`、`.model-routing-grid`、`.model-chain-list` 一律 `flex: 1` 且不带 `max-height`，滚动只发生在列表内部，两列收在同一条基线上。这一页没有任何按 `vh` 写的封顶：容器、面板、列表各写一个上限时，窗口底部会留下一整条空白带，而头部一旦换行（最大轮数条展开时）容器就顶出 `.main-content` 的 `overflow: hidden`，最后一行再也滚不到。角色链的容器有两个渲染方（`org_graph_llm.js` 的 `.role-chain-section` 与 `org_graph_app.js` 的 `.model-role-section`），只给后者配 flex 列时模型页实盘走的那一份仍是普通 block，列表按内容长度顶穿卡片。瓦片同样来自两处：`org_graph_llm.js` 的 `renderRoutes` 覆盖了 `window.renderModelRoleEditors`，模型瓦片由它生成，组瓦片仍走 `models/role-view.js` 的 `renderModelGroupChainTile`；两处必须保持同一结构（`.resource-list-title` + `.resource-list-subtitle` + 一行 `.model-inline-meta`），否则同一个列表里会出现两种卡。三列（负载均衡组 / 模型列表 / 角色链）共用一套列头与卡片表面：列头 15px/700、高 48px（=38px 输入档 + 10px 下衬），容器透明无边框，卡片是唯一可见面。列头右衬 12px 配列表的 `scrollbar-gutter: stable`，所以搜索框与「新建组」的右缘一直压在卡片右缘上，三条列表也从同一条基线开始。
- 角色链没有「编辑模式」这一档：模型卡与组卡随时可拖、可删、可改，页面上不存在进入编辑的入口。第一次交互（拖动 / 移除 / 改轮数 / 建组）经 `models/actions.js` 的 `ensureModelRoleDraftSession()` 自动开启草稿会话，读侧仍是「有会话读草稿、没会话读服务端」——CEO 面板那类不碰模型页的消费者因此不会读到空草稿。有未提交改动时头部才出现「保存」（唯一填重点色的动作）与「取消修改」，两者都由 `syncModelRoleDraftState()` 这颗脏位驱动（它同时翻按钮的 `hidden`，不等整页重绘）；保存走 `PUT /api/llm/routes`，取消把草稿回滚成服务端读数并留着会话。窄屏是单列堆叠，保留列表自身高度，不做铺满。
- The detail header shows the binding's display title followed by a pencil (`编辑配置名称`) icon. Clicking it enters name-edit state, and the header renders an inline 配置名称 input from that state rather than mutating the DOM — a later re-render (protocol switch, probe result, validation error) keeps the typed value and its original-name baseline. Escape cancels. The name is not submitted on its own: 保存修改 compares it against the baseline and, when it changed, rejects a duplicate case-insensitive name before any request, then sends `PUT /api/llm/bindings/{key}` alongside the draft update.
- The header info button next to `关闭` is a click-to-show popover (`配置说明`) layered above the modal; it does not rely on hover. Its content is the current single-key/retry/fallback contract: one API key per config, multiple configs form the model chain for fallback, retry rounds and eviction semantics, and name-uniqueness/display sync notes. The popover closes on outside click or Escape.
- Create/save of connection details rejects multiple API keys (comma/newline separated) with a single-key policy message: capacity and failover come from adding more configs to the model chain, not from multiple keys on one config. Non-retryable-key errors and 400/422 shape errors still follow the per-config chain rules (see `config-and-models.md`「Model Retry And Key Rotation Config」).
- `测试最大并发数` is folded behind a `⋯` button next to the per-key concurrency input and requires an explicit confirmation dialog before running because the escalating probe can trigger provider rate limits. That input's caption is `最大并发数` and it is per API key of this config (a comma list lines up one value per key); the `最大并发数` in the page-header role strip is a different reading under the same words (see below).
- Per-role `最大轮数` / `最大并发数` limits live in a collapsible strip on the `模型配置` page header instead of inside the role chain cards; the strip is reachable any time (there is no edit mode to wait for), and its value rides the same draft session as the chains. Each limit group expands to one numeric input per role; `-1` means unlimited and is persisted as `null`, while the memory Agent `最大并发数` is fixed at 1 and renders as a non-editable pill.
- 模型页头部动作组另有一颗 `上下文信息更新时间`，与那颗脏状态才出现的 `保存` 并排但完全独立：`保存` 的文案不切换（只有 `正在保存...` 一档），空闲刷新按钮不挂进它的状态机，也不随选区或脏位隐藏。点开是 `长时间未活跃后刷新上下文信息` 弹窗，内含 `启用` 勾选（默认开）与 `刷新时间（分钟）` 数字框（默认 `120`，`min=1`，不写 `max` — 上限没有可推导的约束）。弹窗打开时先 GET 服务端读数当草稿基线，勾选与输入只改草稿，`应用` 在草稿等于读数时禁用；只有点 `应用` 才发 PUT。写失败时窗口留着、错误行与失败 toast 各一份，且服务端读数保持旧值——绝不把一次没落盘的编辑显示成已生效。文案按事实写：这道边界是"距上次请求超过设定时间"，不是"缓存命中清零"（实测节点道空闲再久也仍有约八成那跳带着头部命中，见 `context-and-cache-troubleshooting.md`「跨普通 fresh turn 的 tool schema churn」）。
- 真实推理请求只由 `测试连接` 与 `测试最大并发数` 两颗按钮发出：`保存修改` / `添加配置` 只做字段校验与落盘，不探测、也不复用谁的探测结果。弹窗的 `测试连接` / `保存修改` / `添加配置` / `删除配置` / `测试最大并发数` 在探测或保存进行中一律禁用——低 RPM 端点上重复点击 `测试连接` 会在同一秒并发打出多次真实推理，把后续探测自己挤进限流。
- The model editor carries a `思考与输出` field group in both create and detail modes: a `深度思考（Reasoning Effort）` six-level select (`none` 关闭深度思考 / `low` / `medium` 默认 / `high` / `xhigh` / `max`) and a `最大输出TOKEN` input (default `65536`, minimum 1). Both fields two-way sync with the JSON draft's `parameters.reasoning_effort` and `parameters.max_tokens`, exactly like `上下文窗口Token`; the select stores `none` literally to disable deep thinking, and saving goes through the existing record draft save path (`POST /api/llm/bindings` on create, `PUT /api/llm/configs/{id}`-style update on edit), so no dedicated endpoint is involved. Draft validation (`normalize_draft`) keeps the provider-template `reasoning_effort` allowlist equal to the same six levels, so every level the editor offers also survives 验证 and 保存; narrowing that template list makes the editor reject valid levels with `Invalid option selected`. Runtime defaults and provider-side behavior: 详见 `config-and-models.md`「Model Request Parameter Defaults」.
- `上下文窗口Token` and `最大输出TOKEN` are text inputs, not `type=number`, because they take a scale suffix: `parseTokenScaleValue` accepts `200K` / `1m` / `200000` (K = 1000, M = 1000000, case-insensitive, optional decimal so `1.5M` = 1500000, optional space) and returns `null` for anything else. `200KB` and `1e3` are rejected rather than truncated — the previous `Number.parseInt` shape read them as `200` and `1`, which is a silently wrong window on a runtime-authoritative field. What reaches the draft and the record is always the converted integer. Two rules keep the field and the JSON draft from overwriting each other: a box that carries *any* text is never rescued by the draft's reading (`reconcileBindingContextWindowTokens` only fills an empty box from JSON), so a typo stays on screen and blocks the save instead of being replaced by the old number; and the two failure kinds get different messages — 无法识别 names the notation, 必须大于 25000 / 不小于 1 names the bound. On save the box keeps the operator's own notation with the letter uppercased (`200k` → `200K`), which is what `tokenScaleDisplayText` decides: same number, keep the text; different number, fall back to the plain integer.

### Backend Responsibilities

- `POST /api/llm/drafts/validate` and `POST /api/llm/drafts/probe` check an unsaved provider draft; `POST /api/llm/drafts/probe-max-concurrency` derives per-key concurrency limits; `POST /api/llm/drafts/models` fetches the provider model catalog (`GET {base_url}/models`) using the draft's credentials and rotates across multiple API keys on authentication failure.
- 测试连接 is not a reachability check: the draft probe reads the model catalog and then sends one inference request shaped like the live sender (streaming, protocol envelope fields, one placeholder function tool run through the same normalizer the provider uses). Catalog success alone does not pass, and a rejected envelope returns the upstream error text in the probe message, so a config whose protocol fields the provider refuses is caught here instead of on the first real turn. 保存不探测，因此这一步失败不挡落盘。Request-shape rules are owned by `config-and-models.md`「llm_config 子系统」.
- `POST /api/llm/bindings` creates a binding and derives a unique `key` from the record's `default_model`, appending a numeric suffix when a same-name model already exists, so different providers can share a model name. The binding payload accepts the display `name`; non-empty names are deduplicated case-insensitively across the catalog (excluding the binding being edited), and the created/updated item exposes `name` on both `/api/models` and `/api/llm/bindings`. `POST /api/llm/bindings/{model_key}/rename` remains a back-compat endpoint that renames a binding key, rewrites matching references in `models.roles.*` and `agents.multi_agent.orchestrator_model_key`, and rejects an empty or duplicate key.
- Display surfaces follow the binding `name`: the task `Token统计` window resolves every model identifier through the catalog to `name` (falling back to the identifier itself when the catalog has no match), and that resolution covers the per-config heading, the 模型 column of 模型调用明细, the breakdown's sort order, and the call-table search — one resolver (`taskModelDisplayName` → `ceoModelUsageHeadlineTitle`), never a per-surface rule. The ledger stores only the binding `key` (`ModelTokenUsageRecord.model_key`, written from `LLMModelAttempt.model_key`), so labels follow the current config name and a deleted config's history reverts to its key; the key stays reachable through the breakdown subtitle and the 模型 cell tooltip so a rename never costs the way back to the config. Opening the window re-requests the catalog when it is still empty, because a failed boot-time load would otherwise degrade the whole panel to raw keys.
- `GET/PUT /api/context-refresh/settings` are **legacy config endpoints**: the section (`contextRefresh.enabled` / `contextRefresh.idleMinutes`) still round-trips for existing installs, but idle time is not a runtime reprint boundary. Pinned heads reprint only at compaction; changes between reprints are delivered as one-time `[RUNTIME_NOTICE]` messages. The PUT keeps the same validation (`idleMinutes < 1` / non-numeric → `invalid_idle_minutes`) and save → `_refresh_runtime` path as other admin settings, and issues no provider request.
- Draft endpoints validate the draft first and report field-level errors without issuing provider requests when validation fails. Draft validation normalizes endpoint-style `base_url` values (trailing `/chat/completions`, `/responses`, `/models`) to the provider API root instead of rejecting them.

### Maintenance Boundary

- If `获取模型列表` fails but `测试连接` succeeds, compare the draft `base_url`/`api_key` sync state in `g3ku/web/frontend/org_graph_llm.js` with `POST /api/llm/drafts/models` before debugging the provider.
- If catalog fetch returns a non-JSON or empty-catalog error, treat it as a provider endpoint-shape problem (same triage as a failed model-catalog connection probe), not as a frontend bug.

## Frontend Theme And Layout Contract

The browser shell renders one visual system per theme. Theme and layout are frontend-only concerns: they never change API, WebSocket, permission or state-machine contracts, and the backend stays the authority for session, task and runtime state.

### Layering And Ownership

- `g3ku/web/frontend/org_graph.css` is the only stylesheet the app loads. `org_graph.html` links the fonts sheet, then `org_graph.css`; there is no second visual layer, no `[data-ui-version]` attribute and no runtime switch that enables or disables one.
- Shared design tokens (`--ui-*`) live in `:root` and `[data-theme="dark"]` / `[data-theme="light"]`; a legacy-token re-anchoring block maps the old names (`--bg-*`, `--text-*`, `--brand-*`, `--status-*`, `--radius-*`, `--shadow-*`, `--space-*`) onto them. Retheming edits `--ui-*` values instead of chasing component rules. A legacy rule that hardcodes a color, radius or shadow still needs a rule of at least equal selector depth.
- File order is the cascade contract: token blocks, then shared controls (navigation, buttons, inputs, panels, status badges, overlays, checkboxes), then component blocks (page header, memory, model, task, CEO session panel, CEO chat, settings). Shared rules carry a `:root` prefix, keeping the extra class-level specificity they had as attribute-gated rules; component rules come later and win at equal specificity.
- Verification is per component: the same DOM is measured with the pre-change stylesheet and the new one, every element in the view is compared property by property at 1440/1024/mobile and both themes, and state-only rules (open menus, drag states, execution-node statuses, dialogs) are forced or synthesised so they are measured too.
- `org_graph.html` loads exactly two stylesheets: the fonts sheet and `org_graph.css`. A rule written in any other file under `g3ku/web/frontend/` never reaches the page, so that file is the only place appearance can be changed, and a test asserting on styling must read it.
- Checkbox styling is one shared component plus explicit per-surface cases. The component is `.ui-checkbox-input` (the real `<input>`, `position: absolute` at 1×1 with `opacity: 0` and `pointer-events: none`, so it still owns clicks, focus and form state) followed **immediately** by `.ui-checkbox-box` (the drawn 18px square, `input:checked + .ui-checkbox-box`): adjacency is part of the contract, and any element inserted between the two draws nothing. Its border and fill come from `--ui-border-strong` (`rgba(255,255,255,.15)` dark / `#b6b6b6` light) and `--ui-bg-input`, so both themes show a legible box.
- There is no bare `input[type="checkbox"]` global rule, which is what makes the coverage question answerable: every checkbox site falls in exactly one of three registered tiers — shared component, its own surface's drawn box (`.ceo-session-checkbox__box` for session bulk select, `.llm-image-checkbox-indicator` for the multimodal binding switch, `.qq-bot-sandbox-input`), or the box hidden by design with the pill carrying the state (`.role-toggle input { opacity: 0 }` for skill/tool role visibility, plus one hidden `name="enabled"` form field). A site in none of the tiers is a regression: `tests/resources/org_graph_checkbox_styling.test.js` walks every `type="checkbox"` site, classifies it by a stable marker rather than a line number, requires the component tier to have its adjacent box, and asserts the no-global-rule premise itself so the tiering stays readable if someone ever adds one. A "keep the native box, set size and `accent-color`" tier existed and was retired: no surface may reintroduce it, because a recoloured native box renders per-OS and cannot match the component's checked/ disabled / focus states.
- The centered dialog shell (`.confirm-dialog`, the same skin behind 设置 / 修改密码 / 配置包 / 负载均衡组配置) is never a scrolling owner: it keeps `overflow: clip`, not `hidden`. `hidden` is still a scroll container, and a dialog body that owns its own scroller (`.project-settings-body`, with a nested list scroller inside it) leaves the shell's `scrollHeight` above its `clientHeight` — the browser then reveals a focused control near the bottom of that list by scrolling the shell itself, which pushes the header out of view, drags 取消/确定 upward and leaves a blank band at the bottom of the panel. That offset lives on the permanent shell node, so reopening the dialog for another object keeps showing it and only a page reload clears it. The scrolling owner is `.project-settings-body` alone, and because that body container is likewise a permanent node whose `innerHTML` is swapped per open, opening a dialog resets its `scrollTop` (`openLoadBalanceGroupDialog`) rather than inheriting where the previous object's list was scrolled to; a repaint of the same open dialog (validation error, live member toggle) must not reset it. `tests/resources/org_graph_app.model_route_editor.test.js` pins both halves.
- Anchoring is the cheaper fix and is what the boot/unlock screens use: the boot/unlock card, its banner, its legacy-data panel and preview, and `.interaction-flow` consume only `--bg-panel` / `--bg-surface-inner` / `--bg-app` / `--border-color` / `--shadow-*` / `--status-failed-*`, so one declaration serves both themes and no extra rule is needed. A legacy surface that hardcodes a dark background instead renders in light mode as a dark card floating on the light canvas carrying light-theme ink — unreadable text, and undetectable until someone opens that surface in the other theme, which is why every theme check covers the boot screen and not just the unlocked views.
- A tinted block that holds prose keeps `--text-primary` for its text and lets the accent border carry the meaning. The ~3:1 accent-on-own-tint floor sized for chips and status badges becomes unreadable under a full sentence of 16px body text.
- In the stylesheet light is the `:root` default and dark is an override set, so both themes carry explicit token blocks. A change checked in one theme is incomplete until the other is checked.
- The light block changes surfaces (canvas / surface / elevated / input / subtle, borders, text tiers, shadows) **and the accent tiers**. Each accent carries its own light value, darkened on its own hue until it clears 4.5:1 as text on white: `--ui-brand #1e8478`, `--ui-success #288556`, `--ui-warning #9a6d17`, `--ui-danger #e91c1c`, `--ui-info #196ef7`, `--ui-inspecting #8058f8`. `--ui-on-brand` is white there because the brand fill is dark enough to need it; dark keeps the bright accents and `--ui-on-brand #04211d`. An accent therefore does not read byte-identically across themes — light mode optimizes for legibility on white.
- Accent tints carry no alpha in either theme. The `--ui-*-soft` / `--ui-*-soft-strong` fills are opaque hex values, one pair per theme: dark uses the composite of the old translucent tint over `--ui-bg-surface` (so the dark theme looks unchanged), light uses 30% / 42% of its own darkened accent mixed with white — an alpha tint would composite against white and wash the chip out, which is what "light mode must not get lighter" forbids. Chip text on its own tint lands at about 3:1 and that is the deliberate floor: a tint is made of the text color, so 4.5:1 between the two is unreachable, and depth stops there instead of buying contrast by bleaching the fill back out. Accent borders on chips and switches are the plain accent (`border-color: var(--ui-brand)`, chip borders `currentColor`). The status stroke family is the exception: `.status-badge` borders and `.execution-tree-node` status borders are the same accent at 50% alpha (`color-mix(in srgb, … 50%, transparent)`), because 1px is the thinnest border this engine paints — `border-width: 0.5px` computes back to `1px` at DPR 1 — so a stroke that reads too heavy can only be lightened through opacity, not geometry. That 50% is that family's weight, not a global stroke default: a tinted box whose fill already states what it is takes a lighter edge, and the user bubble sits at 30% of `--ui-brand` because `--ui-brand-soft` alone says "mine" while a 50% ring makes the bubble the loudest object in a feed whose model text carries no frame at all. Neutral overlays keep their alpha in dark (`--ui-neutral-soft`, scrollbar thumb, focus rings, shadows) because they must composite over varying backgrounds and carry no hue; light's `--ui-neutral-soft` is the solid `#e6e6e6`, because `rgba(188,188,188,.12)` over white computes to `#f7f7f7` — an invisible status badge and a no-op hover. `--ui-scrim` stays alpha in both themes: it is a floating overlay and has to show what is underneath.
- Surface tokens are achromatic in both themes: every background tier, border, `--ui-neutral-soft` and `--ui-scrim` has equal R/G/B channels (dark `#121212 / #191919 / #212121 / #272727 / #151515`, light `#f6f6f6 / #ffffff / #ececec / #e6e6e6`, borders `#d3d3d3 / #b6b6b6`). Hue belongs to the accent tiers only, so a new surface token must not carry a green or blue cast.

### Theme Persistence

- Light is the default theme, and the default has two carriers that must agree: the static `data-theme="light"` on `<html>` in `org_graph.html`, and the fallback inside `initializeTheme()`. The static attribute is what paints, because the preference is applied by an end-of-`<body>` script; a stored value opposite to it therefore renders for one frame before the script corrects it. The markup's sun/moon inline visibility follows the same default so the toggle never carries the other theme's glyph pre-script.
- `#theme-toggle` flips `data-theme` on `<html>`, stores the choice under `g3ku.ui.theme.v1`, and updates the sun/moon icon plus the button `aria-label` / `title` so they name the theme the next click produces. An absent key, an unreadable store, or any value other than `dark` / `light` resolves to light, so an operator's explicit `dark` selection is honored and never silently flipped by default work.
- Switching theme does not reload the page, request data, or rebuild the current view.
- Every `localStorage` read/write is guarded: blocked or unavailable storage degrades to light theme plus the default navigation state instead of breaking startup.
- 阅读面字号是同一族的第二个显示偏好：设置里的 `#project-settings-reading-scale` 把档位（90%–130%，每档 5%，默认 110%）存进 `g3ku.ui.reading-scale.v1`，落成 `<html>` 上的 `--reading-font-scale`。它只乘在阅读面的字号上——消息气泡（`.message .msg-content.msg-content`）、markdown 标题、`.task-trace-code`、`.memory-note-preview-body`——控件字号与间距不跟，所以它不同于桌面窗口的缩放（宿主 `ZoomFactor`，见 `operations-and-maintenance.md`「首选一键启动脚本」）。
- 档位归一在 `normalizeReadingScale`：越界夹到两端、非 5% 网格就近吸附、空串/不可解析回默认 110%。空串按"没设过"处理是必须的——下拉框没有匹配项时读出来就是空串，而 `Number("")` 是 `0`，照数值走会落到 90%。
- 改档位只写变量、不发请求、不重建视图；下次打开设置时下拉框回显的是存档值（`applyReadingScale` 同时同步变量与控件）。

### Navigation Bar And Compact Mode

Primary navigation is a full-width top bar, 56px tall: the brand at the left, the eight view buttons in a row, and the theme toggle and 设置 at the right. Below 900px the bar scrolls horizontally; there is no drawer layer. The current view is a filled chip — `--ui-brand` background with `--ui-on-brand` label (white on light's darkened brand, near-black on dark's bright one), the same color logic as the page's filled header button — while idle items stay transparent with secondary text, so the active item is the only filled surface in the bar. The audit badge keeps its own danger fill and reads unchanged on top of the chip.

Because there is no left sidebar anymore, fixed overlays must not keep the old sidebar compensation: `.app-toast-viewport` shipped with `left: 160px` to center toasts over the content area, which after the top-bar change pushed every toast and the 正在打开任务 notice 80px right of the window center. The active layer resets it to `left: 0; right: 0`, and it is the only `position: fixed` surface that carried such an offset (drawers and dialogs center with `top/left: 50%`). That same rule owns the layer's vertical anchor: `top: calc(env(safe-area-inset-top, 0px) + 95px)`, putting the toast's top edge 39px below the bottom of the 56px bar. The number is a literal and shares no variable with the bar rule, so resizing the top bar means re-deriving it, otherwise the toast silently creeps back onto the bar. The notice that stacks in this layer is unaffected by the anchor: `alignTaskTreeLoadNoticeToSearchBox()` derives its margin from the measured viewport rect rather than an assumed offset.

**Toast reveal contract — one channel per fact.** `showToast` drives a single element (`#app-toast`), so every call overwrites the previous title/text and resets its timer; `persistent: true` (or `durationMs <= 0`) means the layer stays until the next call or a manual close. A success toast is only warranted when the action leaves nothing the operator can read: a background write, a cross-process sync, an accepted-but-not-yet-applied request, or numbers the surface never prints (本次新增 N 个附件 vs the chips' total, 批量计数, 版本差).
