- 显示面与落盘面是分开的两件事：思考全文既不写转录也不写 inflight 侧车。快照只带 `_assistant_reasoning_snapshot()` 的有界读数 `{attempt, seconds, chars, est_tokens, tail_line}`，`tail_line` 截到 180 字符，作用是把重连后的那一行恢复出来（`applyCeoThinkingFromInflightSnapshot`）；思考帧的 flush 窗口是 `_ASSISTANT_REASONING_FLUSH_WINDOW_SECONDS`（250 ms），且**不**调 `_sync_persisted_inflight_turn()`——正文那次 flush 顺带落盘是按 75 ms 档设计的，思考每帧整份重写快照等于把显示故障换成 IO 故障。回合收尾时转录 assistant 行带 `thinking = {seconds, chars, est_tokens[, tokens]}`，历史行据此渲染同一行汇总，并且**不带可点形状**（`thinkingExpandable: false` → 行头没有 `role="button"`，也没有 click 监听）。
- 该行对所有会打模型的回合都画，包括 heartbeat / cron / 内部轮。"这帧值不值得占一个回合"那道闸门会丢掉没有正文的内部轮进行帧，所以思考读数同时进可见性判据（`ceoNeedsAssistantTurn()` 一类与 `ceoInflightTurnHasVisibleAssistantState()`）与空行出帧闸门（`_build_ceo_snapshot` 的 `thinking` 分支），否则内部轮仍然只有一条静态转圈。
- 前端形状：`.ceo-thinking-line` 插在 `.assistant-text` 之后；折叠态只占一行（`思考 1分15秒 · ~300 tok` + 最后一行，行内 `title` 给同一行全文）。**开关就是这一行本身**：`.ceo-thinking-line-head` 带 `role="button"`、`tabindex="0"`、`aria-expanded`，点一下展开、再点一下收起，Enter/Space 等价——不设独立的"展开"按钮，因为那颗按钮无论挂在行尾还是单占一格，都会把这行本来要省的宽度还回去。CSS 的可点判据也跟着用 `role`（`.ceo-thinking-line-head[role="button"]`），历史行不带 `role` 就没有光标与可点错觉。展开态是 `.ceo-thinking-line-pane`（`max-height` 定死 + `overflow-y:auto`，盒子不按内容撑开），追加时跟随到尾部，用户上滑即停止跟随、滑回底部恢复（`turn.thinkingFollowTail`）；窗体自己**不**挂 click，所以在窗里选文本、滚动都不会把行收起。正文按**原样**显示、不脱敏：这是操作者的明确决定（风险＝思考里可能带敏感字句，而正文车道早有同样暴露），不要再加清洗层。`[hidden]` 必须显式 `display:none`——该族用 flex 布局，会盖掉 UA 的 hidden 规则；样式合同由 `tests/resources/org_graph_app.ceo_reasoning_row.test.js` 钉住。

### 2.11. In-App Media Viewer And Download Lane

界面里所有 app 自己供的文件（图片、附件、签名媒体、轨迹正文 ref）都在这一层显示或下载，代码边界 `g3ku/web/frontend/ui/media-viewer.js`。

- 为什么决定权在渲染侧而不是壳侧：WebView2 把 `<a target="_blank">` 与 `window.open` 当成一次新窗请求，pywebview 对该事件写死交给系统浏览器，壳里再挂回调也无法区分"这是界面自己的图片"还是"这是外站链接"——只有渲染那一刻知道目标是不是 app 供的。所以媒体锚点不带新窗语义，改带 `data-media-open`（`image` 或 `auto`）与 `data-media-name`；真外链（`http(s)` 且非媒体路由）照旧 `target="_blank"` 交系统浏览器，那是正确行为，不要为了"看起来一致"改掉。
- 点击是文档级委托（`bindMediaViewer`），只在无修饰键的左键上 `preventDefault`；Ctrl/Shift/Alt/Meta 或中键点击留给浏览器默认语义，用户想"在别处打开"时仍然有路。
- 分派按类型：图片进 `<img>`；pdf 与 `md/txt/csv/tsv` 进 `<iframe>`；其余一律走下载道。内嵌那一档只收服务端确实按 inline 供的类型（判据在 `ceo_media.py` 的 `inline` 计算：image（svg 除外）/`application/pdf`/`text/*`），把 attachment 类型塞进 iframe 得到的是一次下载而不是显示。svg 走 `<img>` 而不是 iframe——它在 img 元素里不脚本化。
- 轨迹正文（节点详情那颗「查看完整」）用同一具查看器的分页档：本层渲染成 `.task-trace-field` + `.task-trace-output-value` + `.task-trace-more` 那组既有形状，交给 `hydrateTraceOutputBlocks` / `loadMoreTraceOutputBlock` 取数，于是一屏闸门、续读判据、404 终态缓存都只有一份实现。整份正文不进应用页（实测单行 artifact 7,024,971 字符），要下文点「继续加载」。
- 下载道 `triggerMediaDownload` 是一条带 `download` 的锚点，导出配置包与非图片附件共用。宿主必须允许下载，否则桌面窗口里这一次点击被取消、界面拿不到任何反馈——开关与判读归 `operations-and-maintenance.md`「首选一键启动脚本」。
- 层位与收口：backdrop/drawer 在抽屉族之上（z-index 111/112），Escape 阶梯里排在确认弹窗之后、其余抽屉之前；关闭时清空本层内容，让已经展开的 pdf/正文文档停止解码，否则下一层继承这一帧的卡顿。
- 回归 `tests/resources/org_graph_app.media_viewer.test.js`（锚点形状、分派、委托点击、分页档、下载道）。

### 3. Context Loader Notices

- Successful context-loading calls do not render as ordinary `Interaction Flow` steps under the assistant bubble. This family has two entry forms: the explicit loaders (`load_tool_context` / `load_skill_context` and their `_v2` variants) and a direct call to a tool whose contract is not in the request view — that hop delivers the contract instead of executing, and its tool result carries the runtime-owned `contract_delivery` marker (`staged` / `invalid`).
- Frontend loader-notice detection must treat both legacy and v2 loader names as the same UI family:
  - `load_tool_context` / `load_tool_context_v2` => tool notice
  - `load_skill_context` / `load_skill_context_v2` => skill notice
  - any successful step carrying `contract_delivery` => tool notice for `step.tool_name` (the tool that was loaded, not the loader)
- Instead, the browser shows a short-lived composer notice above the input row, using the loaded `tool_id` or `skill_id` when the runtime payload exposes it. For a direct-delivery step the notice target is the step's own `tool_name`; no payload parsing is involved.
- These notices are intentionally stackable rather than single-slot: multiple successful loader calls may coexist in one right-aligned floating column that lines up with the send-button edge.
- The type-specific styling comes from a leading icon rather than a leading green dot:
  - tool notices use the same `wrench` icon family as the sidebar Tool page
  - skill notices use the same `sparkles` icon family as the sidebar Skill page
- The risk-colored dot remains present on the trailing edge so operators can still distinguish low / medium / high loader risk at a glance.
- Risk color comes from the resource catalog in browser state (`S.skills` / `S.tools`), not from the tool result. Catalog population is on demand: the node detail path awaits `ensureContextRiskCatalog()` (which fills both lists from `/api/resources/skills` + `/api/resources/tools` and de-duplicates concurrent calls) only when the rendered trace actually contains context-load steps. An empty catalog degrades every risk color to the medium fallback rather than failing the render.
- Tool risk lookup reads that tool record's own `risk_level`: `S.tools` is keyed by `tool_id`, so a loaded `filesystem_write` colors from the write tool itself and a loaded `filesystem_stat` from the measure tool. There is no group-level fallback and no second name space to reconcile.
- The task node detail page renders the same loader family as round chips rather than hiding it: the chip title is `加载 skill` / `加载 tool` with a leading risk-colored kind icon (`sparkles` / `wrench`, same palette as the notice dot), and the loaded resource id is exposed only as the chip tooltip. Expanding a loader chip shows a single `内容` field holding the loaded body (`content` from the loader result payload), with no arguments field and no tool name. A direct-delivery step uses the same `加载 tool` chip with `step.tool_name` as both the loaded resource id and the risk lookup key; its body is the contract block carried in the result, so the chip falls back to the ordinary output field when the payload is not loader JSON.
- Loader and delivery steps that did not succeed keep the ordinary tool-step rendering (name, arguments, raw output), so gate errors, failed loads and failed deliveries stay diagnosable instead of collapsing into an empty `内容`.
- The intended motion contract is still "launch from the composer, settle into the notice stack, then fade out"; the full lifecycle is currently about 5 seconds per notice.
- That notice is intentionally live-only UI state. It should fade away after a short timeout and must not be appended into the persisted CEO session `messages` list.

### Manual Pause Resume Rule

- Manual pause means “freeze the current round as previous-round context,” the operator-visible “pause” button is a terminal stop for the current visible turn, the runtime state ends as `completed` tagged `stop_reason=user_pause`, and ordinary user sessions are never left in a long-lived resumable `paused` state. The next outbound user message after pause must start a new round.
- The paused round's user message, execution trace, turns ledger, tool calls, and compression state are preserved in transcript and snapshot context so the next round can inherit them without rewriting the original user text.
- Manual pause writes the completed-session continuity sidecar immediately, clears that session's ordinary paused/inflight restorable snapshots, and archives the paused assistant bubble into a persisted message with `status=paused` during the stop flow itself, not only when the next user turn is about to dispatch.
- That archived paused assistant is durable UI history for `snapshot.ceo` restore/reconnect, but it remains hidden from prompt-history assembly and session-summary counts via `history_visible=false`.
- Browser-side restore should therefore render that persisted paused assistant as a paused bubble rather than a completed reply. The next ordinary user turn inherits context from visible history plus completed continuity state, not from resuming the old paused turn.

### CEO Turn Timeline Rendering Contract

- 回合轨道（CEO 会话轨与任务节点详情共用）的唯一数据源是 `turns[]`：`seq` / `turn_id` / `text` / `text_ref` / `created_at` / `status` / `tools[]`。节点侧由 `main/monitoring/execution_trace.py` 从 `node.output[]` 组装，CEO 侧由 `frontdoor_turns` 账本携带；两者都经 `buildTurnGroupDescriptors`（`tasks/node-detail.js`，CEO 轨直接复用）分组渲染。
- 分组规则由模型自己写的叙述决定：带非空 `text` 的 turn 起一个叙述组；一个 turn 的 `tools` 挂进**最近一条**叙述组（中间没有新叙述的工具轮继续并进同组），叙述之前就出现的工具轮直接铺成无标题的裸块（`.task-trace-step--bare`，不自动加「开始执行」标题）；只有文本没有工具的 turn 画成一条纯叙述行，不带 chip。
- 组标题就是那条叙述（保留全文，不按 80 字截断）：折叠时单行省略号，展开后同一段原文变成 **16.5 行**视窗内部滚动（`max-height: calc(16.5 * 1.6em)`，max-height 不预占空白），**不在组体内再复制一行正文**；框透明、无边框，看起来就是原文本本身。前门与节点详情的执行流程收进**一个可折叠的总框**（节点 `#task-flow-box.task-flow-box`、前门 `.interaction-flow`），默认折叠、点标题行展开；节点框标题写 `输入 … 缓存命中 … / …% · 输出 … · 轮数 …`（加粗，与中途文本同款）；前门框标题只写这一轮的总时长（裸秒数，如 `38s`，无「执行流程/耗时」前缀），固定按整秒显示（跨分钟也写 `172s`，不换算 `m`/`h`），并每秒重算一次：起点是本轮第一条请求（提问行的 `timestamp`，在飞快照里是 `user_message` 的时间戳，本地发送用发出时刻），终点是最终文本输出（收尾帧落下的 `completedAt`，在飞期间取当前时刻）；提问行缺失（已被压缩掉、或内部轮本来没有可见提问行）时退回本轮最早的工具开始时间，两端缺一就留 `--`。压缩切出的头段与基础气泡是同一轮，共用同一对时间锚点，读数一致。框不画边框线。节点流程框**可折叠但不画展开箭头/引导线**：点统计行（`task-flow-header`）开合，统计行（`#ad-token-summary`）与上方初始提示词正文左对齐（`padding-left: 18px`）；流程内所有条目的时间行（`.task-trace-round-meta`）与工具条统一缩进 28px，和中途文本正文同一条左基线。前门头部的圆点与向下引导线画在**同一个元素**里（`.interaction-flow-guide`）：圆点是写死 9×9 的 SVG 背景（不随盒子高度变化，任何缩放下都是正圆），细线是它的 `::after`（占 4~5px、中心 4.5px）。两者共用一个定位盒，圆心就是线中心。整个 guide 的位置由 `--ceo-flow-dot-x` / `--ceo-flow-dot-y` 给出（缺省 `8.5px` / `23.5px`）；`y` 由脚本按秒数盒子的实际位置加上字体墨迹修正算出——基线临时塞零尺寸 `inline-block` 探针读布局自己摆出的基线，墨迹半高由 SVG `<text>` 的 `getBBox()` 紧框给出（和正文同一套字体解析，不用 canvas 的 font 串），量完即移除。换字体、字号档位、字体延迟加载或窗口尺寸变化后重量，每秒 ticker 也顺手校一次（值没变就不写、探针不重跑）。展开动画只作用在 `::after`（`scaleY 0→1`，0.22s），圆点不受影响。秒数用 `padding-left: 42px` 让出点位（原 16px 点位 + 26px 间隔），仍与内部中途文本正文、时间行同轴。前门内容淡入/上移 0.18s。节点「执行流程」标题不带数量徽标；初始提示词移出总框，在框上方单独成框（`#ad-initial-prompt`）。总框内部是扁平列表：每条中途文本只占一行，最左侧是 `>` 展开钮（`task-trace-turn-toggle`），点击只展开这一条正文（16.5 行透明视窗），不画左上角状态点；工具调用按原样穿插其间。中途输出**列表本身**（节点详情 `#ad-input .task-trace-list` 与 CEO 回合轨 `.interaction-flow-list.task-trace-list`）在 **16.5 条**处封顶并内部滚动（`max-height: calc(16.5 * 48px + 16 * var(--space-3))`），条数多时不把整页刷长；不足 17 条时按内容自适应。中途输出行右侧不挂成功/失败状态芯片（共用 `renderTraceStep`，`turnText` 非空即 `showStatus=false`），状态仍由工具行/树徽标表达。工具 chip 在组体内：`tool_name`、状态、时间行（`.task-trace-round-meta`）、参数与输出面板。叙述是裸文本（`.task-trace-round-text`，无边框、无填充、无内缩），时间行与 chip 同一条 grid 列；再套一层容器就是给已经属于轨道的文字加第二个框。
- 参数面板读 `tools[].arguments_text`（全量入参原文），固定高度封顶 + 内部滚动，长入参不许把面板撑成一整屏。前端给这一行挂 `data-arguments-lookup="<tool_call_id>"`，点开时按 `GET /api/ceo/sessions/{id}/tool-arguments?tool_call_id=` 回取一次：驻留会话直接读 `frontdoor_turns`（按引用，不 deepcopy 也不 normalize；取账本到序列化之间不出现 `await`），会话未驻留时读完成态 sidecar 的同名字段；账本里已经没有这次调用时端点回 `404 tool_arguments_not_found`，面板退回提示串。同一份原文只取一次：按 `tool_call_id` 缓存，与输出的 `output_ref` 车道共用终态判定。回归 `tests/test_ceo_tool_arguments_api.py` + `tests/resources/org_graph_app.trace_arguments_lookup.test.js`。
- 不在轨道上画的行：`silent` 工具（它的成绩就是折叠后的静默行）、成功的上下文加载投递（`contract_delivery`）与成功的 loader 调用（加载动作以右上角 live-only pill 呈现）；失败的加载/投递仍按普通工具行画原因。过滤是显示规则，不改账本。
- 在飞工具行住在**最后一组的组体内**，不是组的兄弟节点：`ceoLiveToolStepHost` 把它们挂进末位组的 `.task-trace-body`（容器 `.task-trace-live-steps` 自带 `role=list`），账本已画上这次调用的按 `tool_call_id` 摘除。**组开合归用户**：挂行不顶开收起的组；该回合还没有任何叙述组时（纯问答轮，或首批工具轮），实时行退回 `.interaction-flow-list`。收尾帧先 `clearCeoLiveToolSteps` 再重建轨道。实时行的转圈图标用 `loader`，不用中心对称的 `loader-circle`；实时行不画左上角的轨道状态点（正式轮次在卡内同样不画），运行态只由标题、转圈图标与右侧状态芯片表达。
- 气泡上 `.interaction-flow-meta` 那行（`updateCeoTurnMeta`）是**卡片收起时唯一一直在动的进度面**：轨道存在时必须写 `ceoRailMetaLabel(turn)`——`N 组 · M 次调用 · 正在跑 K 个工具`，两个写入口（轨道渲染与工具事件）用同一句；工具帧把它换成 `正在处理中` / `处理完成` 是缺陷不是简化。
- 压缩边界：压缩区分线的 `stats.boundary_turn_seq` 决定一个回合被切成两段时各自渲染哪截——`turnWindowFrom` / `turnWindowTo` 由服务端切分给出，`sliceCeoRailTurns` 只放行窗口内的 turn；缺 `seq` 的条目（非前门来源）不参与切分。压缩帧早于轨道到达时，`reconcileDeferredCeoCompressionSplit` 在轨道落地后把 `seq <= boundary` 的组补回头段。
- 快照与实时同源：`snapshot.ceo.messages[]` 的 assistant 行携带该回合的 `turns`（不是累计的整本账），实时 `ceo.turn.patch` 携带 `turns` 全量并按 `seq` 合并；补丁不带 turns 时对轨道是 no-op，浏览器保留已渲染的组与在飞行，不退回等待占位。渲染签名投影 turns 骨架（seq / status / text / 工具的 name + status + 输出长度），漏字段会让新组被缓存先行渲染整份跳过。
- 节点详情摘要档的 `execution_trace_summary.turns[]` 与本契约同形；摘要工具步的入参/出参按 160 字预览（`arguments_text` / `output_text`）时仍要能在面板上打开全量输出（`output_ref`）。`contract_delivery` 必须穿过每一个重建工具行的归一化器（读侧 sanitizer、两份前端 normalizer、摘要构造器），漏一层就把加载卡片画回成普通工具调用。
- 前端不得从平铺 `tool_events` 或 session 级累计账本重建轨道；`turns` 由服务端按消息顺序生成，刷新/重连因此重建出同一份分组。

### CEO Feed View State And Scroll Preservation Contract

- `renderCeoSnapshot` is a full feed rebuild (message replay plus inflight/preserved turn restore) used for session loads, session switches, and as the authoritative fallback whenever the rendered DOM cannot be trusted. Rebuilds are avoided when unnecessary: an incoming `snapshot.ceo` whose render signature (full messages projection plus live turn key fields) equals the current render is skipped outright, so reconnect re-pushes do not churn the feed; and `ceo.reply.final` closeouts carrying `user_messages` update incrementally — missing user bubbles are appended before the running turn and the turn finalizes in place — whenever the rendered DOM exactly matches the recorded message-key model (each child carries its `data-ceo-key` in order, the final child is the active turn). Any mismatch (promoted follow-ups, hand-sent bubbles, preserved-turn coexistence) falls back to the full rebuild, which stays the authoritative self-healing path.
- The live/preserved turn part of the signature covers the turn's `turns[]` skeleton, not only `source`/`turn_id`/`status`/`assistant_text`/`usage`: each turn's seq, status and text presence, and each tool's name/status/output length. A live turn grows its ledger while `assistant_text` and `usage` stand still (the model is executing tools between provider calls), so a skeleton-less signature lets a session switch-back's cache-first render occupy the signature and silently skip the authoritative snapshot that carries the groups created while the operator was away — the fix looks like "refresh the page and they appear". Signature additions stay pixel-relevant and bounded: never put tool output bodies or time-ticking values in it. Pinned by `tests/resources/org_graph_app.ceo_message_actions.test.js`.
- Both full feed rebuilds and live turn re-renders must preserve the operator's reading position and expansion choices. Live updates (`ceo.turn.patch`, `ceo.reply.final`) re-render the turn's whole stage track through a wipe-and-rebuild (`renderCeoStageTraceIntoTurn`), so a turn-scoped capture/restore contract applies to every such re-render, not only to snapshot rebuilds.
- Feed follow behaviour is one intent bit (`S.ceoFeedFollowLatest`), never instantaneous geometry. Live mutations pin to bottom only while follow is on; while disengaged no mutation, compression divider, or image-load re-pin may move the feed scrollbar, and `#ceo-scroll-to-latest-btn` stays visible until the operator returns to the bottom or clicks it. Sending a message or opening/switching a session re-arms follow. Geometry-only checks re-armed follow mid-drag (64px threshold re-triggers on every mutation), which read as "scrolling up gets yanked back repeatedly".
- Disengagement has to be observable on a lane that pins every animation frame. Each bottom pin renews the programmatic-scroll window (`markCeoFeedProgrammaticScroll`, +1s) and the mutation-silence window, so the `scroll` event that would prove the operator left the bottom is swallowed for as long as the stream runs — relying on it alone locks the page ("正在输出时无法滚动"). Follow therefore drops on the input itself, on two surfaces. Directional input disengages in `handleCeoFeedUserGesture` (`wheel` with negative `deltaY`; `PageUp` / `Home` / `ArrowUp`), gated by `ceoFeedGestureReachesFeed`, which walks the event target up to the feed and stops at the first nested scroll container that can still absorb that direction — scrolling through a long tool output must not drop the feed out of follow, while an exhausted inner box chains up and does. Direction-less input (`pointerdown` / `touchstart`: scrollbar drag, touch) is resolved at mutation time in `captureCeoFeedScrollSnapshot`, which compares the gesture timestamp against the previous mutation and reads geometry **before** the DOM grows; that ordering is what keeps "click to expand a stage card" (content grows inside the same mutation) from reading as an upward scroll. Programmatic scrolls still never flip the bit, and re-arming mid-stream goes through the button, since a scroll back to the bottom cannot be distinguished from the next pin while the lane is live. Pinned by `tests/resources/org_graph_app.ceo_live_view_state.test.js`.
- The button's breathing is a second signal with a second gate, so visibility and pulsing never share one condition. Breathing answers "the runtime is still producing output you are not looking at", which requires follow to be disengaged **and** a turn to be running: the same `S.ceoTurnActive` bit behind `.ceo-turn-active` on the feed, covering heartbeat/cron internal rounds as well as user rounds. `updateCeoScrollToLatestButton()` (visibility writes) and `syncCeoFeedTurnActiveClass()` (turn-state writes) are the only two writers and both re-derive `.is-breathing` from that bit, so a turn closing while the operator reads history stops the pulse on the spot and leaves the button up; scrolling back to the bottom clears both at once. The glow itself is a `::after` halo that is out of flow and whose only animated property is `opacity` — the halo's reach, the button's own shadow, and its 42px box stay constant across the cycle. Interpolating `box-shadow` spread on the button (or leaving `backdrop-filter` on it) makes the painted outline swell and collapse every 1.8s, and the peak reaches past the small clearance above the composer onto neighbouring message text, which reads as the effect "filling up the surroundings" rather than breathing. `prefers-reduced-motion` removes the pulse and keeps the resting glow.
- Tool-output boxes are scroll containers of their own (`.interaction-step-detail` and the turn rail's `.task-trace-code`, both capped with `overflow: auto`), so the reading position lives one level below the feed's own `scrollTop` and needs its own preservation. In-place output sync (`setCeoToolStepOutput` and the lazy output hydration) writes text only when it differs and re-applies the previous `scrollTop` when it does, because replacing a scroll container's children collapses its content height and zeroes the offset — otherwise every tool event and every one-second duration tick throws away the position an operator is reading from. `hidden` writes are skipped when the state already matches for the same reason. Turn-scoped capture/restore additionally carries each nested box's `scrollTop` keyed by owning-stage trace key plus its class discriminator and in-step order. Pinned by `tests/resources/org_graph_app.ceo_tool_output_scroll.test.js`.
- Before a live turn-rail re-render wipes the turn DOM, the browser captures turn-scoped state: Interaction Flow open/closed, per-group `<details>` open keyed by `data-trace-key`, and the round tool strip selection keyed by owning-group trace key + `data-round-key` (in-turn host-order fallback when the round key is empty, group-scoped because `round_index` repeats across groups). Restore runs right after the rebuild and before the `[open]` step hydration pass, so re-opened stages re-fetch output blocks and a restored tool selection re-activates its panel through `setTraceRoundActiveTool`. Capture is gated on `turn.lastExecutionTraceSummary`: a turn-id change clears that field, so a new turn never inherits the previous turn's expansion state (stage ids repeat across turns). A turn's first track render uses the defaults — flow open, stages collapsed. 在飞工具行不参与开合：挂行不顶卡、摘行不合卡，见「CEO Turn Timeline Rendering Contract」的在飞工具行条目。
- Turn closeout respects the operator's Flow container choice when a rail exists — `finalizeCeoTurn` does not force the container open; a track-less tool-event turn keeps its default-open closeout. Pause/approval holds still force the container open so a pending approval stays visible.
- Before a full rebuild, the browser captures a transient per-session view state from the live DOM: the anchor element (`data-ceo-key`, with a bottom-relative positional fallback) plus its in-element scroll offset; per-turn Interaction Flow open state and `展开全部` history expansion; per-stage `<details>` open state (scoped per turn because `stage_index` fallback trace keys collide across turns); and the active round tool chip (scoped by per-turn DOM order because an empty round key would collide across rounds).
- After the rebuild, the browser restores that state onto the new DOM and re-syncs the scroll position to the anchor element's new geometry (element-anchored, not pixel-preserving), re-pinning once more after a double rAF so late media/lazy output blocks settle; restored-open steps re-run output hydration because assigning `open` directly skips the toggle listener, and restored round tool selection re-activates its panel through `setTraceRoundActiveTool`. Operators following the bottom keep bottom-follow behavior; a missing anchor falls back to pixel clamp.
- Every feed mutation runs under a preserve-mode scroll snapshot (`captureCeoFeedScrollSnapshot` / `restoreCeoFeedScrollSnapshot` in `mutateCeoFeed` / `withCeoFeedBatch`): an at-bottom operator (`ceoFeedNearBottom`) follows new content to the bottom (stick-to-bottom), and a scrolled-up operator gets the position restored against the anchor element's post-mutation geometry, falling back to pixel clamp when the anchor is missing. The live follow pins synchronously without the async re-pin of `scrollCeoFeedToBottom`, so a pending frame cannot yank the operator back after they scroll up mid-stream.
- Stable identity is mandatory for rebuilt feed nodes: messages get `data-ceo-key="m:{turn_id}:{role}:{occurrence}"`, turns get `data-ceo-key="turn:{turn_id}"`, and the recorded message-key model (`S.ceoFeedRenderedMessageKeys`) plus the render signature are updated by every render path (full rebuild, incremental finalize, and the skipped-snapshot fast path alike). New render paths that add feed nodes without these keys, change stage `traceKey` composition, or fail to maintain the recorded model silently weaken scroll anchoring, rebuild-stable expand state, and the incremental-finalize gate.
- Capture is per-session and is skipped whenever the feed still shows a different session than the render target (`S.ceoFeedRenderSessionId`), so session switches and new-session loads never clone the previous session's view state.

### Per-Turn Token Usage Contract

- Each assistant turn renders a small per-response token line under the final bubble (`输入 X · 缓存命中 Y / P% · 输出 Z · 轮数 N · 完成于 HH:MM:SS`), built from a normalized usage object `{input_tokens, output_tokens, cache_hit_tokens, call_count}` plus an optional `thinking_tokens`, mapping directly onto normalized provider usage (`g3ku/providers/base.py`): `input_tokens` is the uncached lane, `cache_hit_tokens` the cache-read lane, `output_tokens` the generated output, `call_count` the visible frontdoor model calls accumulated for that turn (printed as `轮数`); `thinking_tokens` is written only when the provider reports `reasoning_tokens`, so a missing key means "not reported" rather than zero. That key is consumed by the reasoning row, not by this token line, and the browser turns none of these fields into a cost figure (contract: 「Live Reasoning Row Contract」). The one arithmetic it does perform is the hit rate printed next to 缓存命中: `P = cache_hit_tokens / (input_tokens + cache_hit_tokens)`, i.e. the share of the whole prompt that was a cache read — the same 口径 as the 命中率 column of the per-call table (`modelCallHitRate`), because `effective_input_tokens` on the server side is that same sum. The completion time is the assistant transcript message's `timestamp` for history and the browser clock at closeout for a live turn.
- `buildTokenUsageParts` (`g3ku/web/frontend/org_graph_app.js`) is the only producer of that wording, and three surfaces read it: the turn line (`setCeoTurnUsage`), the plain-bubble meta row (`buildCeoMessageMetaText`), and the node detail drawer header (`#ad-token-summary`, fed by that node's own `token_usage` from `GET /api/tasks/{task_id}/nodes/{node_id}`). A wording or 口径 change therefore lands once; a drawer header that disagrees with the session line means a second copy was written somewhere. `轮数` drops out when `call_count` is absent (legacy rows), and when no input/output lane carries a number the whole group is omitted instead of printing zeros — an untracked node header is simply empty.
- Visibility is state-dependent: while a turn runs the token line is always visible and updates live; once the turn settles (finalized, paused, or loaded from history) it collapses and reveals on a click inside that bubble — `toggleCeoMessageMetaReveal` toggles `.meta-open` on the `.message` root, and the next click takes it back. The reveal bails on clicks that already belong to something else (`button`/`a`/`summary`/`input`/`textarea`/`select`/`label`) and on the click that ends a text selection, so opening a stage card, copying, or selecting text never spends it. A settled turn with a known completion time shows at least the time segment, so a revealed row always carries something; only a turn with neither usage nor timestamp keeps the line hidden.
- The same row carries the bubble's copy affordance, and the row is the only place it appears: a bare `copy` lucide glyph at the same size and color as the metadata text, hidden with the row (`opacity: 0` + `pointer-events: none`) rather than removed, so nothing is permanently visible on a bubble. `.meta-open` is the sole reveal gate for both families — hover carries no reveal duty, because in a long session the pointer sweeping past lights up neighbouring bubbles at once. The two action buttons in that row sit outside the gate — the reply's Fork on the left of the usage text, the user bubble's 编辑 on the right of its send time — because they are actions rather than readings, and a blocked session still needs a visible exit. `.ceo-turn-meta` drops out of layout when usage is empty, but a row holding the Fork button is kept even then (the button does not live or die with the usage text). On an assistant turn the row is `.ceo-turn-meta`, which wraps `.ceo-turn-usage` and pins the glyph to the bubble's right edge; `setCeoTurnUsage` keeps writing `textContent` of the usage node, so the glyph is a sibling and never a child it would wipe. On user and track-less assistant bubbles the row is `.msg-meta`: `发送于` time for user messages, `完成于` time plus token usage for assistant fallbacks, with the glyph at the user bubble's left corner and the assistant fallback's right corner. `message-stack` 在用户车道仍按 `fit-content` 收拢（那一行的宽度等于正文宽，角标落在正文角上）；系统/回复车道已改成吃满整列（见「CEO Turn Timeline Rendering Contract」的不装盒条目），所以它的元信息行与复制角标落在**整列**的两端而不是正文右下角——这是"回复印在会话面上"的既定结果，不是回归。A bubble with attachments but no text gets no glyph.
- Copy reads the rendered visible text of that one bubble — `.assistant-text` for a rail turn (so the turn rail is excluded) and `.msg-content` otherwise — not the markdown source and not a server round-trip, and it reaches the clipboard through the shared `copyTextToClipboard` + `flashTraceCopyButton` lane used by tool-output copy. Clicks bind through one feed-level delegation on `[data-ceo-copy]`; per-bubble listeners would not survive feed rebuilds. Pinned by `tests/resources/org_graph_app.ceo_message_meta.test.js`.
- The live send path stamps the browser clock onto the bubble and into the cached inflight `user_message.timestamp`; server snapshots supply the authoritative transcript `timestamp` on both roles.
- The usage object reaches the browser through the same CEO websocket lanes as the turn it describes: `snapshot.ceo.messages[].usage` for persisted history, `inflight_turn.usage` / `preserved_turn.usage` for live turn patches, and `ceo.reply.final.usage` for the authoritative closeout.
- Consumers outside the process read that same object off the `message_end` payload: the session stamps it at the closeout emit (`_turn_usage_payload`, one normalized dict shared with the transcript write), so a bridge relay passes the event's own value through instead of looking up the per-turn accumulator by turn id — the accumulator lives in the emitting session and its keys are that session's own namespace. Outward contract: `external-agent-api.md`「事件流」.
- Live-turn value comes from the session's in-memory per-turn accumulator (`RuntimeAgentSession._frontdoor_turn_usage`, keyed by `turn_id` and incremented once per visible frontdoor `call_model`), exposed through `_build_execution_context_snapshot`. At closeout `_persist_turn_transcript` persists that per-turn aggregate onto the assistant transcript message (`usage` field), so history usage survives independently of artifact retention. Reopening a completed session prefers the transcript-level `usage` in `_build_ceo_snapshot` and falls back to aggregating persisted actual-request artifacts by `turn_id` (`read_session_turn_token_usage`) only for legacy transcripts without the field; all sources produce the same shape, and the aggregation participates in nothing else — baseline/handoff and cache-family decisions never read it.
- Browser-side stability invariants: the turn object keeps sticky usage/completedAt across finalize calls (`setCeoTurnUsage` never clears metadata it already holds, so the history re-finalize path cannot wipe the line); the feed render signature covers per-message `usage`/`timestamp`, so a cached render missing metadata is rebuilt when the authoritative snapshot arrives; and the finalized snapshot-cache write always carries the assistant message's `usage` and `timestamp`.
- The token line is display-only telemetry. It is not prompt-assembly input, not a shrink reason, and must not be used to gate rendering, queueing, or approval behavior.

### Heartbeat Compatibility

- Heartbeat turns count as active session work for the composer button and queueing logic.
- Queued follow-ups should not interrupt heartbeat execution.
- Active-turn follow-ups stay in the browser lane during heartbeat execution too, and become the next fresh user batch once heartbeat finishes; only a per-chip 插话 lets the backend see them earlier, where they may merge into the next safe `call_model` boundary of that same running turn.
- Terminal-event delivery is idempotent per session: a re-delivered `task_terminal` whose `dedupe_key` already produced a visible reply is acknowledged silently without running the agent or emitting a second `ceo.reply.final`, so the user sees that reply from the transcript snapshot instead of a live push, and the transcript stays authoritative. Key derivation, the durable callback/outbox boundaries, and the persisted `handled_terminal_dedupe_keys` list belong to `heartbeat-system.md`「Task Terminal Repair Contract」.

### Task Hall Action Contract

- The browser task hall exposes only `pause`, `resume`, and `delete` task actions; `retry`, `continue-evaluate`, and `open continuation` are absent from the UI and REST surface.
- Status pills derive from the current task `status` plus final-acceptance state, but the task's `is_paused` flag is checked **first**: a task in task-level pause renders as `已暂停` even while its `status` is still `in_progress`, and a task that reached `success`/`failed` never renders as `已暂停` because its pause flag is cleared on the terminal transition. What sets and clears that flag — it tracks the task root node's pause state — is owned by `main-task-runtime.md`「Node-Level Pause and Recovery」. The multi-select menu uses the backend buckets `已暂停` / `完成` / `未读` / `失败` / `未通过` / `进行中`; `完成` maps strictly to `success`, and `未通过` maps strictly to `unpassed`.
- Batch delete sends one `POST /api/tasks/bulk-delete` request with `task_ids`. The per-task response is authoritative: inspect each `items[]` result (`deleted`, `not_found`, `failed`) before choosing toast behavior.
- The task detail tree exposes node pause/resume controls; pause is local by default, and cascading asks about all descendants including inspection nodes. The backend stores `pause_requested`, `is_paused`, `pause_reason`, and an optional remark; a non-cascaded child continues until its parent reaches a safe boundary.
- Every node pause goes through the shared centered confirmation modal (never a native dialog, never a direct request), because pausing any node interrupts executing work: no node-pause request is sent before the user confirms, and cancelling sends nothing. When the node has an active or waiting child the modal explains that pausing the parent does not stop children and exposes an unchecked `同时暂停所有子节点（包括检验节点）` option, with confirmed `cascade` equal to that checkbox; a node without active children gets the same confirmation without the checkbox, and its request carries `cascade=false`. Only `resume` stays single-click.
- During task-level pause, the tree displays every non-terminal node as `任务暂停` via frontend projection; node pause fields remain unchanged, and recovery reveals any pre-existing node pause.
- The `错误日志` drawer reads `GET /api/tasks/{task_id}/error-log`, shows time, node, and error text, and treats the node id as a navigation target. Clicking it runs the shared tree-node locate pipeline (`locateTaskNode`): each ancestor's round selection switches to the round that contains the next hop, the ancestor's subtree is refetched with that round id (`GET /api/tasks/{task_id}/nodes/{node_id}/tree-subtree?round_id=...` — fetching the default round instead would delete old-round children from the frontend snapshot), then the node is centered and highlighted once; a node that cannot be displayed in any tree resolves to centering its nearest visible ancestor instead of failing silently. Pause/resume changes arrive through the task-node patch/snapshot path, not by reconstructing state from raw storage tables.
- 任务树左上角悬浮节点搜索框：输入节点 ID 或 goal 关键词后，在前端快照（`nodes_by_id`）中按“精确 > 前缀 > 包含”（ID 与 goal 同分优先 ID）排序展示候选，每条显示 goal、节点 ID 与状态。点击候选走同一定位管线并把缩放固定到 `TREE_FOCUS_SCALE`（1.2）以便看清节点；定位不清空搜索框内容与结果列表，方便在候选中连续跳转。定位命中的节点播放一次性放大脉冲动画（`.task-tree-node-locate`：放大到 1.18 倍并保持，配 #39c5bb 光圈后回落，约 1.2s）；动画窗口期记录在 `S.treeLocateHighlight`，整树重渲染后以负 `animation-delay` 补挂并从当前相位续播，保证运行中任务的高频重渲染不会吞掉高亮。因跳转到旧轮次而产生的手工轮次选择会让「回到最新树」按钮出现，点击它恢复所有节点的最新树视图。清空选择本身不够：切轮次走的是「先删旧子树再整体重建」的合并，被切过的那个节点在默认轮次下的孩子已经不在前端快照里，所以「回到最新」在确实存在手工选择时补一次整树快照，否则该节点会渲染成空子树，整棵树看起来像没加载出来。
- Task pause is a synchronous experience with an in-card hint: after a single-card or batch `pause` request succeeds, that task's card renders a small hint box (not a page-top toast) showing `暂停中 (stopped/total)` with a spinning icon and a `恢复运行` button while the backend reports draining, and switches to `暂停成功` (auto-hidden after ~2.5s) once draining ends; the page-top success toast is suppressed because the hint owns that feedback. Truth comes from `GET /api/tasks/{task_id}/pause-state` (`internal_total` / `internal_stopped` / `draining`), polled every 400ms with a ~30s cap: `draining` is true only while that task's `pause_task` command is still unfinished and a live worker exists, so `暂停成功` means the worker actors truly stopped rather than only the durable flags being set. The `恢复运行` button runs the ordinary resume path — worker command FIFO guarantees the resume lands after any still-pending pause command.
- 任务大厅性能条的「节点队列」一项读**回合闸**，三个数各带标签、不叠成 `N/M`：`entry_gate_running_total` 是运行（占住的格数）、`entry_gate_queued_total` 是等待（闸口排队）、`entry_gate_limits_total` 是当前容量（共享池现在开多大，不是配置地板，也不是上限）；任一读数缺失显示 `--`，不回落到模型侧队列——拿一个语义不同的数填位比空着更坏。这些键必须在 `GET /api/tasks/worker-status` 的顶层契约里，只活在 `worker.payload` 的 raw 心跳载荷里时前端读不到。为什么闸口排队才是「等待请求位」的读数、模型侧 `node_queue_*` 为什么仍留在历史与 `perf_inspect` 里，见 `runtime-overview.md`「节点回合闸（执行器存在的成本也要闸）」；每个任务自己的闸外排队数在 `runtime_summary.dispatch_queued` 与 `dispatch_pool`，合同见 `main-task-runtime.md`「节点调度读数与共享池」。
- 「监控新鲜度」一项量的是**距离上一次收到新读数过了多久**（`taskWorkerSampleAgeMsLive`）：归零的判据是读数身份 `pressure_sample_at` 变了，不是年龄小到某个阈值——心跳 1–2s 一拍、时间戳只有整秒精度，REST 回来的 `pressure_sample_age_ms` 稳态就在 2.4 秒上下，任何「年龄小于 1 秒」的条件在实盘永不会命中。身份不变时只在本地累加，所以读数停更后数字照旧往上走（` 5秒前`、` 5分钟前`、` 2小时前`）；读数越走越旧才算断供，读数变年轻只可能是来了新的。payload 里没有身份串时才退回「年龄变小才算新读数」。每档预留两位数字宽度，个位数前面**留空不补零**（`.task-performance-age-value` 的 `tabular-nums` + `white-space: pre` 保住前导空格），所以 9→10→99 秒这些跳变不改变胶囊宽度、整条性能条不抖；该项自带 `task-performance-item--freshness` 的左右留白。渲染沿用 1s 的性能条刷新（顺带做 worker 状态与大厅对账），不为它另起更快的钟。
- 「压力状态」与新鲜度**共用同一根按读数身份归零的秒表**（`taskWorkerPressureSnapshotFresh`）：身份 15 秒没变才算停更，标签带着时长（`停更16秒`、`停更3分钟`），从未拿到读数才显示 `未采样`；正常节拍 1–2s、WS 断了退到 5s 轮询再叠上秒级截断也才 7s 上下，15s 是三倍余量。性能条不读服务端那枚 `pressure_snapshot_fresh`——它的阈值是「年龄 ≤3s」，量的其实是这条流水线本身的多长，读数一直在更新时也会翻成 false。机器资源读数缺失（`machine_pressure_available=false`，此时「CPU/内存/磁盘」显示 `--`）单独显示「无资源读数」，不谎报停更。worker 连接状态优先于这两档：`离线`/`启动中`/`连接过期` 由 `normalizeTaskWorkerState` 判。
- 任务磁盘治理 UI（契约本体见 `runtime-overview.md`「磁盘写保护与治理」）：任务大厅性能条的磁盘数据与剩余空间合并显示在「CPU/内存/磁盘」一项的磁盘段（`0%(剩余10.1G)`，紧急=critical 着色落在该项上；数据来自 `GET /api/tasks/worker-status` 的 `machine_pressure_disk_busy_percent / machine_disk_free_bytes / disk_emergency_active`，1s 轮询；该接口只给最新快照，历史区间的读法与模型侧入口见 `runtime-overview.md`「Worker Performance History」和 `tool-and-skill-system.md` 的 `perf_inspect` 行），不单列「磁盘剩余」项，紧急态在任务网格顶部渲染红色横幅。任务卡片动作只有 `暂停/恢复/清除临时文件/删除`（kebab 菜单，`taskCardActions`），删除确认文案明示「报告类产出自动导出到 deliverables 目录保留」；「清除临时文件」同样走确认框（warn 档），成功后按 `freed_bytes` 报释放量、`failed_dirs` 非空时降级成「部分临时目录未删除」警示，两者都只重载列表让占用数字刷新、不移除卡片。两个动作的可见性判据不同且必须与后端逐字一致：`canDelete` 含暂停态，`canClearTemp` 只认 `success`/`failed`（清除契约见 `runtime-overview.md`「手动清除任务临时文件」）。任务数据没有归档/压缩/固定状态，也没有任何自动删除——任务只随用户手动删除（或模型删除工具）彻底清除，任务卡片经 WS `task.deleted` 推送从列表消失，无墓碑徽章；`.pc-corner-badge`、「已清理」chip、pin/compress/decompress 端点与按钮均不存在。

#### 任务大厅大小与排序

- 任务总占用 `disk_usage_bytes` 随 `GET /api/tasks` 与 WS `task.summary.patch` 下发（口径 = files/artifacts/event-history/temp 目录实测 + 五张大行表 payload 字节；小时对账 + 终态对账，展示延迟 ≤1h；patch 缺失该字段时前端合并保留旧值）。每张任务卡片渲染「占用大小」一行（`org_graph_tasks.js` `formatTaskBytes`），卡片增量补丁（`patchTaskCardElement`）同步刷新该值；任务由 `in_progress` 转终态时前端延迟一次全量 `loadTasks`，拿终态清理与对账后的最终值。大厅工具栏提供排序开关（`#task-sort-select`）：按时间（默认，创建时间倒序）或按大小（占用字节倒序，同为 0 时回落时间序）；排序是纯前端客户端行为（列表全量下发，比较器 `compareTaskListOrder` 与增量插入共用，切换经 `resortTaskOrderedIds` 重排并重置渲染签名；`diskUsage`/`sortMode` 进渲染签名）。排序状态不持久化，刷新回默认时间序。清除临时文件与终态清理都会在动作结束时同步重算一次对账，所以这两个动作后的占用大小是即时值，不等小时级 loop。模型侧对应能力：`task_stats` 工具 `mode=list` 支持 `sort=size`，大小读自 `task_disk_usage` 记账表（无记账的新任务兜底一次目录实测）。
- 任务卡片时间行与总耗时：卡片时间字段取自 `TaskListItem` 的 `created_at` / `finished_at`（`finished_at` 只在 `success`/`failed` 终态写入，窄读 `json_extract` 与整包回退两条路径都要产出，见上文列表读契约）。首行标签「开始」（`created_at`），任务进入终态时同行追加「结束」+ `finished_at`（`taskIsTerminal` 覆盖 `success`/`failed`/`unpassed`）。「占用大小」行右侧只显示分钟精度的耗时数字（`taskElapsedText`，如 `15h30m`，不足一小时降为 `30m`），无标签。耗时参考时刻的取法决定其是否增长：终态定格在 `finished_at`、暂停（`taskStatusKey=blocked`）定格在 `updated_at`（暂停后 actor 停止、无节点活动刷新该字段，故暂停期间数字不涨），运行中用 `Date.now()`。耗时只随渲染签名重建刷新——运行中卡片因 token/输出变化频繁重建而逐分钟推进，暂停或终态卡片不因心跳/读取动作刷新而保持稳定；不把 `now` 放进签名，以守住大厅滚动防抖。

- 卡片上「输入/输出/缓存」三格只有**一条**服务端来源：任务行的 `token_usage`（`S.tasksById[id].token_usage`，经 `taskTokenDisplayUsage` 换成含缓存的显示口径），而服务端那一格是加性累加。因此"这次带来的数比手里的小"必然是旧数据，两条写车道各挂同一个守卫（`resolveTaskTokenUsage`）：列表整表替换记 `list-apply`、补丁合并记 `patch-merge`，守卫保留手里那份较大的数，所以界面上这三格只会往前进。比较用 `tokenKnownTotal`（含缓存的输入 + 输出）一个标量，`tracked=false` 的全 0 行天然按旧数据处理，卡片不会因此退化成 `--`。每一次压制都可数：`S.taskHallStats.task_hall_metric_rollback_count` 计数，`S.taskMetricRollbacks` 存最近 12 条 `{at, task_id, metric, from, to, lane, source}`，其中 `source` 标出最后一次写这条数据的车道（`list` / `patch`）——这是判"整表替换盖住了补丁"还是"服务端某条整包回写把在飞的合并打回去"的唯一读数，两个修法各自独立，别在没读数之前改服务端。读数只在控制台，没有任何显示面依赖它。
- 另一处会同时看到两个数字的是 Token 统计窗口：顶部总量读任务行，下面「按模型明细」之和读节点汇总，而 spawn 评审那一车道只把用量并进节点、不并进任务总量（口径差异见 `main-task-runtime.md`），所以同任务实盘会出现明细之和大于顶部（`task:869c064afdb2`：37,272 vs 37,488 次调用、391,192,271 vs 391,645,384 输入）。这不是渲染分叉，是两本账，别在显示层对齐。

### Web Event Loop Contract

- The web server runs one uvicorn event loop shared by every `/api/*` route and websocket. All `/api/tasks/*` read handlers (`main/api/rest.py`) are async and run their synchronous query work (SQLite reads, pydantic parsing, snapshot building) via `asyncio.to_thread`, keeping the event loop itself free while heavy tree-snapshot or task-detail builds run. Control-plane handlers (pause/resume/cancel/delete/notice/bulk-delete) stay on the event loop.
- `GET /api/tasks/{task_id}` runs its `mark_read` step on the event loop before offloading the snapshot build: `mark_task_read` publishes `task.summary.patch` through the registry, whose subscriber queues are `asyncio.Queue`s that only the loop thread may touch. Any new query path that publishes to the registry must not move into `asyncio.to_thread`.
- Offloading to threads does not by itself unblock light reads, because `SQLiteTaskStore` funnels every read through one read connection guarded by `_read_lock`; a heavy read (multi-MB `task_node_details`/`nodes` payloads) would serialize the light reads behind it. The store therefore keeps a second `_light_read_conn` (own `_light_read_lock`) for the high-frequency small reads the task hall polls — `list_worker_status`, `list_task_summaries`, `get_task_disk_usages` — so they run concurrently with heavy reads under WAL instead of queueing. `_fetchall_light`/`_fetchone_light` route to it; `refresh_read_snapshot` and `close` service both connections. New high-frequency light reads should use the light fetch helpers; a reader must never mix the two connections in one transaction. A third shape covers one-shot bulk aggregates (whole-table `SUM(LENGTH(payload_json))` byte accounting): they open a dedicated read-only connection per call instead of taking either shared reader, because holding `_read_lock` across a multi-hundred-MB cold-page scan stalls unrelated control reads behind it (measured on a production copy: 43.0 s aggregate with an unrelated `fetchone:tasks` waiting 33.1 s, versus 0.1-0.2 ms on the dedicated connection). A freshly opened connection sees the current committed snapshot, so this lane does not inherit the light-connection staleness rule and stays usable as a decision input.
- `GET /api/tasks` reads through the narrow path: `SQLiteTaskStore.list_task_summaries` extracts the list fields from `tasks.payload_json` with `json_extract` instead of parsing the full `TaskRecord` blob per task (`user_request`/`final_output` reach megabytes on report tasks). `TaskQueryService.get_tasks` falls back to the full parse when the narrow read is unavailable, and both paths produce identical `TaskListItem` rows (equivalence pinned by `tests/resources/test_task_list_narrow_read.py`). New task-list fields need both the `json_extract` expression and the fallback builder.
- The CEO surface obeys the same protection. A `/ws/ceo` connect and `GET /api/ceo/sessions` build the session catalog across every CEO/channel transcript (a cold cache parses tens of MB of JSONL), load the requested session transcript, and project the transcript snapshot; all of it runs through `g3ku/runtime/ceo_catalog_offload.py` on one dedicated single-thread executor (`run_off_event_loop`), so a CEO WS reconnect — browser tabs wake from sleep and reconnect in bursts — never stalls task-hall endpoints. A short-TTL catalog cache collapses the repeated builds inside one connect/publish burst; write-side handlers (create/rename/delete) store their fresh catalog through `store_ceo_catalog_cache`, whose generation bump discards any stale in-flight async build result, and `POST /ceo/sessions/{id}/activate` builds through `build_ceo_session_catalog_async` for the same reason — a synchronous catalog build there pins the event loop for the whole session-switch click. The executor thread reads the same `SessionManager` as the loop, so `SessionManager.get_or_create` guards its load path to keep a session from ever loading into two forked objects. New CEO read paths that walk transcripts must go through the same executor instead of the event loop.
- The turn-event side of the CEO surface is protected the same way: every frame a tool event produces is built on the loop, so per-event work has a hard budget. Session catalog/patch rows for `ext:` channels resolve their bridge label through `g3ku.config.live_runtime.get_runtime_config()` (mtime-keyed cache) rather than `load_config()`, because that builder runs once per `state_snapshot` — i.e. per tool call — and `load_config()` re-runs the whole config migration and validation pipeline (~50 ms) each time. New per-event display helpers take the cached accessor; `SessionManager.get_or_create` on a warm cache and transcript row walks are the other two allowed costs there. What remains after those are the patch build itself, bounded by the per-connection coalescing window described in「Streamed Reply Delta Contract」and by the delta-only live frame rule in「CEO Turn Timeline Rendering Contract」.
- Web cron belongs to exactly one process per workspace, and that fact is read in-process, never from the listening socket. `g3ku/web/launcher.py::holds_web_start_lock` answers whether this process holds `.g3ku/start.lock`, which is acquired before uvicorn binds — so the attribution is settled for the whole process lifetime and does not depend on timing. Asking the port instead (netstat/ss on `web.port`) was wrong twice over: during a cold start nothing is listening yet, an empty owner set read as "another process owns it", and cron was skipped silently; and the probe shelled out from `_cron_runtime_ready`, which `ensure_web_runtime_services` consults on every internal task-event callback, freezing the loop for a second or more per worker event on connection-heavy machines (all task-hall requests stalled at once). Keep the cheap-first order: check the cron service status first — an enabled cron is ready with no attribution decision at all — and keep the attribution decision an attribute read. New readiness checks on this path must stay allocation-free on the loop or go through the offload executor.
- Slow reads report in two tiers, and the two tiers answer different questions (`main/storage/sqlite_store.py::_with_reader_lane`). Between `_SLOW_READ_MS` (200 ms) and `_SLOW_READ_EVENT_MS` (800 ms) the store logs `slow read: new reader lane section=… from=…` **once per (table, caller) pair for the whole process**, so that line's timestamp marks when the pair first crossed the threshold — not when a particular stall happened. Reading it as an event stamp attributes a slow request to the wrong query. At or above 800 ms the store logs `slow read: <section> took <ms> ms from=<caller>` on every occurrence, and that is the line to correlate against a wall-clock minute. Both tiers pay the stack walk only past 200 ms, so fast reads stay free. Pinned by `tests/resources/test_sqlite_slow_read_tiers.py`.
- The frontend icon pass is bounded by what is actually pending. `lucide.createIcons()` copies `data-lucide` onto the `<svg>` it produces, so the `[data-lucide]` set never empties and every call re-renders every icon in the document (measured 16–47 ms at 930 icons and 38–58 ms at 1654, roughly 30 µs per icon — the bill scales with the whole page, including the hidden conversation panel). `icons()` in `org_graph_app.js` therefore clears the marker off rendered `<svg>` nodes after each pass, which brings a repeat pass on the same page down to 1–2 ms. Nothing reads the attribute's value and no CSS selects `svg[data-lucide]`, so the removal is invisible; unreplaced `<i data-lucide>` keep their marker and still render, and the same-frame `requestAnimationFrame` coalescing is unchanged. Pinned by `tests/resources/org_graph_app.icon_pass.test.js`.
- `GET /api/tasks/{task_id}` carries the root node's execution trace summary, **not its body**. Measured on `task:1d9cddf9858e`: the trace body was 1.42 MB of the 1.82 MB response while the summary needs 85 KB, and building it cost `query_service.get_task_snapshot.root_node_detail` 449–780 ms — 1082 ms per request when eight details overlap, which is also when a 34 ms light request turns into 403 ms. The browser never reads that body from this lane: opening a task selects no node, `S.rootNode` is only read for `.node_id`, and the panel fetches the body itself through `detail_level='full'` on the per-node endpoint. So `TaskQueryService.get_node_detail(..., ship_execution_trace_body=False)` still resolves the trace (the summary's `latest_tool_calls_full` is derived from it) and clears only the outgoing field, and `get_task_snapshot` reads it through `_snapshot_root_node_detail`, which reuses the built copy while the detail row's stamp holds — bounded to `_SNAPSHOT_ROOT_DETAIL_CACHE_MAX_TASKS` tasks and `_SNAPSHOT_ROOT_DETAIL_MAX_AGE_SECONDS`, because `updated_at` is second-granular and "same stamp" therefore only guarantees "changed ⇒ rebuilt", never "unchanged ⇒ definitely unchanged". The cached dict is shared by reference: nothing outside writes `root_node[...]`, and the frontend merges it into a fresh object, so a cached copy can never be seasoned by a later consumer. Pinned by `tests/resources/test_snapshot_trace_lane.py` and `tests/resources/test_watchdog_reads_trace_summary.py`.
- The web process's own long-block ledger is readable from the browser-facing endpoint, because the worker's copy cannot answer a web-side question. `main/api/rest.py::get_task_worker_status` returns `web_long_blocks` (last 8) and `web_worst_long_blocks` (longest 8, with `started_at`) from this process's `RuntimeDebugRecorder`, alongside the worker payload it already returns. Without it, a request that took seconds while logging no read over `_SLOW_READ_EVENT_MS` stays unexplained: the ring names the section that held the process — `sqlite.query.*`, `query_service.get_task_snapshot`, `runtime_service.publish_live_snapshot` — with its own duration, which is what separates "one query was slow" from "this process was busy with something else". The recorder only keeps blocks ≥200 ms, and reading it is an in-memory snapshot with no query path. Pinned by `tests/resources/test_web_long_block_lane.py`.
- Slow `/api` requests leave their own marker in `console.log`, so "the browser was waiting on the server" is decided after the fact instead of needing an operator watching the page. `g3ku/web/main.py::slow_request_log_middleware` times every request and emits `slow web request: <METHOD> <path> -> <status> in <ms> ms` when an `/api*` path exceeds `_SLOW_REQUEST_LOG_MS` (1000 ms). It is observation only: it never alters the response, logs nothing below the threshold, and skips the page and static assets, because those are not what a hall/detail click waits on. WebSocket routes (`/api/ws/*`, `/ws/ceo`) are outside its reach — ASGI http middleware does not see an upgrade — so a stalled live lane is still measured from the browser side. The registration position carries meaning: `@app.middleware` pushes each new entry to the *front* of `app.user_middleware` and Starlette then wraps from the back inward, so the middleware declared **last** is the outermost one. The timing layer is declared after `bootstrap_lock_middleware` and `audit_error_capture_middleware` for exactly that reason — declared before them it would only measure route handling and report a number smaller than what the browser waited for. `tests/resources/test_web_slow_request_log.py` pins that outermost position by reading the built app, not the source text.
- Task-hall grid rebuilds are signature-gated and scroll-preserving (`org_graph_tasks.js`). The render signature (`taskGridRenderSignature`) may only contain values that change rendered pixels: `workerLastSeenAt` was removed after it turned every worker heartbeat into a full grid rebuild — the grid element is its own scroll container, and the rebuild's `innerHTML=""` reset `scrollTop` to zero, so hall scrolling looked frozen while the performance bar kept updating. `renderTasks` captures `scrollTop` before a same-page rebuild and restores it afterwards; a page-number change intentionally starts at the top. In-place token updates go through `patchTaskCardElement` and re-stamp the signature without rebuilding. The three token metrics carry one growth cue, and its unit is the digit, not the number: `taskMetricValueMarkup` diffs the newly formatted text against the rendered one character by character and wraps only the changed positions in `.pc-metric-digit.is-increasing`, so a 10-digit counter keeps its stable prefix at body color while just the digits that call moved — plus any carry-affected ones — play the one-shot `pc-token-breathe` color swell. A metric that did not grow renders as plain text. Both render paths use that helper: the patch path for in-place cards, and `renderTasks` for rebuilt cards, where only the task ids in `taskMetricAnimationTaskIds` (set by the patch handler, cleared at the end of the rebuild) are eligible. Rebuilding the spans restarts the animation by itself, so nothing removes and re-adds a class or forces a reflow to replay it. The animation is the deliberate exception to the global `prefers-reduced-motion` silencing (the glow drops, the fade stays), so "tokens grow with no feedback" points at the digit diff and that exemption rather than at the media query; the `.pc-metric` box itself carries no highlight style. New fields belong in the signature only if they change visible output; anything ticking with time (heartbeats, clocks) stays out. Pinned by `tests/resources/org_graph_tasks.hall_scroll_survival.test.js`.

### Task Tree Live Sync And Self-Healing Contract

- The task-detail websocket (`/api/ws/tasks/{task_id}`) reconnects automatically at 1s intervals after an unexpected disconnect while its task detail view is still open and `S.currentTaskId` still names that task. Intentional closes (switching views, opening another task) detach the close handler before closing and never schedule a reconnect; the task-list WS follows the same reconnect policy.
- Live events missed during a disconnect window are not replayed. After a reconnect the browser therefore refetches the task detail and the full tree snapshot with `preserveView + reopenSocket:false` to re-align state; manual round selections survive that reconcile.
- The tree snapshot cache defends itself against corrupting incremental merges:
  - `applyTaskTreeSubtreePayload` rejects any subtree response that does not contain its own `root_node_id` and falls back to a full `tree-snapshot` reload. Its merge is a destructive wipe-of-old-subtree-then-rebuild, and a response missing its root would cut that root out of the cache; when the subtree root is the tree root, the whole tree disappears while node search still matches the stale cached entries.
  - When `renderTree` finds `S.treeRootNodeId` missing from the cache, it renders the empty state and schedules a debounced full-snapshot reload, capped at two attempts; a successful full-snapshot apply resets that budget.
- `renderTree` replaces the whole `U.tree` DOM, so anything carrying user interaction state must be re-findable afterwards by a stable identity rather than surviving the rebuild:
  - 节点树按父节点平铺，不按派生轮次分组：一个父节点的全部子节点平铺渲染，轮次下拉与「回到最新树」控件已删除。`S.treeSelectedRoundByNodeId` 只为旧状态兼容保留，恒为空。后端侧 `log_service` / `task_projector` 已停止写 `task_node_rounds`，`query_service` 不读它：快照的 `rounds`/`default_round_id` 恒空，子节点关系由 `auxiliary_child_ids`（全部直接子节点）与 `parent_node_id` 表达。表定义保留以兼容旧库，新代码不得写入。
  - 终态过滤与展开：运行中的任务默认只显示非终态节点（决策 C），终态执行子节点折叠、验收节点无条件附着；**任务本身到终态后默认显示整棵树**——`treeShowTerminalEffective()` 在用户未手动切换时按 `S.currentTask.status` 推导，手动切换过（`S.treeShowTerminal` 为 true/false）以手动值为准。折叠必须留入口：节点行渲染 `▸N`（`hidden_child_count`）展开钮，展开状态存于 `S.treeExpandedNodeIds`，只放出该节点的**直属**子节点（逐层展开）。终态节点底下还有存活后代时该分支强制可见（`contains_live`），否则非终态节点会连同祖先链一起消失。节点行同时渲染未读消息角标（`pending_notice_count`，设计 §10）。搜索定位（`locateTaskNode`）会把命中节点的每一级父节点加进 `S.treeExpandedNodeIds`，否则被过滤的终态节点命中后无处高亮。
  - `fitTaskTreeToView` 的 `W`/`T`（包装器自身的布局偏移）**不除 `scale`**：`transform-origin: 0 0` 下 `rendered = layout + offset`，只有容器内部的 `C`/`D` 与树尺寸按 `scale` 换算。此前两者都被除了一次，布局偏移被放大 `1/scale` 倍，内边距一变树就被推出视口。适配区域还要扣掉悬浮搜索框与悬浮工具的高度（`taskTreeTopOverlayInset`），否则按整块容器居中的第一行正好压在搜索框底下。
  - （节点树的轮次下拉与「回到最新树」控件已删除；本条是 `buildResourceSelect` 这条弹出控件稳定 id 合同在其余弹出控件上的形状。）An expanded popup is restored, not preserved: the builder gives the `<select>` an id that does not change between rebuilds, `buildResourceSelect` copies it onto the shell as `data-select-id`, and `renderTree` finishes by calling `restoreOpenResourceSelect(U.tree)`, which reopens the shell named by `S.openResourceSelectId` and returns focus to the selected option. `S.openResourceSelectId` is cleared on close (option picked, Escape, click away), so a rebuild that follows a selection does not pop the menu open again. A node whose dropdown is gone from the new tree is skipped silently. Any future in-tree popup control needs the same stable id, because `buildResourceSelect` falls back to a random `selectId` when `select.id` is empty and a random id cannot be recognized after a rebuild.
- Node badges state what the node is doing, never its raw status token: a non-terminal node renders 运行中 / 请求中 / 检验中 / 等待中 and never `IN_PROGRESS`. The judgement lives in `resolveTreeNodeStatusLabel` and reads three live inputs — the node's own runtime frame (phase, `stale`, `await_marker`) and its acceptance children's statuses:
  - `运行中` / `检验中` require the node to be inside its own turn: `isLiveTurnFrame` accepts the `before_model` / `after_model` / `message_distribution` phases or a still-in-flight tool call, and rejects a frame that is waiting on children. A frame normally exists only while that node's turn is in flight (`remove_frame` clears it when the turn returns a terminal result, and the task's terminal path clears the whole set), so "has a frame in a turn phase" is the browser's evidence of real execution. An acceptance node in that state reads 检验中, an execution node 运行中. The phase set mirrors the `runnable` derivation in `main/monitoring/log_service.py::_frame_index_payload`; changing one means changing the other.
  - `等待中` covers every other non-terminal node: a parent whose spawn round has not returned, an acceptance node that has never been dispatched, and an acceptance node idle because its verdict sent its execution node back for a retry. 检验中 therefore never stands for "an acceptance node exists".
  - `请求中` is the in-flight sub-state of 运行中: the node's own fresh frame carries `await_marker` = `model.chat.dispatch` or `model.chat.await_response`, i.e. the provider call has been sent and its stream has not come back. It writes `visualState: "running"`, so the badge keeps the execution blue and the card treatment is unchanged — the label only names which part of the turn is holding. `model.chat.response_postprocess` is deliberately excluded: by then the stream has ended. A stale frame never reaches this branch (`stale` is checked first), so a node that stopped producing frames at 15:00 reads 等待中 rather than a permanent 请求中.
  - A submitted execution node (`success`) whose acceptance child has not reached a terminal status is still `等待中`, not a completion label: the delivery has not been accepted yet.
  - Pause outranks all of this: a task-level pause renders every non-terminal node as 任务暂停; a node-level pause with `pause_reason=error` renders 暂停·异常 (and is the emphasised state for `reject_no_progress` / watchdog stops), any other reason renders 暂停·普通. Parked phases render 等待（显式）/ 等待（验收待决）, and an in-flight tool call renders 运行中（工具在途）. Tool-side reading contract (`task_progress`) lives in `main-task-runtime.md`「Node-Level Pause and Recovery」.
- Every rewrite of a node's `status` must land in both tree readings at once: the projection row the `tree-snapshot` serves, and a `task.node.patch` frame. `update_node_status` performs both by itself. The acceptance lane writes status through four paths that bypass it — a rejection reactivating the inspected node and its acceptance node (`NodeRunner._reactivate_node_for_retry`), voiding a stored verdict before re-verifying (`NodeRunner._reset_terminal_acceptance_for_reverify`), waking a terminal acceptance node with a new submission, and canceling a live acceptance node when its execution node fails terminally (`NodeRunner._cancel_waiting_acceptance_for_execution_failure`) — plus the distribution-side void (`LogService.invalidate_acceptance_node`). All of them go through one exit, `main/monitoring/log_service.py::publish_node_state`, which re-projects the node and then publishes a frame built from its current status. Skipping the frame leaves the badge frozen on the previous verdict while the snapshot already says otherwise — the whole rework window reads `FAILED` on the acceptance node and `SUCCESS` on the node it kicked back, until a branch sync or reopen; skipping the projection keeps a terminal `failed` serving as non-terminal, so no reload turns it into `FAILED`.
- Because those inputs arrive on `task.live.patch` and `task.node.patch`, badges move with the runtime: `refreshRenderedTreeNodeStatuses` re-derives the whole tree model and patches each rendered node's badge, card color and connector color in place on the next animation frame, without refetching the snapshot. The same pass refreshes the open node drawer's status line (`syncSelectedTaskNodeDetailStatus`), so a selected node's label tracks its activity without reopening the drawer. Display state must therefore not be computed from snapshot-only fields: `task.node.patch` carries node-record fields, and `children_fingerprint` changes only when the child set changes, so a label derived from handshake or other snapshot projection fields stays frozen until a branch sync or a reopen. Not every frame write produces a patch: a rewrite whose content fingerprint is unchanged is dropped, and clearing the turn-level `await_marker` on await exit writes the frame row without pushing (`runtime-overview.md`「帧写与推送不是一对一」). A label that must move therefore needs its state carried by a write that does push — which is what `model.chat.dispatch` / `model.chat.await_response` do, so `请求中` never depends on the demoted turn-level clears.
- A frame the backend marks `stale` never reads as in-turn. Every runtime frame reaching the browser carries `stale` and `await_marker` — `TaskLiveFrame` on the task snapshot, and on `task.live.patch` either inside `runtime_summary.frames` (full payload) or inside `runtime_summary.stale_node_ids` (delta payload, which carries only the changed frame) — derived from the frame row's own `updated_at` against `main/monitoring/frame_liveness.py::STALE_FRAME_MINUTES`, the same threshold `task_progress` uses for its 疑似中断 demotion (`main-task-runtime.md`「Node-Level Pause and Recovery」). The patch channel spreads the frame projection so it carries every field, while the snapshot channel serializes a model: a badge predicate that reads a frame field needs that field on `TaskLiveFrame` too, otherwise the label only appears after the first patch and the two transports disagree on the same node. The tree demotes such a node to 等待中 and does not borrow the tool's 疑似中断 wording: a stale frame proves nobody rewrote it, not that the node stopped existing, and the browser has no frame age to quote. The patch channel comes in two shapes: a full one re-sends every frame, a delta one (`runtime_summary.frames_partial === true`) sends the single changed frame plus the three role lists and the stale id list, and the browser merges it into `S.frontier` / `S.liveFrameMap` with `mergeTaskLiveFrameDelta` (order preserved, `stale` re-stamped in place). Structural writes — a node's first frame, a removal, a bulk replace — always send the full shape, so the merge only ever adds an entry as a fallback; a delta arriving with an empty frame table triggers `reconcileTaskDetailAfterWsReconnect` instead of being applied. The server side of that split is owned by `runtime-overview.md`「帧写与推送不是一对一」. The flag is computed while the payload is built, so it ages out on the next patch or reload rather than on a browser-side clock.
- 活跃节点 (`syncTaskTreeHeaderState`, which also writes `S.taskSummary.active_node_count`) counts nodes in their own turn plus paused non-terminal nodes. It uses the same in-turn predicate as the badges so that undispatched acceptance nodes, which are always rendered, do not inflate it.
- If an operator reports "the task tree is frozen although the task is running", check the tab's task-detail websocket first: a tree stuck on a stale snapshot means the detail WS dropped without a working reconnect. Confirm the reconnect loop in the browser console before debugging the runtime side.

### Task Tree Chunked Load Contract

- `GET /api/tasks/{task_id}/tree-snapshot` serves one bounded chunk per request when `max_nodes` is set: nodes are sliced in a stable order (`sort_key, node_id`), so consecutive chunks never overlap, and the response carries `truncated`, `total_node_count`, and `next_after_node_id`. The browser continues with `after_node_id=next_after_node_id` until `truncated=false`. Without pagination params the endpoint keeps the historical whole-snapshot payload; the per-node subtree endpoint is unchanged.
- `_build_tree_snapshot` materializes only the current chunk's nodes and reads per-node state through the light projection rows (pause/handshake/pending-notice fields are written there at sync time); the runtime `nodes` blobs are fetched only for legacy projection rows that predate those fields — a per-node `get_node` when the row carries no handshake state, plus one batched read per build for rows lacking the pending-notice count. The node badge `pending_notice_count` = the projection's root-side count plus that node's delivered child notices, and the child half is one `list_task_delivered_notifications(task_id)` read per build whose rows are filtered by `is_system_relay_notice` into a `node_id → count` map; querying notices per node is a rule violation, not a micro-optimization, because `task_node_notifications` only carries its primary-key autoindex, so every per-node notice read scans the task's whole notice set (a 1,227-node task measured 4.64s for those scans against 17ms for the single read). Per-node full-payload `get_node` reads are what made whole-tree builds exceed the browser's 10s default timeout on large trees, which is why display state is derived in the browser instead of from an extra per-node read here. Token aggregation likewise reads `payload_json` via `json_extract` instead of parsing whole detail payloads.
- The build is instrumented in three named sections — `query_service.get_tree_snapshot` (whole request unit of work), `query_service.build_tree_snapshot.projection_maps` (the per-build whole-task projection read every chunk repeats), and `query_service.build_tree_snapshot.node_materialization` (badge counts plus per-node model builds). Without them the lane is invisible on the long-block board: the board only keeps segments ≥200 ms, and this lane's cost arrives as hundreds of few-millisecond queries, so a multi-second chunk registers as nothing. Board semantics (recent vs worst list, threshold, how to read a silent board) belong to `runtime-overview.md`「Worker Performance History (perf_samples)」.
- The browser treats the chunk pipeline as the task-open bootstrap: `loadTaskTreeSnapshot` resets the tree cache on the first chunk, merges later chunks by node id, and gates `renderTree` via `S.treeBulkLoadingTaskId` so no partial tree renders mid-load. While loading it shows a one-line progress notice `正在打开任务：加载中 (loaded/total)` in its own element (`U.taskLoadNotice` / `#task-load-notice`, a pill inside the toast viewport): no title row, no close button, `pointer-events: none`, and it stacks above a toast rather than overlapping it. Its vertical offset is measured at each show against the tree's top-left search box (`.task-tree-search`), so the line lands on the search box's height — the details header wraps at narrower window widths, which moves that box by ~80px, so the offset is not a constant; when the anchor is missing the CSS default applies. The notice belongs to the task-open path only — `loadTaskTreeSnapshot` arms/updates/finishes it solely under `announceLoad`, which `loadTaskDetail` sets when `preserveView` is false. In-place reloads never raise it: node pause/resume, append-notice, snapshot self-heal and subtree fallback call `loadTaskTreeSnapshot` without `announceLoad`, and the `loadTaskDetail({preserveView: true})` refreshes (pausing/resuming the currently open task, batch actions, the post-reconnect reconcile) fall under the same rule; reporting 正在打开任务 there reads as if the task had been reopened. An in-place reload that supersedes an in-flight open flow cancels the open flow's stale notice instead of adopting it. The notice first appears after a ~400ms delay (while the task-detail request is still in flight it reads `0/…`), so small-tree opens do not flash it; once visible, every chunk updates the numbers — the chunk-count anti-flicker gate only decides whether to *start* showing, never whether to update. When the last chunk lands, the final numbers stay visible for ~800ms before the notice hides (show then immediately hide would leave no painted frame), and the loader renders the tree once, keeping the existing fit-to-view behavior. A mid-load failure shows the inline tree error plus an error toast.
- A per-load token (`S.treeBulkLoadToken`) invalidates an older chunk loop when a reconnect or reopen starts a newer load for the same task; chunk merges are idempotent by node id. A vanished cursor node restarts the slice from the head — the id-based merge skips nothing.
- Leaving the task-detail view cancels the tree-load pipeline instead of letting it finish in the background (`cancelTaskTreeLoading`, called from `switchView`): the per-load token is bumped so an in-flight chunk loop stops at its next request boundary, the load notice and snapshot self-heal timer are cleared, pending branch-sync timers and dirty flags are dropped, and a view generation (`treeDetailGeneration`) is bumped so an in-flight `tree-subtree` response is discarded before its merge. `loadTaskTreeSnapshot`, `renderTree`, and branch-sync scheduling additionally early-return while the detail view is hidden, so operation callbacks (node pause/resume, append-notice) cannot relaunch the chunk stream or a full tree rebuild from the task hall, and a `loadTaskDetail` that resolves after the user has left applies nothing and does not reopen the task-detail WS. Re-entering a task always runs a full reset-and-reload, so the cancellation discards no visible state; it exists to keep the task hall responsive instead of burning chunk requests and whole-tree DOM rebuilds in the background.
- The same exit path releases the retained detail state and its rendered DOM (`releaseTaskDetailRetainedState`): the whole-tree snapshot, per-node full details, context caches, and the trace/message containers are dropped so the hidden view does not keep megabytes of heap and thousands of DOM nodes resident. On memory-overcommitted machines a large resident tab gets trimmed and paged out by the OS, and the next interaction freezes for seconds while it faults back in (queued wheel input replays all at once). Reopening a task refetches everything, so the release costs no visible state. When leaving the detail view, the release and its companions (view-state capture, token-stats drawer close, persist scheduling) do not run in the click frame: `switchView` synchronously cuts the live WS and cancels the tree load, then defers the heavy steps to the next macrotask, so the click frame only flips views and fires the hall requests — the same requests the sidebar nav fires (`GET /api/tasks`, `/api/ws/tasks`, worker-status). This aligns the back-arrow timing with the two-hop sidebar detour path (which users observed as smooth) instead of stacking the detail teardown's concentrated cold-memory touches with the hall re-render in one frame; if the user re-enters the detail view within the same tick the deferred teardown is voided wholesale. Pinned by `tests/resources/org_graph_app.task_detail_exit_defer.test.js`. (History: a temporary two-hop detour shim — back-arrow via the conversation-list view — was trialed to spread the exit work across frames and reverted unchanged once instrumentation showed the perceived freezes came from heartbeat-driven whole-grid rebuilds (`taskGridRenderSignature`) and machine-level memory storms, not from the back path's click-frame timing.)
- `ApiClient.getTask` uses a 120s client timeout instead of the 10s default: the payload scales with task size and the 10s bound surfaced as `Failed to open task: Request timeout` on large trees.

### Node Detail Redraw And Expansion State Contract

- 节点详情抽屉的三条列表（执行流程 / 消息列表 / 派生记录）每次刷新都是**整段重建**：`renderExecutionTrace` 比对的 renderSignature 由每行的 key、标题、状态与正文拼成，工具一有动静就变，变了就整块换掉 `innerHTML`。卡片开合（`<details open>`）与轮内选中的工具面板都只活在 DOM 上，重建即清零，所以这三条列表的状态必须在重建前捕获、重建后按稳定身份还原（`captureTaskDetailViewState` → `applyTaskTraceItemViewState`）。
- 捕获取**渲染前 DOM 的当下状态**，不是详情请求发起前的那一次：`showAgent` 在入口捕获之后还要 await 一次节点详情读，这段在飞窗口足够用户再点开几张卡，沿入口那份还原就是把用户刚放下的位置折回去。只有当屏上已经没有这个节点的行（首帧，或详情未缓存时被 loading 占位顶掉）才退回入口捕获或按节点存档。
- 还原的身份是两级：整行靠 `data-trace-key`（工具组为 `turn:<seq>`）；一张卡里可以多组，每轮各自选中一个面板，靠 `data-round-key` → `roundActiveToolKeys` 这份**按轮账本**放回正确那一轮。视图态每经过一次 `normalizeTaskDetailViewState`（补丁车道的 pending 存档、`S.taskDetailViewStates`、sessionStorage 快照都要过它），这份按轮账本就得同字段穿过去：只剩整卡第一条选中项时还原器会把它兜到第一轮，别的轮的面板等于被折掉，所以已有按轮账本时不做那次兜底。
- 还原只搬用户放下的位置：不替用户展开卡片（承载内容的卡收起时内容跟着藏起来，要看进度自己点开），也不替用户合上；跨节点不串态，另一个节点按自己的存档起板，没有存档时按默认档（阶段卡只展开末位）。
- 抽屉头部那颗副标题（`#ad-token-summary`）只印这一格 token 读数，文案与口径归「Per-Turn Token Usage Contract」：详情读落地后按 `mergedNode.token_usage` 写一次，首帧、切节点的 loading 档与 `resetTaskView` 都把它清空，所以头部空白读作"这一格还没数"（未加载完，或该节点没有模型调用），不是坏了。派生轮次与子节点完成数不在头部出现——轮次状态在树节点自己的行上看（轮次下拉与「回到最新树」见「Task Tree Live Sync And Self-Healing Contract」）。
- 回归 `tests/resources/org_graph_task_view.refresh_keeps_expanded.test.js`。

### Node Detail Error History

- The node detail drawer embeds a `历史错误记录` section between `验收` and `日志`. It reads `GET /api/tasks/{task_id}/nodes/{node_id}/error-log` and lists that node's raw records (time, node title, full error text) newest-sequence first; an empty node shows `暂无历史错误`, and a header `刷新` button refetches.
- History rows are the same `task_error_logs` records as the task-level `错误日志` drawer, filtered by node. They are durable history, not live-only state: they persist while the task exists and are deleted with the task.
- The runtime appends a record for every invalid final-result submission while the node is still running (not only when the node dies): the entry carries submission count, `response_tool_call_count`, tool names, `output_tokens`, `finish_reason`, provider model, `sent_max_tokens`, `sent_reasoning_effort`, plus a `疑似触及输出token上限被截断` marker when `output_tokens` reached the sent cap **or** `finish_reason=length`. 发送值只在自行组请求体的 adapter 上可得（Chat Completions 带参数面副本、Responses 带完整 body）；两条都拿不到时 `finish_reason` 仍独立承载截断信号，否则被截断的提交会被读成普通的缺字段错误。A reasoning-only reply additionally names what bound it in its first line — `[output-capped]`（思考吃满声明的上限）/ `[window-clamped]`（被窗口剩余挤掉）/ `[not-truncated]`（`finish_reason=stop`，没有上限拦住它）/ `[unknown]`（无发送值可判）；判据只比回执 `output_tokens` 与发出的输出上限（Chat 的 `max_tokens` 或 Responses 的 `max_output_tokens`），不读 `context_window_tokens`。Enough for operators to confirm truncation or no-tool-call failures without reopening request artifacts. The row heading itself carries the attribution: a row on the reply-shape lane leads with `Upstream payload shape fault detected (第N次)`, a row rejecting a payload the node authored leads with `Invalid final result submission detected (第N次)`, and the reason names the shape inside (`provider output limit truncated ...`, `reply carried no tool call and no text (reasoning-only)`, `provider tool-call serialization fault [parameter-marker] ...`). The marker row also appends `upstream_raw_bytes` and `upstream_raw_tail`: the gateway's argument string as the stream delivered it, before lenient parsing, capped at `_RAW_ARGUMENTS_TAIL_LIMIT` characters because the end is where the answer lives. Those bytes reach the record only because `ToolCallRequest.raw_arguments` carries them out of the provider boundary (both the streaming accumulator and the non-streaming parse); nothing below that boundary can reconstruct them, and every other reading in the row is post-parse — whether the parameters after the marker ever arrived is exactly what separates "repair it here" from "stop using this chain member". Lane budgets and dispositions: see `main-task-runtime.md`「Node-Level Pause and Recovery」.

### Node Output Content Read Contract

- `GET /api/content/{describe,search,open,read}` plus the excerpt/`full` lane of `GET /api/tasks/{task_id}/artifacts/{artifact_id}` are the HTTP face of `ContentNavigationService` (`g3ku/content/navigation.py`). They split by how much body one surface is willing to hold. `open` answers **one screen** of body — line-aligned under `OPEN_EXCERPT_CHAR_LIMIT` (16000 chars) and `MAX_OPEN_LINES` (200), with `start_line` / `end_line` / `truncated` in the payload — and is the only content route wired into the frontend: the node detail drawer, the CEO feed and the media viewer's 查看完整 lane all hydrate a stored ref through it (见「In-App Media Viewer And Download Lane」). Every body line of this lane carries an absolute line-number gutter, so those surfaces render the numbers too; the contract (which lanes add it, which do not, and why) is owned by `tool-hydration-and-callable-chain.md`「外置工具结果信封」. A whole multi-MB body never reaches the app page in one shot: that means five full-string escape scans, a `JSON.parse`, an indent-2 re-serialisation and a single `textContent` swap on the main thread — measured on a 7,024,971-character single-line node-detail artifact that is a frozen page, while the same click through `open` is 31,973 bytes / ~0.25 s against 11,530,388 bytes through `read` (the server-side cost of either is the gunzip, ~126 ms). `read` is therefore not a panel lane: its whole-file body serves the OpenAI-compatible `response.content` route (`g3ku/runtime/api/chat_render.py`), and `search` / `describe` stay model-facing. `open_content` and `read_content` answer without `await service.startup()`, unlike `describe_content` / `search_content`, so a cold process can fail those two differently.
- A ref whose bytes are gone is an expected state rather than a server fault: disk governance prunes a task's `temp/tasks/` outputs and its artifact rows while node trace rows keep quoting those refs. `_resolve` reports it as `FileNotFoundError`, and `main/api/rest.py::_content_action` answers `404` with `detail.code` `content_not_found`; a ref that resolves to something other than a file answers `400` with `content_ref_invalid`. Both sit under the `≥500` audit threshold, so a cleaned output never enters the log audit stream. Pinned by `tests/test_web_content_ref_error_mapping.py`.
- Hydration de-duplicates by ref **and window start line**, not by DOM node, because every panel render recreates the code block: `getTraceOutputWindowByRef` stores a `404` as a terminal cache marker, and the block renders `完整输出已被清理，仅保留预览片段` under the retained preview while the copy lane keeps returning the preview text alone. Any other failure (503, timeout, transport) stays retryable.
