The panel unhides 继续加载 only while the target actually has more lines than it has been given (`end_line < line_count`, and the window must have advanced at all): `truncated` alone only means "this requested range did not fit", so a page delivered exactly full reports `truncated: false` at line 168 of 420 and the button would vanish with the body still unread. A single-line artifact never gets the button — `open` reports `start_line == end_line == 1` there and the next window would return the same segment forever, so that case shows the server's own truncation notice and nothing else. Text that has no ref to page through (tool 参数 from the ledger, archived stage rounds) is capped at **display** time by `capTraceDisplayText` under the same 16000-char one-screen contract, while `ensureTraceArgumentsCodeBlockContent` still returns the full body to the copy lane — display bound and copy completeness are two different promises there. A block that does carry a ref copies exactly the window it fetched; 查看完整 hands that same ref to the in-app media surface, where 继续加载 walks the remaining windows (见「In-App Media Viewer And Download Lane」). Pinned by `tests/resources/org_graph_app.trace_output_cleaned.test.js` and `tests/resources/org_graph_task_view.tool_output_copy.test.js`.

### Task Token Stats Window Contract

- The task-detail `Token统计` modal (`ensureTaskTokenUi` in `g3ku/web/frontend/org_graph_app.js`, `renderTaskTokenStats` in `g3ku/web/frontend/tasks/hall-view.js`) renders a five-tile topline (总输入 / 总输出 / 缓存命中 / 缓存命中率 / 模型调用), the per-model breakdown, and the 模型调用明细 table. The topline is where the window prints its totals: the header line under the title is fixed operator wording, and the only other place those numbers appear is the hover tooltip of the `Token统计` button itself (`taskTokenSummaryLine`). 缓存命中率 divides 缓存命中 by the whole prompt (新增输入 + 缓存命中) — the same 口径 as the per-call 命中率 column (`modelCallHitRate`) — and reads the same converted object as the 总输入 tile, so the two cannot disagree. The table's data is `recent_model_calls` of the task snapshot merged with live `task.model.call` websocket events; the snapshot carries a bounded window only (`main/monitoring/query_service.py::_recent_model_calls`, default `_TASK_SNAPSHOT_MODEL_CALL_ROWS` = 300, SQL-side LIMIT) because the ledger grows linearly with calls (a live in-flight task measured 31686 rows / 75 MB, and reading it whole cost 1979 ms warm / 18.4 s on cold pages). The honest task-wide count is `summary.total_model_calls` from the count-only read `SQLiteTaskStore.count_task_model_calls`, and `taskModelCallLedgerNote` is the single place that turns "(carried rows, total count, page size)" into the header sentence: it states "任务开始以来共 N 次调用" only from the server count, adds "明细带最近 X 条" when the window is truncated, and falls back to "已收到 X 次调用明细" when no count is present. Never derive that claim from the row length. Paging inside that window is a local slice with no request; paging beyond it goes through the page-only route below, and widening the window itself goes through `get_task_snapshot(..., model_call_limit=N)` or the ledger refresh endpoint, never by removing the LIMIT. Each row is a `TaskModelCallRecord` (`main/monitoring/models.py`), which carries `call_index`, `node_id`, `created_at`, `call_kind`, prepared-request counts, the per-call usage delta, the per-call gauges `duration_ms` / `first_token_ms` / `thinking_tokens`, and `stream_incomplete` — the provider's own fact that no chunk of that response carried `finish_reason` — so the same record family also feeds the `task_progress` tool's `model_calls` field, and node-level attribution stays consistent between the web window and tool output.
- Two lanes write that ledger, and the window must show both or a task under message distribution reads as idle while its control turns are spending tokens. Ordinary node turns write through `TaskLogService.append_node_output` (`main/monitoring/log_service.py`), the only writer that also appends a node output entry and an actual-request artifact. Message-distribution control turns (`main/runtime/node_runner.py::_run_distribution_node`) call the provider directly and never enter that loop, so each attempt — including decision-repair retries — is billed by `TaskLogService.record_distribution_model_call`: it writes the row, merges the usage delta into the initiating node and the task, and produces no node output entry, no actual-request artifact, no unread mark, and no live `task.model.call` event, which means those rows reach the window with the task snapshot or 刷新 rather than while it is open. `call_kind` is what separates them: empty for ordinary node turns (rows written before the field exists read the same way), `message_distribution` for control turns. `call_index` is that node's own round number (`len(node.output) + 1`), never a task-global sequence, so it repeats across nodes constantly and across epochs for control turns; 序号 is display and search text only, and a consumer that needs a stable row must key on `(node_id, call_index)`.
- The topline and the per-model breakdown have separate sources: the topline reads the task record's own `token_usage`, while the breakdown is aggregated by `main/monitoring/query_service.py::_projection_token_usage_by_model` from every node-detail projection row and **delivered by the window's own narrow fetch, not by the task snapshot**. That aggregation was moved out deliberately: cold-page it scans the whole task's detail rows (measured 989 rows / 71 MB, 85% of it legacy inlined trace summaries) for **4447 ms**, while warm it costs 41–105 ms — so on the snapshot lane it only ever bit the first 点开详情 after a restart, and it is tracked as `query_service.get_task_token_ledger.token_rollup`, the lane that actually runs it. Until that fetch lands for the current task the model section reads 按模型明细正在取数; the ownership check is `S.taskTokenLedgerTaskId === S.currentTaskId`, so 尚无按模型明细 is only ever claimed when the server truly returned nothing for this task. That aggregation never loads the multi-MB detail columns: it narrow-reads each row with `json_extract(payload_json, '$.payload.token_usage_by_model')`, because a node's per-model list lives inside the detail record's own `payload` object (`main/monitoring/models.py::TaskProjectionNodeDetailRecord.payload`), not at the row's top level. A window stuck on 正在取数 therefore points at that fetch or that read path, not at the token-recording writer. Both readings convert to the display 口径 exactly once: `tokenDisplayUsage` swaps 总输入 for `effective_input_tokens` (input + cache hit), and `taskTokenSummaryLine` performs that same conversion itself, so callers hand it the unconverted usage. Handing it the already-converted object inflates the 按钮 tooltip by exactly the cache-hit total while the topline tiles stay right — a tooltip whose 总输入 is the 总输入 tile plus that tile's own 缓存命中 is a double conversion, not a bad ledger.
- 模型调用明细 puts 序号（the call sequence number）first and defaults to 时间（`created_at`）descending with `call_index` descending as the same-instant tiebreak; records without a timestamp sink last. The table additionally shows 节点ID so per-node token attribution can be confirmed without leaving the window, 类型 (`call_kind` rendered as 普通回合 / 分发消息, with the raw ledger token on hover) so a distribution attempt can be told apart from a work turn at the same node, and the three gauges 总耗时 / 首 Token 耗时 / 思考 Token so a slow call can be told apart from a call that merely sent a large context.
- Unreported readings render as `--`, never as a fabricated 0 (the same convention covers 思考 Token and both duration columns). A `stream_incomplete` row additionally dashes 新增输入 Token and 缓存命中, with the tooltip 上游未回终止分片，这一跳没有 usage 回执: an upstream stream closed before `finish_reason` never emits the usage chunk the request asked for (`stream_options.include_usage`), so those zeros mean "no receipt", not "this call was free" — the request body it sent is still estimated by the preflight path at tens of thousands of tokens.
- The gauges are per call, not per node: the 咽喉点 (`main/runtime/chat_backend.py`) times each provider request and fills `LLMModelAttempt.duration_ms`; the streaming providers fill `LLMResponse.first_token_ms` from their stream diagnostics (first received chunk, falling back to the first text delta); thinking tokens come from the provider's own `reasoning_tokens` usage field, extracted by `normalize_usage_payload`. `TaskLogService._model_call_attempt_metrics` then folds one call's attempts into the row: durations and thinking tokens sum across retries, while first-token latency takes the **last** request that reported it — a fast failed retry must not become the row's value. A gauge no provider reported stays `null` and renders `--`; it must never be coerced to `0`, which would assert the model did not think. Non-streaming requests have no chunks to measure, so their 首 Token 耗时 is `--`.
- The window is frozen while open: live events keep updating `S.recentModelCalls` in the background but do not rebuild the open window, so scroll position, the current filter and the search box content survive real-time patches. Opening the window and the 刷新 button beside the search box share one re-fetch entry: `setTaskTokenStatsOpen(true)` calls `refreshTaskTokenLedger({ quiet: true })` — the open-time fetch deliberately raises no 已刷新 toast, because nobody pressed 刷新 and the model table filling in is its feedback — while 刷新 stays the manual entry that reports 已刷新 or 刷新失败. The window therefore never depends on the task snapshot for the per-model breakdown, and its contract is to re-fetch rather than repaint: `refreshTaskTokenLedger` (`g3ku/web/frontend/org_graph_tasks.js`) calls `GET /api/tasks/{task_id}/token-ledger?limit=300` (`main/api/rest.py` → `main/service/runtime_service.py::get_task_token_ledger_payload` → `main/monitoring/query_service.py::get_task_token_ledger`, `limit` clamped to 1..1000), which returns the task record's `token_usage`, the `_projection_token_usage_by_model` breakdown, and the newest `limit` ledger rows oldest-first. Fetched rows merge into the in-memory list on the `(node_id, call_index)` identity above, and a payload that lands after the operator switched tasks is discarded. Refreshing deliberately does not re-request the task detail: that snapshot carries the whole unbounded ledger (measured on a live task — 8,071 rows / 6.79 MB / 6.5s), which is why the narrow route caps rows at all; the window therefore covers the snapshot taken when the task opened, plus the live lane, plus the last 300 rows per refresh. Both outcomes are visible — 刷新中 with the button disabled in flight, then a 已刷新 or 刷新失败 toast and an enabled 刷新 — because a repaint that changes nothing is indistinguishable from a dead control.
- Rows older than the carried window come from a page-only route: `GET /api/tasks/{task_id}/model-call-page?page=N&size=S&anchor=A` (`main/api/rest.py` → `main/service/runtime_service.py::get_task_model_call_page_payload` → `main/monitoring/query_service.py::get_task_model_call_page` → `SQLiteTaskStore.list_task_model_calls(offset=…, max_seq=…)`). `size` is clamped to 1..200 and `page` to that anchor's last page, so the route cannot be turned back into a whole-ledger read (a live task measured 49,758 rows / 123.9 MB). It returns one page oldest-first plus `total_calls` / `total_pages` / `anchor_seq`, and deliberately **not** `token_usage_by_model`: that aggregation reads node projections rather than this ledger and costs 80–160 ms per request on a live task, while the page itself is 0.5–3.5 ms of SQL on `idx_task_model_calls_task_id_seq` (deep offsets included — offset 49,458 measured 3.5 ms) plus ~2.6 ms to model 100 rows, i.e. a 76 KB response.
- The footer's page count is computed from `summary.total_model_calls`, so the extent of the whole ledger is visible without paying for it, and a request only happens when the clicked page leaves the loaded rows. Paging therefore has two states. Inside the window: rows come from `S.recentModelCalls`, live events keep merging, and search covers the window. On an anchored page (历史快照): `loadTaskModelCallPage` puts the rows in `S.taskModelCallPageRows` and never in `S.recentModelCalls` (that array has no length cap), `task.model.call` events stop merging, search covers that page only with the input labelled 搜索本页, 刷新 rebases the numbering onto the current ledger tail and reloads the same page, and 回到最新 leaves the state and re-runs the refresh route. `anchor` is the ledger tail `seq` captured when paging starts and is echoed back on every later page because the numbering would otherwise drift under incoming calls — 2.9 calls/minute on an active task moves a 100-row page roughly every 34 minutes, and the same ledger's lifetime average is 1.1 calls/minute. A page whose task changed is dropped, and opening another task exits the state.
- The search input to the right of the 模型调用明细 title filters by 序号 (call sequence number, substring match), node id, 类型 (either the rendered label or the raw `call_kind` token — the whole call-kind vocabulary is two values, so a dropdown would be a second filter to keep in sync for no gain), or the model cell's searchable text — the resolved config name plus the raw ledger identifiers (`model_key` / `provider_model` / `provider_id`), per「Backend Responsibilities」; the filter runs over every row that lane holds before pagination, which is the window in the live state and one page in 历史快照, then pagination paginates the filtered set. It is never a ledger-wide query: a substring match over the whole ledger measured 0.34–0.37 s warm and 30.3 s on cold pages, so the box stays client-side and the placeholder says what it covers. Search and pagination rebuild only the table region (`refreshTaskTokenCallTable`), leaving the search input element, its focus and its value intact.
- The modal is `min(1200px, 100vw - 32px)` wide and the thirteen-column table is compacted to fit it (0.78rem cells, 6px/7px padding) rather than being allowed to overflow: 节点ID and 模型 cells ellipsize in-cell, so the right-most columns are not pushed out of view. `.task-token-call-table-wrap` keeps horizontal scrolling as the narrow-viewport fallback.

### Task Message Distribution UI Contract（已删除）

> distribution epoch 删除后，`runtime_meta.distribution` 在现行代码里没有读写方，本节的横幅/冻结徽章/受影响子树染色/消息 received·frozen 投影全部失效并从代码中删除。保留作旧库数据的解释。现行节点消息状态见本节末尾三条（信箱三态）。

- Task-tree distribution UI is scope-driven: the frontend treats `runtime_summary.distribution.mode == "subtree_barrier"`（兼容旧持久化 meta 的 `task_wide_barrier` 名称）as the authoritative banner source instead of inferring state from the root node's pending-notice count alone. `target_node_ids` names the frozen subtree roots and `blocked_node_ids` carries exactly the frozen subtree union — the tree view banners and highlights only the frozen subtree: nodes listed there render `barrier_blocked`, and connector lines turn the amber distribution color only between affected nodes; nodes outside the subtree keep their ordinary status rendering and keep executing.
- While the epoch is in `barrier_requested`, `barrier_draining`, or `distributing`, the task-tree view shows a task-local sticky notice (not a global shell toast) reading 「新消息分发中 · 已停步 k/n · …」, where n 取 `blocked_node_ids`（应冻集）、k 取排空账本与它的**交集**（陈旧账本项不得把分子顶过分母）；n 为空时不编造分母，退回纯文本「新消息分发中」。账本的单一真源是 epoch 行 `payload.frozen_node_ids`：`main/monitoring/query_service.py` 的读数侧在 meta 副本为空且存在 `active_epoch_id` 时回查一次该 epoch（epoch 落成 `failed` 后驱动器不重算 meta、worker 重启也不重算，不回查就会长期显示 `0/n`），而三处 meta 全量写入（`_publish_distribution_meta`、每波决策落库处的刷新、`_task_distribution_state` 重建）都必须带上这一项——meta 的 `distribution` 是整包通道，漏写的键会被 `_sanitize_distribution_state` 直接吃掉。k 是**下界**而非精确进度（判读口径见 `main-task-runtime.md`「分发状态机与屏障」），尾句按 k<n / k=n 分别给「其余节点仍有在飞请求未落」与「已进入分发决策回合 / 全部已停，等待决策回合」。这一计数存在的理由：排空要等在飞回合落地，分发窗口可以达到分钟级，静态一句文案读不出"在推进"与"卡死"的差别。横幅同时 marks affected connectors: `distributionAffectedNodeIds` expands `blocked_node_ids` ∪ `target_node_ids` into full snapshot subtrees; a rendered item receives `execution-tree-item--distribution-affected` when its node is affected, and its child list receives `execution-tree-list--distribution-affected` when the parent node is affected. CSS colors a connector only when both endpoints are affected (list + item carry the classes), so the boundary edge between a frozen subtree root and its unaffected parent keeps its status color. When the epoch completes (target notices consumed/processed) the distribution state clears, the classes disappear on the next render, and connectors restore their status colors.
- A distribution `state == "failed"` renders the same sticky notice in a red failed variant (`task-tree-distribution-bubble--failed`) whose text carries `distribution.error_text` plus the intervention hint (re-append the notice to retry, or resume the task), and keeps the affected-subtree connector marking because the subtree stays frozen as the failure lever; an explicit resume downgrades the banner to the ordinary pending-notice state (`resume_ready`) and clears `blocked_node_ids`/`target_node_ids`. Failure semantics are owned by `main-task-runtime.md`「分发状态机与屏障」.
- Node detail receives a backend-owned message list (its own section before `派生记录`) rather than a pseudo turn, and the frontend must not reconstruct entries from raw mailbox tables or prompt tail blocks. The section has two tabs: **接收** (`node.message_list`，本节点收到的通知) and **发出** (`node.sent_message_list`，通知台账里 `source_node_id=本节点` 的行，带 `target_node_id`/`target_title`)。Both tabs filter system-generated「定向通知转述族」(【任务收到用户定向通知】/【后代节点收到用户定向通知】/【被检验节点收到用户定向通知】/【正在检验的节点接收了用户通知】, marked `payload.origin=system_relay` on ledger rows, or matched by message prefix for legacy rows) while the ledger rows themselves and the node context merge path stay untouched. Collapsed rows are one line, left to right: **状态图标 → 时间 → 节点 id 药丸 → 内容预览**（`taskMessagePreviewText`，预览靠 CSS 省略号；无「 · 」分隔，也不显示节点标题或「来自/发送至」文字）。节点 id 药丸去掉 `node:` 前缀、带方向图标（`arrow-down-left` / `arrow-up-right`），药丸内 id 前预置关系文字位：`node_relation=parent/child/sibling` → 父节点/子节点/同级节点；后端尚未下发该字段，当前统一显示中性占位「节点」。点击药丸直接打开该节点详情（`data-node-jump` → `jumpToTaskNodeDetail`）。`merged` 与 `consumed` 同显示「已消费」，状态图标统一为 `circle-check`。Expanded rows show **only the message body** — the old 状态/接收时间/消费时间/并入上下文时间/分发情况 fields were removed. 状态标签仍由 `messageStatusDescriptor` 统一映射，原始账本 token（`delivered` 等）不得外泄。消息列表与派生记录容器内的 `.task-trace-list` 各自在 **10.5 条**处封顶（`max-height: calc(10.5 * 48px + 10 * var(--space-3))`，内部滚动；滚动位置按该 list 捕获/还原）；`max-height` 保证不足 11 条时按内容自适应、不预占空白。
- **消息状态档位**：message-list 条目渲染 `status` 为 `已接收`(`received`) / `已冻结待释放`(`frozen`) / `待处理`(`pending`：已投递到本节点信箱、任何回合尚未处理) / `已消费`(`consumed`：控制或决策回合已处理，内容等待恢复路径并入或不并入) / `已并入上下文`(`merged`：内容已真正注入模型上下文)，另有旁路终态 `未下发`(`skipped`)。`messageStatusDescriptor` 是标签字面量的**唯一**出处，`messageListStatusDescriptor` 只做字段整形；任何一档都不得把原始账本 token（`delivered` 等）当标签外泄，未知值统一落 `待确认`。`consumed`/`merged` 的判定源仍是后端账本（`merged_at` 字段与归档记录），前端不做推断；发出侧把账本 `delivered` 归一成 `pending`、`consumed+merged_at` 归一成 `merged`，与接收侧同词表。
- **投递前的两档是视图层投影，不是账本**：`received` / `frozen` 由 `main/monitoring/query_service.py` 的 `project_pending_distribution_entries` 在 `_node_message_list` 末端算出，判定源是活跃 epoch 的 `scope_node_ids` 快照与排空账本 `frozen_node_ids`（合同见 `main-task-runtime.md`「子树级控制事务与排空账本」）。同一 epoch 上一旦出现该节点的真实 `task_node_notifications` 行，投影即让位，无需清理步骤。投影**不写**账本表、不进 `pending_notice_node_ids` / `pending_mailbox_count`、不参与 hold 谓词与释放集反算、不进 spawn 评审的 `consumed_distribution_notices`——接进这些路径会让展示物变成运行时依据。`received` 与 `pending` 不可合并：后者说"已投递"，前者说"已受理但还没投到我"，合并等于把投递前的空窗说成已投递。
- （消息行不带「请求中(已等 Ns)」后缀：在飞判据仍由树徽标使用，但消息列表的折叠摘要只保留时间/状态/预览三样，少一个会抖的尾巴。）
- （树视图不渲染 distribution 冻结徽章；`resolveTreeNodeStatusLabel` 的参数表里没有 `distributionFrozen`。）
（`barrier_blocked` 的更精确一档，仅非终态节点会被标注），`resolveTreeNodeStatusLabel` 据此把徽标显示成与详情条目同一串文案，避免同一个事实两种说法。
- **节点消息输入框**：节点详情「消息列表」标题右侧（`.agent-detail-section-header` 内与 h4 平级，h4 内容会被 `renderMessageHeading` 整体重写）常驻一个输入框+发送按钮（`syncNoticeComposerState` 控制可用性：验收节点/终态节点/已结束任务禁用并给出原因 placeholder）。提交走 `POST /api/tasks/{task_id}/nodes/{node_id}/notice`（body `{message}`，网页端不做会话归属校验，其余校验与工具一致：终态目标 400 `target_terminal`、被检验目标 400 `target_under_acceptance`），成功后刷新整树快照并强制刷新当前节点详情。分发是异步链路（`deliver_node_message(origin='user')` 落节点信箱），提交返回时新消息通常尚未进入节点详情：前端随后启动局部 settle 轮询（`settleTaskNoticeAfterSend`/`runTaskNoticeSettleTick`，间隔 2.5s、单条消息 90s 超时），轮询只重拉目标节点的 full 详情并局部重渲染消息列表，消息一经记账即自动出现；超时静默退出，由 WS `task.node.patch` 与手动刷新兜底。`resetTaskView` 清空轮询状态。
- Tree snapshots expose two visibility contracts that must not be collapsed: `parent_visible` / handshake fields are the distribution-oriented recipient projection, while browser rendering shows every node the snapshot carries. Execution nodes stay visible in every status, and an acceptance node stays in the tree for as long as its execution node has one — dispatched or not, terminal or not. Force-showing all nodes while distribution is active is not proof that they were all valid recipients.
- **未读徽标随 node.patch 实时更新**：`task.node.patch` 的 `node` 载荷现在带 `pending_notice_count`（`_node_pending_notice_badge_count`：待处理根通知 + 本节点 `delivered` 行，转述族不计）。消费/并入路径在 `_consume_inflight_notice_ids` 末尾主动 `publish_node_state`，所以消息并入上下文后节点行角标立即减一，不必等下一次整树快照；前端沿用 `normalizeTaskTreeSnapshotNode` 合并 + `treeNodeChipSignature`，签名变化即重绘。整树快照仍是权威读数。
- **派生记录是节点列表**：轮次展开后只列本轮的派生节点（`entries[].review_decision=allowed` 且有 `child_node_id`），每行 = 专注/全面 图标（`requested_specs[].execution_policy.mode`：`focus`→`target`、`coverage`→`layers`）+ 目标一行 + 节点 id 药丸。id 药丸与消息行同一组件（去 `node:` 前缀、关系文字位预置、可点击、`data-node-jump`），没有单独的「跳转到节点详情」按钮；点开节点显示原始提示词与验收结果（`direct_child_results[].check_result`；缺失显示「暂无验收结果」）。跳转先打开该节点详情抽屉，再后台 `locateTaskNode` 居中，不阻塞详情打开。被拦截候选不占节点列表。
- **提交输出（原「最终输出」）**：节点详情输出区标题为「提交输出」。执行节点调用 `submit_final_result` 进入等待验收时，`_maybe_start_acceptance_handshake` 会先 `update_node_output` 写入本次 `final_output` 并推 `task.node.patch`——验收还没跑完也能看到刚提交的内容；终态写回仍由 `_mark_finished` 负责。`update_node_output` 只改输出与 `updated_at`，不改 `status`/`finished_at`。提交输出与验收结果两个框均在 **15 行**处封顶（`max-height: calc(15 * 1.65em)`，内部滚动），右上角各有一个常驻复制按钮（`#ad-output-copy` / `#ad-check-copy`，复制框内全文；空态给失败提示）。
- **任务卡片节点数**：`GET /api/tasks` 的 `TaskListItem.node_count` 由 `count_task_nodes_by_task` 一次分组计数填充（`task_nodes` 投影行数），`task.summary.patch` 也带 `node_count`；卡片在「占用大小」旁渲染「节点数 N」，增量补丁走 `[data-task-node-count]` 局部更新。

### Task Recovery Notice UI Contract

- 「本任务遇到异常停止，已回退到稳定步骤继续。」（`task.metadata.recovery_notice`）以全局 toast 呈现：打开对应任务或该任务数据刷新时弹出一次，`kind=warn`、persistent（不自动消失），标题「任务自动恢复」。
- 全站只有一个 `#app-toast` 元素，所有提示（含任务恢复提示）共用它，因此外观合同只有一处：**上下定档、左右跟文案**。标题与正文排同一行（13px / 行高 1.5），卡片高度只由这一行里的 32px 关闭键加 4px 上下内缩决定（1280×690 实测 42px），`width: fit-content` 让横向等于文案实测宽（短提示 137–189px），`min(420px, 100vw - 48px)` 只是长文案的折行上限而不是固定宽（30 字左右的恢复提示因此折成 3 行、68.5px）。圆角固定 16px（与 `--ui-radius-control` 同档），不随盒子变小收档。关闭键收档：`.icon-btn` 全局是 36px 见方，比这行文字高，会把卡片顶回一行装不下的尺寸。文案下方没有进度条（persistent 与非 persistent 一视同仁），垂直位置顶部 20px + safe-area。视口的居中基准归「Navigation Bar And Compact Mode」的 fixed 层规则。同一视口里的「正在打开任务」进度条不是可关闭面，按自身宽度渲染，不跟这条合同走。
- 用户可以点击关闭：点击 toast 任意位置（含右上角关闭按钮）即关闭，并把该任务的关闭记录写入浏览器 `localStorage`（键 `g3ku.taskRecoveryNotice.dismissed.v1`，内容为 任务 id → 提示文本），前端在首次评估提示时一次性读入 `S.taskRecoveryNoticeDismissals` 后按内存值判重——整树每次渲染都会重新评估该提示，只靠内存集合会在刷新页面或重启 Web 后把已关闭的提示再弹一次。同一任务不重复弹出；切换到其他带提示的任务仍会弹出自己的提示；提示文本变化（含后端换文案）使记录失配，该任务会再提示一次。
- 该记录只存在于浏览器侧，不是后端状态：任务元数据里的 `recovery_notice` 不会因关闭而被清除，换浏览器或换设备会重新提示一次，也不需要任何服务端清理入口。
- 若 toast 在用户关闭前被其他提示覆盖，下一次任务树渲染会重新弹出该提示（显示状态按“当前显示的提示文本”去重，而不是按“显示过”）。
- 提示是否出现由后端元数据决定：只有非优雅中断后的恢复清洗写 `recovery_notice`；优雅暂停 + 自动恢复不产生该提示。生命周期语义见 `main-task-runtime.md`「Graceful Shutdown Pause and Startup Auto-Resume」。

### Task Depth Default Contract

- The task-hall "global task tree depth" control is a global main-runtime default, backed by `PUT /api/main-runtime/settings`.
- New CEO/web sessions inherit that global default lazily. The runtime must not freeze the current global depth into ordinary session metadata just because a session was created, listed, or reopened.
- A CEO session only overrides the global task depth when the session has an explicit session-scoped override saved through `PATCH /api/ceo/sessions/{session_id}/task-defaults`.
- That explicit override is persisted as session-owned metadata and remains authoritative for later `create_async_task` calls from that session until changed again.
- Legacy session records that contain `task_defaults` without an explicit session-override marker must be treated as inherited/global, not as an override. Maintainers debugging "I changed global depth but new tasks still use an old value" should check for this distinction first.
- The practical rule is:
  - global task-hall updates should affect later new tasks immediately;
  - explicit session overrides may intentionally diverge from the global default;
  - unscoped legacy `task_defaults` must not pin later task creation to stale values.

### Heartbeat Visible-Turn Contract

- Browser-side CEO websocket payloads may carry both `inflight_turn` and `preserved_turn`.
- `inflight_turn` is the current real running turn. For heartbeat this means the heartbeat turn itself, not the earlier user bubble that is being kept on screen temporarily.
- `preserved_turn` is a live-only carryover bubble that should remain visible until a later `ceo.turn.discard` closes it.
- `preserved_turn` only exists for an older bubble that has not yet been superseded by a persisted assistant transcript entry with the same `turn_id`. Once that assistant turn is durable history, the backend/frontend should stop surfacing the preserved copy.
- Frontend rendering should treat these as two separate bubbles. It must not reuse `preserved_turn.turns` as the `Interaction Flow` for the current heartbeat bubble.
- Frontend trace fallback is only safe within the same rendered turn identity. Reusing the previous bubble's trace across `turn_id` or across `source=user -> heartbeat` is a contract bug.

### CEO Session List Interaction Contract

- The session list distinguishes "session switch is still settling" (`ceoSessionBusy`, a session-view readiness flag, not a general catalog lock) from "session catalog is being mutated" (`ceoSessionCatalogBusy`, covering create / rename / delete / bulk-delete checks).
- Catalog items always carry the shape of their own session family: `china:*` / `ext:*` keys are built by the channel-shaped builder (`session_family=channel`, `channel_id`, readonly/`can_message` flags) in both the REST catalog and global `ceo.sessions.snapshot` / `ceo.sessions.patch` pushes; only `web:*` keys go through the local-shaped builder with the generic summary fallback. Emitting a local-shaped item for a channel key makes the browser insert that channel session into the local web-session list momentarily.
- Local catalog items carry two separate run-state axes: `is_running` (derived: the in-flight flag OR `status == running`) and `status` (the runtime gear verbatim — `idle` / `running` / `paused` / `completed` / `error`; empty when no runtime session exists for that key). `is_running` on its own cannot tell "waiting for approval" from "this turn died", which is why both ship. Both come from resolvers injected into the catalog builders over the runtime manager, so a new catalog read path passes `is_running_resolver` and `status_resolver` together.
- The sidebar reads those two axes as three ring states on `.ceo-session-card::after`: green and breathing while the turn is live; amber and static when the session stopped in a way that needs a human (an unanswered approval interrupt, or `status=error`); nothing otherwise. `status=paused` with no interrupt — a manual pause or the shutdown pause — is not an anomaly and gets no ring. Breathing means "still running" everywhere on this surface, so the attention ring must never animate. The ring is card-level in the expanded panel and moves onto `.ceo-session-glyph::after` on the collapsed rail, where a card-level outline would box two glyph tiles into one ring. Colors come from the `--ceo-session-running-ring` / `--ceo-session-attention-ring` token pair (one value per light/dark block); the amber is the model-retry notice family, so "yellow" keeps exactly one meaning in the sidebar. The accessible name of the card carries the same distinction (`（运行中）` / `（等待审批）` / `（异常停止）`), which is the only channel for it on the collapsed rail.
- The persisted active session id survives page refresh whenever it names any valid session: a `web:` key with a transcript/artifact, a parseable `china:` key with a transcript, or an `ext:` key that has a registry entry or a transcript. Only unknown ids fall back to the most recent local session (and write that fallback back to the state store), so resolving an active `ext:` channel session as "invalid" is a contract bug.
- The browser fetches the REST session list only on page load; live catalog updates arrive as global `ceo.sessions.snapshot` websocket pushes. The `/ws/ceo` open sequence therefore sends `hello`, the session catalog, and `ceo.state` BEFORE `snapshot.ceo`: the transcript snapshot can be tens of megabytes on long channel sessions (canonical-context payloads), and the sidebar must never be queued behind it. Frontend envelope handling is order-independent, and any reconnect re-delivers the current catalog first.
- Bulk session delete-check and delete execution are backend-owned batch contracts (`POST /api/ceo/sessions/delete-check` and `POST /api/ceo/sessions/bulk-delete`, both with `session_ids` plus one shared `delete_task_records` flag). Each returns the per-session `results[]` and the refreshed session catalog (`items`, `channel_groups`, `active_session_id`) in one payload. The frontend does not loop per-session calls for a bulk action.
- `can_delete` 只回答"有没有绑定任务"，不回答"现在有没有人在用"。删除前的活性判据要自己查：该会话在 `.g3ku/web-ceo-requests/<session>/` 里最近 N 分钟有没有新请求、`is_running` 是否为真。`message_count=0` 不是安全信号——会话的第一回合在转录落盘前一直是 0 条，而它可能正在跑。删掉的会话其 transcript、请求 artifact 与 continuity/inflight 侧车一起消失，且前门请求不进 `artifacts` 表（那张表按 `task_id`/`node_id` 建行），所以事后不可恢复。
- During `ceoSessionBusy` alone, the left rail still allows `new session` and bulk-selection entry so operators are not trapped in a fully disabled sidebar, though composer send/pause may stay blocked; destructive or catalog-writing actions key off the stricter mutation-safe state.
- An expanded session card carries the title and the preview only. Session id and creation time are not card fields: they render as two rows at the top of the `⋯` menu, before 命名 and 删除 (`.ceo-session-menu-info`), because they cost two lines of vertical space and one line of preview width each. The id row is also where the session key gets copied (`.ceo-session-menu-copy`, `data-session-copy-id`, delegated on `#ceo-session-list`): the click flashes the button through `flashTraceCopyButton` and leaves the menu open, `copyTextToClipboard` does not swallow a rejected clipboard so the handler has to catch it or a denial ends with no feedback at all, and copying is not a catalog mutation so the button stays outside the `syncCeoSessionActions` disabled set. Within such a row the field name is the fixed part and the value is the flexible one (`.ceo-session-menu-info-row > span:first-child`); without that split a long `ext:` key squeezes the label into vertical text. The panel keeps its horizontal budget for text instead of chrome — 10px panel padding, 6px list right gutter, 12px card padding, and only the title row reserves 32px for the absolutely positioned `⋯` — so the preview line is as wide as the card while the menu button never overlaps text.
- Bulk selection exists only in the expanded panel: collapsing the rail clears `ceoBulkMode` plus the selection and re-renders the list, so no `.ceo-session-checkbox` node survives on the collapsed rail where cards render as glyph-only tiles. In bulk mode the checkbox owns the card's top-left corner and the unread badge relocates to the card's top-right corner (the per-card actions menu is hidden there). Any rule that hides expanded-only chrome while collapsed must cover the checkbox as well as `.ceo-session-actions`.
- Local session cards are drag-reorderable in both panel states, on the model-chain lane: `dragstart` / `dragover` / `drop` / `dragend` are delegated on `#ceo-session-list`, cards carry `draggable` plus `data-ceo-session-index`, and the insertion point is the vertical midpoint of the card under the pointer. A drop writes the whole visible id sequence to `S.ceoSessionOrder` and `g3ku.ui.ceo.session-order.v1`; `sortCeoSessionsByTime` ranks manual position first and only then `created_at` desc, so an unranked session (created after the last drag) surfaces above the pinned block. Because the array is otherwise sorted only on ingest, a drop re-sorts `S.ceoLocalSessions` itself before re-rendering. There is no server-side order field: the REST catalog still arrives in server order and only this browser remembers the manual sequence. Drag is disabled on the channel tab (items are grouped by channel type and have no flat index) and in bulk-select mode, where the card is a checkbox target.

- Channel-tab groups fold: the group title is a `<button class="ceo-session-group-title" data-channel-collapse="<channel_id>">` carrying `aria-expanded`, and the folded `<section>` gets `is-collapsed` while `.ceo-session-group-list` is not rendered at all — a folded group leaves no clickable-but-invisible card node behind. The caret is lucide `chevron-down` rotated to -90deg by `.is-collapsed .ceo-session-group-caret svg`; the wrapper keeps a fixed 14px box because the icon only becomes an `<svg>` on the rAF icon pass, and without reserved width the label shifts one step on every re-render.
- Folding is one state read by both panel states, not a per-panel feature. In the expanded panel the folded group is the title row above. On the collapsed rail (71px list) the group's title row is hidden chrome, so a folded group instead renders `.ceo-session-channel-tile`: one glyph tile wearing the same 36px box as the session tiles it replaces, carrying the glyph of its first hidden card, with the group's new-message count at its top-right (a session card's rail badge sits top-LEFT, and that side swap is the only thing distinguishing 组 from 单会话 — the full channel name lives in `title` / `aria-label`). Clicking it runs the same `toggleCeoChannelCollapse`, so folding and unfolding work from either panel state. The two affordances are mutually exclusive by `[data-panel-state]`: `display:none` on the tile in the expanded panel, on the title in the collapsed one — a rule that hides expanded-only chrome while collapsed must cover the title as well as `.ceo-session-actions` and the bulk checkbox.
- Fold state is per-`channel_id` browser state on the same lane as the manual session order: `S.ceoChannelCollapsed` ⇄ `g3ku.ui.ceo.channel-collapse.v1` through `readStoredUiPreference`, everything expanded by default, no server field. It is a different mechanism from the backend `hidden` registry flag that removes a channel row for good（见「Channel Session Clear Contract」）: folding never touches the catalog, so the folded rows keep receiving `ceo.sessions.snapshot` / `ceo.sessions.patch` updates.
- A folded group reports new content with one integer on its title (`.ceo-session-group-unread`, `99+` capped, shown only while folded and only above zero). It sums the same `sessionUnreadCount` the card badges read and inherits the card's exclusion — the active session contributes 0 — so a group total and the badges under it can never disagree. Nothing auto-expands: catalog refreshes and patches recompute the number and leave `is-collapsed` alone.
- Folding and bulk selection share one rule — 看不见的行不参与批量: `visibleCeoBulkSelectableSessionIds()` skips folded groups, and folding a group drops its sessions from `S.ceoSelectedSessionIds` (expanding does not restore those checks). Otherwise 批量删除 would count sessions the operator cannot see.

If an operator reports "switching sessions makes the whole Leader sidebar unusable", inspect these frontend flags separately before changing button rules:

1. `ceoSessionBusy`
2. `ceoSessionCatalogBusy`
3. `ceoPauseBusy`
4. `ceoUploadBusy`

Do not treat `ceoSessionBusy` as equivalent to "all session-list mutations must be locked". That coupling is a UX regression for the Leader session rail.

### Channel Session Clear Contract

- Deletion refuses before touching anything while a turn of that session is still running: `_session_is_running` gates the very first step of `_delete_single_ceo_session` and answers `409 ceo_turn_in_progress` (same code the edit lane uses, so the rail already renders it as 「回合进行中，请等待结束或先暂停后再操作」). The reason is order, not policy: the transcript, sidecars and uploads are removed first and `cancel_session_tasks` runs after them, so an in-flight turn would write its own row back under a session key that was just deleted and the card would reappear. `bulk-delete` keeps its per-item shape — that session is reported `result: failed / error: ceo_turn_in_progress` and the rest of the batch still deletes.
- In the CEO session rail, deleting a local session removes the session record. Deleting a channel session has two outcomes, chosen by whether that bridge still has an entry in `externalApi.tokens`: entry present → the card is **hidden**; entry gone → the session is **deleted outright**, identity included. Both outcomes wipe the same content, so the split is only about the external identity.
- Both outcomes remove the persisted `SessionManager` transcript for that channel session key (`ext:*`, or a legacy `china:*` archive), invalidate the in-memory cached session object, and clear the same side artifacts a local deletion clears: inflight snapshots, paused execution context, completed continuity sidecars, uploads, and frontdoor stage-archive artifacts.
- Hidden keeps the registry entry and marks it `hidden` (persisted in `.g3ku/external-sessions/registry.json`). The catalog and the `ceo.sessions.patch` lane both drop marked rows, so the card leaves the rail and the group collapses when it becomes empty. Two lanes clear the mark and then push a fresh `ceo.sessions.snapshot` (a browser only refetches the REST list on page load, so returning rows must be pushed, not waited for): re-registration through `resolve_or_create`, and any inbound turn handed to `ExternalTurnService.submit`. The second one is load-bearing — a gateway that caches `conversation → session_key` inside its own process never re-registers, so a registration-only rule leaves that session answering from an invisible row. Outbound traffic does not clear the mark: the drain and the 60s reconciler re-publish unacked records for exactly these rows, and keying off the outbound side would resurrect the whole hidden batch within a minute. The event hub is not retired: the identity is still in use, and replay semantics must not change.
- Deleting outright is the same teardown an explicit `forget_identity: true` request performs: the registry mapping is dropped and the session's in-memory event hub is retired with it. Session keys are a deterministic digest of `(bridge_id, external_key)`, so a re-registered key lands on the same paths; a surviving buffer would let `Last-Event-ID: 0` replay the previous life's replies into the new one.
- `forget_identity: true` on `DELETE /api/ceo/sessions/{session_id}` (and `bulk-delete`) always wins over the token split and retires the identity. It refuses before touching anything when something still points at the session key: `400 outbox_pending:<n>` when the outbound ledger holds unacked records for it, `400 scheduled_target:<job_id>` when an enabled cron job delivers to it (matched by `payload.session_key` or by `payload.to == external_key`). Every gate is evaluated before any destruction, so a refused request leaves the session exactly as it was — not hidden, not half-cleared. Without that gate a re-registered key would silently inherit routing that `find_by_any_key` only warns about.
- The rail never sends `forget_identity`; it relies on the token split. When the split itself decides to retire (no token entry), the `outbox_pending` gate is deliberately not consulted and `scheduled_target` still is. A token-less bridge has no consumer that could ever ack those records, so keeping the gate would pin the session as permanently undeletable; once the registry entry is gone, the routability judgment in `external-agent-api.md`「持久 outbox」collects those records as `unreachable` and logs each one.
- `china:*` archives and registry-less `ext:*` rows have no entry to mark, so their deletion is always the outright outcome.
- Result rows distinguish the lanes: `deleted=true` for local removals, `cleared=true` for every channel deletion, plus `identity_hidden` / `identity_forgotten` saying which channel lane ran. Batch-delete accepts mixed local and channel selections in one request even though the refreshed catalog arrives as one post-mutation snapshot.
- The rail renders 删除 on channel cards (命名 stays hidden — `can_rename=false`), and both the single and batch confirm dialogs branch on `hides_on_delete`: the hide lane reads as 隐藏 with "对方再发消息会回来", the retire lane as 删除 and promises only the content removal. The success toast reports `identity_forgotten` because that is only knowable after the request.

If an operator reports “the channel conversation was deleted but old context came back,” inspect these layers in order:

1. `DELETE /api/ceo/sessions/{session_id}` response payload for `cleared=true` plus `identity_hidden` / `identity_forgotten`
2. `.g3ku/external-sessions/registry.json` for that session key: entry missing → retired; entry with `hidden: true` → hidden, and any inbound message from that `external_key` clears it
3. persisted channel transcript files (`sessions/ext_*.jsonl`, legacy `sessions/china_*.jsonl`) and in-memory `SessionManager` cache
4. inflight / paused CEO session artifacts
5. frontend snapshot cache only after the backend-owned state is confirmed cleared

### Heartbeat/Cron Visibility Versus Prompt Inheritance

- Browser-side CEO timeline rendering and inflight bubbles are allowed to show heartbeat / cron work as ordinary active turns.
- The same heartbeat / cron round is also durable prompt history, but visibility is split in two: prompt inheritance uses `prompt_visible`, while browser transcript/snapshot rendering uses `ui_visible`.
- Maintainers should not assume "frontend cannot see the hidden rule/event bundle" means "the model cannot see it later". The hidden rule/event messages are intentionally persisted for later prompt reuse while remaining absent from UI transcript surfaces.

The current rule is:

- UI may show heartbeat / cron turns, tool calls, execution trace, compression state, and visible assistant replies directly.
- The hidden heartbeat / cron rule and event-bundle messages must stay out of transcript lists, session preview text, session message counts, and `snapshot.ceo.messages` by way of `ui_visible=false`.
- Later turns inherit heartbeat / cron work from the authoritative continuity baseline plus prompt-visible history, not from a separate semantic-summary-only recovery lane.

## Actual Request Debugging Contract

See `context-and-cache-troubleshooting.md`「Prompt Cache Family 与 Actual Request」 for request-artifact forensics: the split between projected input and the provider-facing request, the `actual_request_ref` / `actual_request_hash` / `actual_tool_schema_hash` fields, cache-family comparison, and runtime- vs provider-side tool-name accounting.

Web-specific artifact locations kept here:

- Every CEO/frontdoor `call_model` round writes the full provider-facing request to `.g3ku/web-ceo-requests/<session>/...json`, including internal lanes such as `token_compression`.
- Inflight / paused CEO snapshots expose only the latest `actual_request_path`, hash/count fields, and a short `actual_request_history`.
- Reopened completed sessions restore baseline state from a compact sidecar at `.g3ku/web-ceo-continuity/<session>.json`. The sidecar also carries `frontdoor_selection_debug`, the only durable landing for the per-hop `tool_contract_presence` reading and `staged_swept_tool_ids` on CEO-only turns (those produce no task/node frames; see `tool-hydration-and-callable-chain.md`).

## Verification Pointers

Use these focused checks when validating shell and frontend behavior:

- `python -m pytest tests/resources/test_bootstrap_runtime_status.py -v`
- `node --test tests/resources/*.test.js` — browser modules are evaluated in a vm stub harness, so code that touches `document` or `window` APIs the stubs do not provide fails here first.

## CEO Compression UI Contract

Context compression has one operator-facing surface: a rule inside the CEO feed that separates folded history from everything after it. It covers both the automatic send-time compression and the operator-triggered manual compression. See `runtime-overview.md`「Frontdoor Context Compression」 for the runtime behavior; this section covers only what the operator sees and clicks.

- `compression_state` only means inline frontdoor `token_compression` progress. The frontend treats `status === "running"` as "context is being compressed right now" and infers no durable semantic-summary state from it.
- A running compression renders `上下文压缩中`, a finished one `会话已压缩`, an interrupted one `压缩已暂停`. All three are the same `.ceo-compression-divider` row and use the `#39c5bb` accent (`--ceo-context-compress-color`). The row is a text label between two thick hairlines that stop short of the feed edges; both sides take the same flex weight so the label is centered no matter how wide the feed or the session panel is. The running and paused states draw their own icon (a rotating ring with two vertical bars in the middle, sized in `em` off the label's font size) instead of a lucide glyph — a rotationally symmetric circle would spin invisibly. That ring is deliberately exempt from the stylesheet's global `prefers-reduced-motion` animation freeze: here the motion is the only "compression is still alive" signal, and operators on machines with animations switched off read a frozen ring as a stalled compression. The icon takes layout width, so the left hairline carries an equal transparent gap to keep the label itself on the center line. There is no separate composer toast — the feed rule replaced it.
- The running rule is live-only: it is appended while a compression is in flight and leaves the feed when the compression ends — a manual terminal poll converts it in place to the terminal rule first, and the durable transcript row replaces it on the subsequent rebuild. Only the manual lane's running rule carries the pause affordance — there the ring-and-bars icon is a button that opens the shared in-page confirm dialog (`openConfirm`), and confirming calls `POST /api/ceo/sessions/{session_id}/compress-context/cancel`; cancelling the dialog changes nothing. That endpoint answers only for the manual task (it returns `compression_not_running` for anything else), so an in-turn compression draws the same rule with the icon as a static element: stopping that compression is the composer's primary pause action, which cancels the same generation server-side.
- `compression_state.status = "running"` may only be held by a live host: the session is running, or this process' manual compression task is running. The check sits at the two write points (`_sync_runtime_session_frontdoor_state` and the continuity restore), never at the readers, because a paused turn's snapshot legitimately records what was happening when it stopped. Without it the abandoned round keeps re-applying the `running` copy it carried in the graph state, the continuity file stores that lie, and the feed shows a 「上下文压缩中」 nobody will ever clear — the same stale flag also refuses the long-press, since `ceoBrainHoldBlockedReason()` reads the very predicate the divider uses.
- A paused rule is the one terminal rule with an action, and only while it is the last divider in the feed: it then reads `压缩已暂停 · 重新压缩`, and the button calls the same `beginCeoContextCompression()` the two-second brain hold ends in, behind the same refusal (`附件上传中` / `正在压缩上下文`). It deliberately has no confirm dialog: any running round appends its own bubble after this row, so a clickable paused row is structurally a session with nothing in flight, and reading history puts the row off-screen. An older paused rule, and every completed rule, renders as text plus the static bars glyph and is not clickable. The retry button's advance width is fixed in `em`, and the left hairline's compensation gap matches it, so the label stays on the center line in both the button and the non-button variant.
- Terminal states are durable: compression completion and interruption each append one `role="system"` transcript row carrying `metadata.kind = "context_compression"` plus `compression_state` (`completed` | `paused`) and `source` (`manual` | `auto`). `prompt_visible: False` keeps it out of the model's context and `ui_visible: True` keeps the snapshot emitting it, so a reopened session still shows where each compression happened. Automatic (send-time) compression writes the same row at transcript finalize, keyed to the turn that compressed, so one compression yields exactly one rule; the row's `metadata.stats` names that turn (`turn_id`) and records `boundary_turn_seq` — the `frontdoor_turns` length at the moment the summary landed, so the browser can split the turn rail at a turn boundary (`runtime-overview.md`「Frontdoor Context Compression」). A `context_compression_marker` session event carrying the same stats is what puts the rule on screen in the live lane, relayed by the CEO websocket as `ceo.compression.marker`. It is emitted the moment the summary lands, and that same moment appends the durable row into the in-memory transcript — no dedicated save (the full transcript rewrite still rides the turn finalize or the next session save), but the snapshot is built from the in-memory transcript, so a mid-turn refresh already sees the row and the rule survives reload; the finalize persists it with that same save and skips a second append behind a turn flag. A process that dies before any later save loses the rule on reload, which is correct: the row is the source of truth and the frame only draws it early.
- Placement is decided by the rule's boundary and the turn seqs it belongs to, never by where the transcript row happens to sit. The rebuild renderer builds a compression plan from the **full** message list; a pagination window only chooses which rows paint, and a marker row consumed by an assistant row is never painted at its row position. Completed rules attach to the last assistant row of their turn; paused rules and completed markers without a `turn_id` (out-of-turn manual compression) keep their row position. For a completed rule with a numeric boundary, one shared resolver returns one of three modes:
  - `before-turn`: `boundary == 0`, or no turn has `seq <= boundary`. The rule sits before the turn's first bubble/card, so the whole turn stays below the line.
  - `split`: some but not all known turns are at or below the boundary. The head keeps `seq <= boundary` and its `data-ceo-key` carries `#pre-<boundary>`; the rule goes between the two halves; the continuation takes the remaining turns plus streamed text, usage, thinking and fork action. A mid-run user message inserted before the current bubble therefore lands below the line.
  - `after-turn`: every known turn is at or below the boundary. The rule sits after the turn's rendered row. When a finished turn has no rail data, `after-turn` is the convention (the turn end is the compression point), not a rail-derived fact; a still-running turn with no rail yet splits instead, because the groups after the boundary have not arrived.
  Multiple completed rules in one turn are applied in ascending boundary order, and each keeps its own `(turn, state, boundary)` identity, so two compressions yield two lines at their own boundaries. The boundary is always a turn seq, so it falls between two groups and no group is cut in half. `boundary_turn_seq = 0` is a valid landing spot but is not written to `data-ceo-compression-boundary`; `#pre-<boundary>` belongs on the head's turn key, never on a message key, and `findCeoCompressionAnchor()` matches `turn:<id>` / `m:<id>:` prefixes only (newest match wins). A marker without a matching assistant row keeps its row position during rebuild; the live lane falls back to the feed tail only when it cannot find an anchor, and only that tail case scrolls, because a rule inserted into history must not yank a reader back down.
- The rule must split even when the turn rail has not arrived yet, or when the marker frame lands before the first turn patch (the local bubble still has no `turn_id`). Leaving the rule at the feed tail or after the bubble would render everything the turn produced afterwards — including the stages that were *not* folded — above the line. The contract:

  - `ceoLiveTurnForCompressionMarker(turnId)` matches the live turn by id; when no turn carries that id yet it claims the single still-anonymous bubble (no `turn_id`, not finalized) instead of giving up. The match must re-check the id itself: `getActiveCeoTurn(null, turnId)` degrades to "any active turn" when the id does not match, which would split the wrong bubble.
  - The split is taken with `turnWindowFrom`/`turnWindowTo` left open on both halves, and the head is remembered on the continuation as `compressionHead`. Nothing is filtered out at that moment, so a group that only arrives later still appears — dropping it would make content vanish.
  - `reconcileDeferredCeoCompressionSplit(turn)` runs right after every live turn-rail render: once the rail exists it sets `head.turnWindowTo = boundary` and `turn.turnWindowFrom = boundary + 1`, then re-renders both halves. From that point the head is frozen and everything new lands below the line.

  Without the deferred reconciliation the two halves stay mixed: folded groups and post-compression groups share one bubble, so the operator cannot tell which content is already summarized history and which is new.
- The frontend learns a manual compression's terminal state by polling `GET /api/ceo/sessions/{session_id}/compress-context` until `status` leaves `running`, then reopens the session websocket so the persisted rule arrives with the transcript. A single failed poll must not end that tracking — the server can hold the loop busy for seconds while it rewrites a large transcript, and abandoning the poll would leave the running rule on screen until a full reload. Polling stops only after `CEO_COMPRESSION_POLL_FAIL_LIMIT` consecutive failures, and it re-arms when the user switches back to the still-compressing session.
- During a manual compression the browser owns the visible running state, because an out-of-turn compression has no inflight turn to hang it on. That state is *mirrored*, not invented: the server publishes it as a session-level `compression` object (`source = "manual_context_compression"`) on **both** `ceo.state` and `snapshot.ceo` (the latter comes from `_build_live_turn_payload`), and `adoptCeoContextCompression(sessionId, compression)` re-claims it into local tracking — the running rule reappears and polling re-arms. Recovery must not depend on which of the two messages arrives first, and it must not rely on the local `S.ceoContextCompression*` surviving, because a page reload wipes exactly that. Automatic compression must not go through that manual adoption path either — it has no poll and no local initiation record. Its running state is read from two carriers on the snapshot the frame writes into `S.ceoSnapshotCache`: `inflight_turn.compression` (the turn-bound copy) and the session-level `compression` object that `_build_live_turn_payload` publishes beside it. The session-level copy is the one that keeps internal rounds visible: a heartbeat or cron round has no assistant bubble yet when the send preflight compresses, and `patchCeoInflightTurn()` drops such a frame wholesale (`ceoNeedsAssistantTurn()` refuses a running frame whose source is `heartbeat`), so the turn-bound copy never reaches the cache. Because that cache merges, `ceo.turn.patch` overwrites the `compression` key on every frame and treats a missing key as "nothing is compressing"; without that explicit overwrite the last running frame would keep the rule on screen after the compression finished. The mirrored running object carries `seen_at`; `ceo.state`, every poll response and every `ceo.turn.patch` that reports running refresh it, and `activeCeoSessionCompressionState()` rejects a cached running object older than `CEO_COMPRESSION_STALE_MS` (ten minutes) unless a live frame refreshes it. A terminal poll writes `null` to the mirror. It also converts the visible running line in place to `会话已压缩` / `压缩已暂停` before reopening the websocket; `not_needed` / `failed` remove it. The durable transcript row then replaces the provisional line during the rebuild, so the operator does not see a blank interval between the two. The mirrored source stays `manual_context_compression` for the whole manual summariser run, including its chunk and retry snapshots; the automatic lane keeps `token_compression`. That distinction is what lets a reload re-adopt the manual task and re-arm polling instead of treating it as an automatic compression with no terminal poll.
- The running rule is a DOM node, not transcript data, so `resetCeoFeed()` deletes it on every full rebuild (`innerHTML = ""`). `renderCeoSnapshot` therefore re-asserts it via `syncCeoCompressionDivider()` *after* the rebuild but *before* `applyCeoFeedViewState()` — dropping that call re-creates the "refresh mid-compression and the indicator vanishes" bug, because `snapshot.ceo` always arrives after the `ceo.state` that first drew the line. `renderCeoSessionLoadingState()` performs the same re-assert after its reset; a cold session with no cached snapshot otherwise shows a blank feed until the snapshot arrives. The re-assert is deferred to the outer edge of a feed batch: cache writes that happen while history rows finalize inside a rebuild queue it, so the line is appended after the full rebuilt feed rather than after the first row. The re-assert must not scroll when `S.ceoFeedFollowLatest === false`, or a user reading history gets yanked to the bottom on every rebuild.
- The compression task belongs to the runtime session, not to the connection: a websocket disconnect cancels only that connection's sender/stream tasks, so refreshing mid-compression does not stop it, and its terminal rule still lands in the transcript.
- Pausing the turn during an automatic compression still goes through the primary send/pause button and sends `client.pause_turn`; the backend cancels that compression and discards any late result.

### Manual Context Compression

- Pressing and holding the context-usage brain is a two-stage gesture: the first 200ms is a silent settle window (`CEO_BRAIN_HOLD_ARM_MS`) where nothing is drawn, so an ordinary click — or a hand tremor — never flashes progress it then cancels. After that window the progress travels the button's own edge: an SVG stroke around the control's rounded rect, started at the top and run clockwise, with `pathLength` normalizing the perimeter so `--ceo-brain-hold` 0→1 reads as a percentage of edge length rather than of angle. The browser calls `POST /api/ceo/sessions/{session_id}/compress-context` once a further `CEO_BRAIN_LONG_PRESS_MS` (2 seconds) has elapsed. Releasing early resets the bar and starts nothing; the click that follows a completed hold is swallowed so it cannot also open the model panel. Touch pointers never arm the hold. The bar's corner radius is the control tier's (`--ui-radius-control`), so the stroke stays on the button's border at every skin size.
- Hovering the brain shows `长按压缩上下文` below the icon and nothing else: hover must not brighten the icon's glow or lift it — that feedback belongs to the open panel only, and the hint chip is neutral rather than accent-tinted. This is the only hover text on the brain; usage figures still appear only inside the panel.
- The hold is refused, with a toast explaining why, only while an attachment upload is in flight or a compression already runs. Channel (`ext:` / `china:`) sessions are compressible too: the feed is read-only there, but a long channel history presses the same context window, and the durable baseline, continuity snapshot and actual-request artifact all already cover those keys. A channel key with no transcript is `session_not_found` rather than a silently created session, and the compression endpoints resolve channel runtime sessions without loading that (potentially tens-of-MB) transcript into the request handler.
- The endpoint never waits on the session: it records `running`, emits one state snapshot and returns. The detached task first pauses (`session.pause(manual=True)` — cancels in-flight requests) only when the session was running, then compresses. Keeping the pause out of the request body is a hard requirement, not an optimization: pausing and the terminal rule write both rewrite the whole transcript, and on the multi-tens-of-MB channel transcripts that exceeds the browser's request timeout, which would make the UI give up on a compression that later succeeds.
- A hold that finds nothing to compress (no compressible history, or a model without a usable context window) ends with `status: "not_needed"` and writes no transcript rule; the browser then says so in a toast instead of letting the running rule silently disappear. The same applies to `failed` (e.g. the session could not be paused), which carries the backend `reason`.

### Context Window Error UX

- If the estimated provider-bound request is already larger than the selected model's `context_window_tokens`, the frontend shows an error toast instead of attempting a semantic/global-summary fallback.
- The canonical message is `上下文大小超出当前模型<展示名>：按这颗模型的窗口压缩后仍装不下`. It states the fact and stops there — telling the operator to change the chain config is wrong for both remaining causes (this model's window really is that small, or this turn has nothing left to compress); the window and compression readouts live in the frame and the per-hop detail. The sibling for a record without a configured window follows the same rule: `当前模型<展示名>的模型记录没有最大上下文TOKEN，本跳没有可用压缩窗口（原因: …）`. No model-facing over-window text instructs a chain or record edit — an instruction of that shape is a write path, and this surface is read by the model.
- `<展示名>` is expected to come from the runtime-selected model's `provider_model`, with model `key` only as fallback.

### Composer Voice Input

The composer carries a mic button (`#ceo-voice-btn`, beside the attach button) that records locally, transcribes on the machine, and puts the text into the input box. The engine and its measured defaults are owned by `speech-to-text.md`; what this surface promises is the following.

- The button is a record/stop toggle and is always rendered: readiness is reported on click (with the exact `negi stt prepare` instruction) rather than by hiding the control, so a not-yet-provisioned install explains itself instead of looking broken. While recording it carries `aria-pressed="true"`, the `mic-off` glyph, and the `is-recording` class.
- **Readiness is asked before the microphone is granted.** `startCeoVoiceCapture` calls `ensureCeoVoiceEngine()` first (`GET /api/ceo/voice/status`): a box that is merely switched off gets that sentence and stops, while a box that is allowed but missing its ~157 MB starts the download in place (`POST /api/ceo/voice/prepare`, polled through the same status route) and only then opens the recorder. The reverse order would let the user speak a whole sentence before learning the model was never installed. The download runs as a single-flight background task server-side, so repeated clicks reuse it, and the toast quotes measured byte counts per stage instead of hiding behind a spinner.
- Two delivery modes, chosen by the 识别后自动发送 checkbox that appears above the input row while recording or transcribing (default on, persisted in `localStorage`):
  - **Auto send**: the text is sent immediately with the `用户语音，机器识别结果：` prefix and the user never gets a chance to edit it — which is exactly why the prefix is required here, and why this lane obeys the same contract as the channel voice lane (`speech-to-text.md`「提示词侧」). If the send lane refuses (session busy, read-only, no active session) the text stays in the composer instead of being swallowed.
  - **Manual**: the text is inserted **at the cursor** without any prefix, keeping both sides of the existing draft and replacing only the live selection. No prefix here on purpose: the user edits the result, and a "机器识别结果" label over human-corrected text would be a false statement to the model.
- Auto send is skipped when the composer already holds a draft. Silently sending text the user had not decided to send is worse than the extra click, so the result is inserted at the cursor and a toast says the auto send did not happen.
- Recording is resampled to 16 kHz mono WAV **in the browser** (`decodeAudioData` + `OfflineAudioContext`, then a hand-written WAV header), which is why this path needs no server-side decoder and why 60 s of audio fits the 2 MiB request cap.
- `POST /api/ceo/transcribe` answers `200` with an `{ok, text, error_code, error, seconds, wall_ms}` envelope for every outcome except an oversized body (`413 voice_too_large`). "No speech detected" is a valid result the composer renders, not a transport failure, so error branches must key off `error_code` and never off the HTTP status. The endpoint itself writes nothing anywhere — it is a pure transcription seam, and the `wav_bytes` the engine hands back are deliberately kept out of its JSON envelope.
- The recording becomes a session attachment **only on the auto-send lane**: after a successful transcription `deliverVoiceText` uploads the 16 kHz WAV through the ordinary `/api/ceo/uploads` seam just before the send, which is what lets the sent message render as a replayable voice bubble. Manual mode is throwaway by contract — the text is handed to the composer for editing, so keeping the audio would ship a bubble whose playback and whose (edited) transcript tell two different stories. The same holds when a draft blocks the auto send. Upload failure is swallowed on purpose — the worst case degrades to a plain text bubble, which beats losing the recognized text. Where the bytes end up and for how long: `speech-to-text.md`「语音气泡与音频字段的存放」.
- The transcription runs off the event loop and is serialized by one global slot, so a long note cannot stall the live CEO websocket lane or overlap a second inference; a concurrent request waits and then receives `stt_busy`.
- `getUserMedia` requires a secure context. `http://127.0.0.1:<port>` works; opening the panel over `http://<lan-ip>` makes the browser refuse the microphone, and the toast says so explicitly.

### Composer Model Mode Panel

The context-usage brain is also the composer's model-and-context entry: clicking it (or Enter/Space on it) opens an upward panel above the composer. The panel leads with the model actually in use and its context numbers, then the `模型链` / `指定模型` choice, then the matching pane. There is no separate model-mode button beside the brain — the numbers live in the panel so they are not shown twice. The one hover text on the brain is the `长按压缩上下文` affordance hint (see `Manual Context Compression`).

- The headline is a single left-aligned pill carrying the display title of the model actually in use. Resolution order is `resolved_model_key` (the binding key the runtime selected for this request) first, then `provider_model`, each resolved against the managed catalog (`name` > `key` > `provider_model`), falling back to the session's pinned binding title, and to `等待 Leader 上下文预估` when neither is known. Binding key first is load-bearing: several bindings can carry the same provider-side model name, and a `provider_model`-only lookup resolves against whichever catalog entry happens to share that string, so the pill labels the wrong binding. The pill is muted (`is-pending`) while it shows a placeholder. Below it sit a ratio bar and an `estimated/total TOKEN` line rendered from the same estimate the brain uses (see `Composer Context Usage Meter`); the model name is never repeated under the bar, and that whole line is hidden when there is no estimate rather than echoing the placeholder a second time.
- Which mode is in effect is shown by the `模型链` / `指定模型` radio state, not by the headline pill.
- The open pane follows the mode actually in effect: a session pinned to a model opens on `指定模型`, a chain session opens on `模型链`, and a pinned state that arrives after opening switches the pane over. A manual pane choice wins for the rest of that opening; closing discards it.
- `模型链` pane opens read-only and has two modes. Read-only mode: one compact row per binding showing only its display title, no drag handle, no remove control, rows not draggable, and the action row holds a single `编辑` button; the list shows five rows before scrolling. What it shows is the chain in effect for this session — the session chain when one has been saved, otherwise `models.roles.ceo`. Opening the panel fires both reads (`GET /api/models` for the catalog and the session `GET .../model-selection`) because the catalog is otherwise only re-read at page load, on entering the model page, and by that page's refresh button: a tab left open across a global-chain change would otherwise label a chain that does not take effect as `模型链（全局）`. A session that never edited a chain therefore tracks the global chain live, new sessions included.
- `编辑` is the only way into edit mode, and edit mode is the only place anything can be changed: rows gain a drag handle plus a remove control and become draggable, and the action row becomes `同步全局模型链` / `加入模型` / `保存`. Those three all work on the draft and send nothing — dragging reorders, `加入模型` opens the catalog list in append purpose, `同步全局模型链` re-reads `GET /api/models` and lays that chain out as the draft. `保存` is disabled until the draft differs from the chain in effect (an affordance that only appears after the first change gives no way to make that change), and it is the one control that writes: the draft goes through `PATCH /api/ceo/sessions/{session_id}/model-selection` as `{mode: "chain", model_keys: [...]}`, so a save affects this session only and never touches `models.roles.ceo`. A successful write drops the pane back to read-only showing what takes effect; a failed write stays in edit mode with the draft re-seeded from the chain in effect, so the pane never displays a chain that did not land.
- The save drops the session chain instead of storing a copy whenever the draft is not a deviation: an empty draft, or one that matches `models.roles.ceo` position by position, is written as `{mode: "chain"}` without `model_keys` — the same write as `恢复全局模型链`, so the backend removes the `model_selection` key and the session keeps tracking the global chain. Only a draft that differs is written as `{mode: "chain", model_keys: [...]}`. That is what makes `同步全局模型链` + `保存` mean "follow the global chain" rather than "freeze today's copy of it", and it is why an emptied draft still has somewhere to go.
- The `模型链` tab names whose chain it is: `模型链（此会话）` while the session setting answers `is_session_chain: true`, `模型链（全局）` otherwise. The label reads server state and never the draft, so an unsaved edit cannot be misread as already in effect.
- Closing the panel discards an unapplied draft and leaves edit mode; switching between the two panes must do neither — a dirty draft is kept as-is and only a clean one is re-seeded from the chain in effect. The same rule covers the two reads that land while the panel is open: a background response re-seeds the list only while the pane is read-only, never into a draft the operator is editing, and `ensureCeoModelCatalog({ force: true })` is what the panel open uses (the non-forced call only fills an empty catalog).
- Two invariants keep the pane usable and both are easy to break from the inside: (a) every control click inside these panes (`编辑` / `同步全局模型链` / `加入模型` / a row's remove control / a catalog item) stops propagation, because the global `clicked outside the panel` judge reads `e.target.closest("#ceo-model-mode-panel")` and a re-render performed inside a click handler detaches the event target; (b) the append path returns to the chain pane itself instead of leaving the catalog list open, so the added row is what the user sees next. The global chain remains editable only on the model page (see `Model Config Page And Admin Contract`).
- A chain whose members mix image-capable and image-incapable bindings is confirmed before submit, in both outlets (the session `保存` button and the model page's role-chain save, for `ceo` / `execution` / `inspection` — `memory` has no image surface at all, so warning there would be a false claim). Load-balance group slots are expanded to their members first: a mixed member inside a group has the same consequence as one written directly on the chain. The dialog names the bindings that cannot take images so the operator can go fix exactly those; an all-incapable chain is not warned about, since nothing is lost by that save. The backend accepts the mixed chain without a guard — the confirm is the operator-facing decision point, and the judgment itself is owned by `Image Upload Gating`.
- Choosing `模型链` while the session is pinned first shows an inline confirm row above the list (`当前会话固定使用 <展示名>，切换到模型链？`) with accept/cancel buttons; only accept sends the `chain` patch, cancel returns to the pinned pane. A session already on the chain switches panes without any confirm.
- `指定模型` pane: the managed binding catalog rendered by display title; disabled bindings render with a `已禁用` chip and are not selectable. It carries a search box filtering on display title, `key`, and `provider_model`. The same list serves two purposes and they must not be conflated: in pin purpose a click fixes the session to that binding; in append purpose (opened by `加入模型`) a click appends the binding to the chain draft, bindings already in the chain are not listed, and the panel stays on the list so several members can be added in a row. Opening the pane on its own always means pin purpose.
- Reading and writing the session setting goes through `GET` / `PATCH /api/ceo/sessions/{session_id}/model-selection`. The payload is `{mode: "chain"}` (follow the global chain), `{mode: "chain", model_keys: [...]}` (session chain), or `{mode: "model", model_key: "<binding key>"}`. Responses carry `model_keys`, `is_session_chain`, and `chain_unavailable_keys` — members not in the catalog or disabled, which the runtime drops one by one while the stored chain stays intact. A chain write rejects an unknown member with 404 and a disabled one with 409, naming the offending key in an object `detail` (`code` + `model_key`) instead of filtering it out silently; `model` mode without a key is 400 `model_key_required`.
- The setting is stored in the session's metadata (`model_selection`) and persists across restart. Local web sessions run through `normalize_ceo_metadata`; channel sessions (`ext:` / `china:`) are resolved from their session file and get the key added or removed without the web-CEO metadata normalization, so channel-owned metadata is left intact.
- `pinned_available: false` in the response means the pinned key does not exist or is disabled. The panel then reports the effective mode (`模型链`, with the chain pane) and explains the automatic fallback in a note; the stored pin itself is kept so a re-enabled or re-created key restores the pin. Resolution semantics (固定模型 > 会话链 > 全局角色链, and how invalid members are dropped) are owned by `config-and-models.md`「会话级模型链与固定模型」.
- Picking a model applies from the next turn: the browser sends nothing extra on `client.user_message`, the runtime resolves the pin when the turn starts. After a successful model switch or chain reorder the browser re-runs the composer usage estimate so the meter and headline reflect the new model's context window.

### Composer Risk Panel

会话页输入行左下那排控件里，语音按钮右边那颗盾形按钮是 CEO 审批档位的唯一入口。它打开的面板标题是「中高风险工具审批」，里面从上到下依次是：`命令行风险拦截设置`（一颗入口按钮）、`全部放行` / `条件放行` 两颗互斥选择、只在条件放行下出现的`白名单`入口，以及一句说明当前档位后果的注。后端判据与 REST 载荷归 `tool-and-skill-system.md`「CEO Regulatory Governance Mode」，这里只记界面合同。

- **档位是一份全局设置，不是会话设置。** 面板打开时读一次服务端读数，页面启动时也读一次，所以按钮的着色反映的是当前生效值，与"这是哪个会话"无关。改动立刻对所有在跑与新建的会话生效，界面因此不需要按会话缓存副本，也不许在切换会话时把读数换回本地缓存。
- **按钮着色只有一档：全部放行时发黄。** 黄色在这套界面里已经是"需要人来处理"那一族（模型链重试提示、侧栏待审批环），这里指的是"这一档不拦任何工具调用"，即风险敞口打开着。条件放行下按钮不着色。选择态本身由面板里那两颗 radio 的 `aria-checked` 表达，不靠按钮颜色重复一遍。
- **同尺寸与同锚点**：这颗按钮与脑图标、附件、语音共用输入区那档 40px 控件（`#ceo-risk-btn` 必须与那三颗一起列在同一条 id 规则里——基础类给的是 58px 档，只有 id 选择器压得住它），面板与模型面板同锚点向上弹出，因此打开一个会显式关掉另一个；点面板外收面板，点面板内不收（面板自己的 click handler 需要 stopPropagation，全局"点外面"判据读的是 document 上的 click）。
- **`命令行风险拦截设置`在会话页就地弹出 exec 工具详情**，走的是与 Tool 页列表同一颗 `openTool("exec")`，**不换视图**。抽屉本来是坐在 `#view-tools` 里的 `position:fixed` 居中层，而会话页时那个 section 是 `display:none`——直接调 `openTool` 等于把窗开在隐藏容器上（症状：点了没反应），先 `switchView("tools")` 又能弹出但会把操作员从会话里带走。现行做法与模型配置里那份 popover 同源：开之前把遮罩与抽屉两个节点搬到 `document.body`（`hoistToolDetailLayer`），节点句柄按元素取所以不受搬动影响，Tool 页自己用它时形状完全一样。风险面板这边不保存、不渲染任何 exec 字段。
- **白名单弹窗**列出 `GET /api/resources/tools` 的那份工具记录，勾选态按 `governance-mode` 回的 `whitelist_tools` 画，界面不自己按风险度派生默认档（派生只在服务端做，`whitelist_customized` 告诉界面这份名单是存过的还是默认的）。行是长内容：列表盒子按视口封顶并自滚，弹窗外壳不当滚动口。保存只提交勾上的那几项，走同一个 `PUT governance-mode` 的 `whitelist_tools` 字段，不带 `enabled`——两颗档位按钮与名单是两个动作，一次点击只改一件事。
- 与审批卡片（见「2.2. Regulatory Approval Flow」）是两条面：那张是"这一批要不要放行"的运行时阻塞态，这颗按钮是"以后要不要拦"的设置态，两者互不读写对方的状态。

### Composer Context Usage Meter

- The Leader composer has a live-only context-size signal: a brain-shaped usage meter beside the attachment button (not a textarea border), always visible and clickable — it opens the model-and-context panel (see `Composer Model Mode Panel`). It is backend-driven with two authority lanes that maintainers must keep separate:
  - Idle/non-running sessions: the browser debounces composer edits and calls `POST /api/ceo/sessions/{session_id}/composer-preflight`, whose payload represents the next outbound user batch (existing queued follow-ups plus the current unsent draft/attachments, in FIFO order). An empty composer still calls it with no messages: with nothing pending, the preflight rebuilds the durable baseline itself, so a session that already has history keeps showing a real figure instead of dropping back to the placeholder. Only a session with no history at all is treated as having nothing to read, and only then may the meter show `等待 Leader 上下文预估`. The response carries the current model-facing estimate and thresholds: `estimated_total_tokens`, `context_window_tokens`, `ratio`, `provider_model`, `resolved_model_key`, `trigger_tokens`, `would_trigger_token_compression`, `would_exceed_context_window`. For multimodal drafts the estimate derives image cost from a dedicated image-token heuristic plus text/schema cost — never from the raw `data:image/*;base64` string length, so a large inline data URL must not inflate into millions of text tokens.
