The figure shown here is the same send preflight the runtime uses, so it is usage-first by the same contract (see `runtime-overview.md`「Frontdoor Context Compression」): the last provider call's effective input — corrected back by whatever the stage window has since compacted out of it — plus an estimate of the newly added content, with the full preview estimate only as the fallback when no comparable usage truth exists. A meter that reads consistently lower than the last request's real input size means the comparison fell back to preview — debug the append-only comparison, not the estimator; a meter that spikes at a stage-boundary hop and settles one hop later means that anchor correction is missing (both 详见 `context-and-cache-troubleshooting.md`「同 turn 的 append-only 规则被破坏」). The preflight only reads the session's own baseline, so it is not gated by the read-only flag: a channel (`ext:` / `china:`) session gets a figure the same way, and must not be answered with `channel_session_readonly` — a read-only feed is not a reason for the meter to sit empty.
  - Running sessions: composer-preflight stops being authoritative and is not called. The valid source is the current `snapshot.ceo` / `ceo.turn.patch` inflight diagnostics — `frontdoor_token_preflight_diagnostics.final_request_tokens`, `.max_context_tokens`, `.provider_model`, `.resolved_model_key`. The visible meter during a running turn means "the actual next provider-bound request the runtime is about to send", not "draft if sent now"; issuing a fresh browser-side estimate during a running turn is a contract bug. Retention is the exception and is required: between turn start and the first runtime diagnostics, `ceoCurrentUsageEstimate()` keeps displaying the last known reading instead of blanking to `等待 Leader 上下文预估` — a meter that drops to the placeholder every time the model is called reads as "context was cleared". Any runtime value wins over the retained one the moment it arrives.
- The icon's color is a live-activity signal, not a static gauge: `--ceo-context-usage-color` carries the ratio-driven green→red hue only while the session is in progress (`S.ceoTurnActive` or an active compression, manual or in-turn). An idle session renders the brain in `var(--text-muted)` — a saturated green sitting on a session that is doing nothing gets misread as ongoing consumption. Greying the color does not grey the meter: the fill height, the panel numbers and the `aria-label` all keep reflecting the ratio.
- The ratio reads as a water level inside the brain, not as a recolored outline: `.ceo-context-usage-brain-fill-inner` is a solid `currentColor` block masked by a closed silhouette of the brain's outer boundary (`center bottom / 24px 24px no-repeat`), so the used portion is one mass whose flat top edge *is* the level line. The brain glyph is drawn once, by the base layer, which keeps the whole outline — folds included — and paints above the water (`z-index: 2`): rising level recolors the interior space only and never eats a stroke, and the only value JavaScript drives is the water height. The mask's `d` is hand-joined from the lucide `brain` outer arcs and is fattened 1px outward (`stroke-width: 2` on the mask path) to line up with the outer edge of the 2px outline — if the glyph is ever swapped, that path must be re-joined, otherwise the water either leaks past the outline or leaves a ring of empty space inside it.
- With no data the shell keeps its faint glow while the colored fill stays `height 0` until an estimate arrives ("always lit" refers to the shell, not a fabricated fill). The shell keeps an `aria-label` of `model · estimated/total TOKEN`, where the model part is the same display title the panel pill resolves (see `Composer Model Mode Panel`) and not the raw `provider_model` (`等待 Leader 上下文预估` only when there is no reading at all) and no native `title`; the same numbers are surfaced once, in the panel. Hovering shows only the `长按压缩上下文` affordance hint, and a completed hold opens nothing: its edge progress bar reaching full is what fires the compression request (see `Manual Context Compression`).
- The brain meter is live-only UI state: it animates with the current ratio, clamps visual fill when the raw ratio exceeds `1.0`, and never creates transcript messages, assistant bubbles, or persisted snapshot entries.
- If the meter disagrees with real send-time compression/error behavior, first check whether the browser is in the idle preflight lane or the running snapshot lane, then debug the corresponding backend source. The runtime's additive image-estimation fields (`estimated_image_tokens`, `image_estimation_method`) are observability only and do not change the persisted transcript contract.

## Tool Admin RBAC Contract

Tool management uses a strict persisted-RBAC contract with **one row per surfaced tool**. See `tool-and-skill-system.md`「Tool Admin RBAC」 for the backend semantics: policy seeding, empty-list preservation and one-time repair, and `exec` execution mode. CEO 审批档位与免审名单**不在这一页**：页头没有档位开关，那套设置住在会话页的风险面板（见「Composer Risk Panel」），后端判据见 `tool-and-skill-system.md`「CEO Regulatory Governance Mode」。

The Tool page lists tools, not groups: one row per `tool_id`, carrying its own status / risk / 核心工具 tags, and the detail drawer holds that one tool's role toggles, its own toolskill, and its own enable & delete controls. Records with `agent_visible: false` stay out of the list. `GET /api/resources/tools` returns the same per-tool records, and every write endpoint is keyed by `tool_id`.

The frontend responsibilities are:

- reflect the backend-owned `allowed_roles` exactly,
- allow every surfaced tool's role toggles to be unchecked, including a core tool's,
- keep `删除` unavailable for core tools (`core_tool_delete_forbidden`) — that is the only remaining core protection, and a disabled core tool stays disabled because nothing force-enables it,
- and show a clear operator-visible hint when a tool is currently disabled for all roles.

Save payload is `PUT /api/resources/tools/{tool_id}` with `{"enabled": bool, "allowed_roles": [...], "execution_mode": "governed|full_access"?}`; `execution_mode` belongs to `exec` alone.

The Skill and Tool detail drawers share one save rule: edits persist only when the operator clicks the `保存` button beside `关闭`, and that click is what issues the policy/file write. There is no autosave or debounce, and closing the drawer discards uncommitted edits. New maintainers often misread the draft hint (`有未保存的修改`) as an autosave indicator — it only marks dirty state that a `保存` click must commit.

If an operator reports "save succeeded but reopen restored the roles", first inspect:

1. the Tool Admin save payload,
2. the stored `tools.payload_json` row for that tool,
3. the post-reload `GET /api/resources/tools/{tool_id}` response.

Do not start with frontdoor prompt debugging unless those three layers already agree.

If an operator reports "Tool 管理 shows one role set but runtime visibility behaves differently", inspect in this order:

1. `GET /api/resources/tools/{tool_id}`
2. the stored `tools.payload_json` row
3. the derived `role_policy_matrix`
4. the runtime-side `list_effective_tool_names(...)` result for the affected role/session

If roles look like shipped defaults right after an upgrade of an existing install, that is the one-time governance migration: the stored group-shaped rows are expanded per tool and the operator's `enabled` / per-action roles ride along (`tool-and-skill-system.md`「Tool Admin RBAC」). It runs once when the governance store opens; `governance_meta.tool_granularity_migration_v1_applied` records how many rows it wrote.

Do not assume the browser has a second hidden RBAC source. For surfaced tools, the API detail payload and runtime visibility are two views over the same backend-owned per-tool state.

### Resource Delete Lane（工具 / Skill 删除）

删除是唯一会真的动磁盘的管理写操作：`DELETE /api/resources/tools/{tool_id}` 与 `DELETE /api/resources/skills/{skill_id}` 先 `rmtree` 安装目录，再重建资源树、清角色策略。MCP 工具的安装目录常带 `node_modules`，Windows 上一次 `rmtree` 十几到几十秒都正常，所以这条车道有三条硬约定：

- **服务端把目录删除放进工作线程**（`MainRuntimeService._delete_ordered_paths` 经 `asyncio.to_thread` 调用），资源树刷新与治理收尾仍留在事件循环线程上。反过来的代价是整个 web 服务（含 websocket 推送与其它管理请求）在一次删除期间全部停摆。
- **客户端不给删除请求设超时**（`ApiClient.deleteTool` / `deleteSkill` 传 `timeoutMs: 0`）。默认 10s 超时会把已经删成功的请求报成 `Request timeout`——实盘症状是"点删除弹 timeout，刷新后工具已经没了"。删除期间详情抽屉的按钮显示 `删除中...`，toast 是常驻的 spinner 并逐秒累计已用时间，没有关闭键：它是唯一的进度信号。
- **失败判据是资源还在不在，不是请求顺不顺**。请求报错（断连、服务重启、用户切页）后前端回读一次列表复核，只有资源仍在才提示删除失败；请求成功返回才清选择并提示已删除。真正的业务拒绝（`tool_in_use` / `tool_busy` / `core_tool_delete_forbidden`，均 409）资源仍在，按失败提示。

排查"删了但列表还在"：先看 `DELETE` 的响应体 `deleted_paths`，再看 `resources.tools` 计数是否下降，最后确认 `_resource_is_busy` 没有把这次删除挡在 409 上。删除对会话/节点 `tools[]` 的影响见 `tool-and-skill-system.md`「Provider Tool Surface」：删除流程在 `rmtree` 之前把该工具的 provider schema 抄进快照（`g3ku/resources/retired_schemas.py`），所以下一跳的 `tools[]` 与上一跳逐字节相同、可复用前缀不断，尾块用 `denied_tools` 点名它"这一跳调不动"。名字真正离开清单仍只在压缩/空闲这类重印点——删一次工具不该把身后整段正文的缓存重付一遍。

## Exec Approval And Allowlist Admin Contract

`exec` 的破坏性命令守卫把「这条命令要不要人放行」交给运维：待审批清单、放行/拒绝动作与命令白名单都住在 Web 管理面这一侧。

入口在 **exec 工具详情的「执行模式」区块里**（那颗「命令白名单」按钮与它的待审批徽标），不在 Tool 页头部——命令白名单是这颗工具自己的设置，和 `governed` / `full_access` 同一屏。会话页风险面板里那颗「命令行风险拦截设置」也走这条：它打开的就是 exec 工具详情抽屉，不另开一份界面。徽标节点由详情重绘重建，所以待审批计数在抽屉每次重绘后由治理侧回灌一次，而不是等下一轮轮询。

端点族由 admin router 提供，实际路径是 `/api/resources/tools/...`——`g3ku/web/main.py` 以 `prefix='/api'` 挂载 `main_router`，而 router 内部的路径段本身**不带** `admin`：

- `GET /api/resources/tools/exec-approvals?status=pending&limit=` → `{ok, items}`
- `POST /api/resources/tools/exec-approvals/{approval_id}/decision` → `{ok, item}`，`decision` 取 `approve_once` / `approve_whitelist` / `deny`
- `GET` / `POST /api/resources/tools/exec-command-whitelist`、`POST /api/resources/tools/exec-command-whitelist/delete` → `{ok, items, approval_wait_seconds}` / `{ok, item}` / `{ok, removed}`
- `PUT /api/resources/tools/exec-approval-wait` → `{ok, approval_wait_seconds}`

这一族在治理取不到时返回 **503**（`exec_approvals_unavailable` / `main_task_service_unavailable`），不是空清单：审批台账挂在 `main_task_service` 上，服务缺席就不存在「零条待审批」这个读数。运维侧的判法因此分三态——徽标有数＝真有待审批；徽标无数＝队里确实空；接口 5xx＝这条面没接上。三者不许混成同一个「0」。

新维护者最容易踩的是前缀：`/api/admin/resources/tools/...` 看起来完全合理（文件名是 admin、路由注册在 admin router 上），但它没有对应路由，请求 404 而页面把失败读成「没有待审批」。`tests/resources/test_web_api_path_parity.py` 拿真实挂载的 app 路由表核对前端每一条字面调用，钉的就是这条；新增端点时两侧都要过它，不要在测试里另抄一份前缀当事实源。

## Container Deployment Contract

The web/admin stack has an explicit container-safe startup mode.

- `negi web --no-worker` is the container-safe web entrypoint.
- In this mode, the web process still owns FastAPI routes, websocket session/runtime integration, heartbeat startup, and cron startup.
- Detached task execution is expected to come from a separate `negi worker` process or container rather than from the web-managed local child worker path.

The default local (non-`--no-worker`) path runs a web-managed task worker with auto-restart supervision; its lease/heartbeat/stale semantics and troubleshooting are owned by `operations-and-maintenance.md`「托管 worker 看门狗」.

`/api/bootstrap/status` is also the preferred healthcheck-friendly read endpoint for the web container:

- it is available even when the project is still locked
- it reports both bootstrap mode and runtime readiness
- Compose healthchecks should use it instead of inventing a second ad-hoc web-only route

There is also a new mutable-resource startup boundary maintainers should keep in mind:

- container images may ship immutable baseline `skills/` and `tools/`
- runtime startup may seed missing baseline files into the mutable workspace copy
- that seed path must be missing-file-only and must not overwrite operator edits already present in the shared workspace volume

If operators report "the image has the new built-in skill/tool but the running project still shows the old workspace copy", debug the persistent `skills/` / `tools/` volume contents first. In container mode, the mounted workspace copy is authoritative after startup.

## Heartbeat/Cron ACK Contract

The browser handles a dedicated live-only ACK event for internal turns that called `silent`.

- `ceo.internal.ack` is emitted when a heartbeat or cron turn ends by calling the `silent` tool; detection reads the `silent_reply` flag on `message_end`, never the reply text. It is not a normal assistant reply and must not reuse `ceo.reply.final` persistence or rendering rules.
- The frontend renders the ACK as a distinct non-conversational bubble so operators can see the internal turn was received and intentionally stayed silent.
- That ACK bubble is ephemeral: it is not appended to the CEO session snapshot `messages` list and disappears on full refresh.
- Heartbeat `task_terminal` turns do not reach the browser as `ceo.internal.ack`; `ceo.turn.discard` still only closes a specific visible pending turn by `turn_id`.
- Non-silent heartbeat/cron assistant replies use the ordinary `message_end -> ceo.reply.final` path.

### CEO Turn Silent Reply Contract

A turn that calls `silent` is visible in the session box as one collapsed line, not as an absent turn.

- `ceo.reply.final` carries the real reply text plus `silent_reply: true` and `silent_reason`; the backend does not blank the text on its way out. Delivery suppression is the flag's job, and the channel/cron lanes read the same flag, so the web surface and the channel surface stay consistent.
- The whole response collapses into one slim line, `静默消息 HH:MM:SS` with a caret (the label falls back to bare `静默消息` when no completion time is available). That line is a direct child of the turn element, **outside** `.msg-content`, so a collapsed silent turn shows no bubble at all — not a bubble containing one line. Expanding reveals the reply text, stage rail, tool steps and usage as on a delivered turn.
- The `silent` call itself is never rendered as a step: `applyCeoToolEventToTurn` drops the live event and `filterCeoInteractionFlowSummary` drops it from every round before the rail is rebuilt (a round left with no other tool disappears with it). Showing "模型调用了 silent" adds no information once the fold line already says the turn was silent, and while the tool was unresolvable it read as a task error.
- Collapse is CSS-only: `renderCeoSilentTurn` adds `ceo-silent-message` to the turn element and the caret toggles `ceo-silent-expanded`; `.ceo-silent-message:not(.ceo-silent-expanded) > :not(.ceo-silent-line) { display: none }` hides the bubble. Nothing is moved in the DOM and `assistant-text` keeps `hidden = false`, so the fold never fights the loading-state classes.
- The turn element is a flex **row** by default (and `.message.message` adds `align-items: flex-end`), so the silent rule must also set `flex-direction: column; align-items: stretch` — without it the fold line lands beside the bubble and squeezes the content right. The rule repeats `.message` in its selector (`<code>.message.message.ceo-silent-message</code>`): a bare `.ceo-silent-message` loses to `.message.message` on specificity.
- There is no separate reason row. For a tool-silenced turn the reply body already *is* the reason (finalize keeps `accompanying_text or reason`), so a second line duplicated it. The reason stays on the transcript row as `silent_reason` for audit.
- The line is created once and held on the turn object (`silentLineEl`), because a turn renders live and then finalizes; it is assembled with DOM nodes and `textContent` — this frontend has no HTML-escaping helper, so interpolating model text into `innerHTML` would open an injection path.
- `renderPersistedCeoAssistantTurn` must pass `silent_reason` into its trailing `finalizeCeoTurn` call: finalize rewrites the cached row from `meta`, and a missing reason there blanks that row's `silent_reason` on refresh.
- A visible turn that ends with no text at all is silent too and renders the same line with a runtime-authored reason and nothing to expand (contract: `runtime-overview.md`「3.3 静默回复（`silent` 工具）」). That shape must not surface an internal fallback sentence (such as "no visible reply was generated") as an ordinary reply: a text-less visible turn is silent, so the collapsed line above is its only rendering.
- Snapshot rows keep their content for the same reason, so a refresh rebuilds the same collapsed line.
- A silent row with no rail still renders: the collapsed line is itself displayable content. Dropping track-less silent rows was correct only while the backend also emptied their text.
- `finalizeCeoTurn` and `renderPersistedCeoAssistantTurn` are the two render entry points; both branch on `silent_reply`, and the turn rail, tool steps, usage line and completion timestamp are finalized exactly as on a delivered turn.

## Update Notification And Restart-And-Upgrade Contract

This surface tells an installed device that a newer release tag exists and lets the operator apply it from the browser. It owns the web-side reading of one file: the update ledger written by the periodic release-tag check (polling cadence and the check channel are owned by `operations-and-maintenance.md`「新设备首次安装与升级」).

**Endpoints** (`main/api/update_rest.py`, mounted under `/api`):

- `GET /api/update/status` — reads the ledger only. It never touches the network, so the 30s UI poll costs nothing and an offline device keeps answering from the last known result.
- `POST /api/update/check` — one forced manual check (`source=manual`), bypassing the interval gate.
- `POST /api/update/apply` — spawns the detached upgrade runner and returns. It performs no shutdown itself. A body `ref` is used as given (no remote lookup, and it is shape-checked before anything spawns). With no `ref`, the endpoint resolves the target from a **live** tag check rather than from the ledger: one `git ls-remote` as `source=manual`, bypassing the interval gate. A stale ledger is never an upgrade target — after a restart the periodic pass may still hold the previous tag, so applying it would move the device onto an older release than the one that exists. When that check yields no tag the request aborts with `409 check_failed` and spawns nothing; a fresh check that reports `newer=false` aborts with `409 no_update_available`. The click therefore costs at most one remote read and cannot act on information the device already knows is out of date.

**The tri-state invariant.** `has_ledger=false` means "never checked", `error` non-empty means "checked and failed", and `newer=true` is the only state that may light anything. Rendering the first two as "up to date" — or as a red dot — is the failure mode this contract exists to prevent, because it makes an offline device indistinguishable from a current one. The settings line therefore has four distinct texts (never checked / last check failed / newer available / up to date), and the sidebar dot plus the 「重启并更新」 button stay hidden unless `newer` is true. `newer` here is **recomputed on every read** by `g3ku.update_check.ledger_newer_now()`: the ledger's `latest_tag` against the version the running process carries. The same-named field stored in the ledger records what the last check concluded and is never the display input, because an upgrade swaps the code without rewriting the ledger — the stored value keeps reporting "behind" until the next check lands, and the automatic pass is a 300-cycle tick further gated by `checked_at`, so a just-upgraded device would otherwise sit lit for a full interval. The startup banner in `g3ku/web/launcher.py` reads through the same function and prints the live version, and `POST /api/update/apply` without a `ref` resolves from a check it just ran, where both readings coincide by construction.

**Where it lives in the UI.** The dot (`#update-nav-dot`) is a static span inside `#project-settings-btn`, absolutely positioned at the button's top-left corner; that button needs `position: relative` to be its containing block, otherwise the dot anchors to an outer container. The panel row reuses the existing `project-settings-actions` shape (button + one status line), and the poll rides the audit badge's existing 30s interval instead of adding a timer.

**Unlock boundary.** All three endpoints sit behind the `423 project_locked` guard like any other `/api/*` route. A locked project shows no dot and issues no remote request: the check loop only starts once the web runtime bus exists.

**Apply is user-initiated, once.** The button posts, the runner stops the service through the existing `POST /api/bootstrap/exit` (so "running work needs confirmation" has exactly one implementation), waits for the port to be released, upgrades the checkout, then relaunches. If the upgrade fails it relaunches the previous version rather than leaving the device without a service — the sequence and its rationale (an interpreter must never import source mid-replacement) are in `operations-and-maintenance.md`「新设备首次安装与升级」.

**The apply outcome is the runner's to report, not the endpoint's.** `POST /api/update/apply` answers `restarting: true` the moment it spawns the runner, which says nothing about whether the upgrade ever happened — a refused exit or a dirty tree finishes the runner within seconds while the page still reads "重启并更新中". The runner therefore writes its state to `<data root>/update-apply-result.json` (`{at, ref, outcome, detail}`; outcomes `started / ok / upgrade_failed / exit_refused / port_busy / port_unknown / not_this_service`), `GET /api/update/status` exposes it verbatim as `last_apply`, and the settings row yields its version pair to a truthful one-line failure text while the raw code, time and detail go to the row's title. `ok` and an absent file both leave the version pair alone, so a device that never applied anything is not shown as failed. This file is separate from the check ledger: the ledger answers "是否更新了版本", this answers "上一次更新动作停在哪一步", and neither renders the other's unknown as success.

**The same file carries the stage trail, and it is the only progress channel.** Every step the runner takes rewrites the record with `outcome="started"` plus `stage` / `stage_at` / `elapsed_seconds` (`requesting_exit → waiting_exit → upgrading → relaunching`, then the terminal outcome). Intermediate steps must keep `outcome="started"` because the single-flight gate keys on exactly that value — a stage written as a new outcome would let a second click spawn a second runner mid-upgrade. While `stage="upgrading"` the runner refreshes the record on each heartbeat, which is the only window where the service is down and therefore the only thing the page can still talk about.

**What the page may and may not claim.** `watchApplyUpdateFinish` polls `/api/update/status` on its own interval and drives one persistent toast: the stage name from the record plus a second counter the **page** ticks from its own click timestamp. During the outage the poll throws and is swallowed — the toast keeps the last stage it successfully read and the seconds keep advancing, which is what distinguishes "working" from "frozen". Two rules follow from that split: the displayed seconds are the page's, never the runner's `elapsed_seconds` (mixing them would print two clocks for one concept), and no percentage is ever shown, because nothing in this lane measures bytes. On `outcome="ok"` with a matching version the toast says so and reloads the page — and the branch returns first, since `location.reload()` returns immediately and a later progress toast would overwrite the success one. Failure and the 11-minute timeout both surface as a persistent error toast naming the stage and the runner's own detail line.

**Success is feedback too, and the page recovers itself.** Because the confirmation row promises "服务回来本页会自动恢复", the client watches for the round trip instead of waiting for a manual reload: a successful `apply` response hands its own `ref` to a watcher that polls `GET /api/update/status` every 3s, and when the service answers again with `last_apply.outcome === "ok"` **and** `current_version` equal to that ref, it raises a `已更新到 v…` toast and reloads the page once. A terminal failure outcome produces an error toast carrying the runner's detail and stops the watch; unreachable service simply keeps waiting until the watch's own deadline. The watcher's interval and timeout are parameters so the round trip is testable without a real restart.

**Apply is single-flight.** While `last_apply.outcome` is `started` and its `at` is within `APPLY_IN_FLIGHT_SECONDS` (600), a second `POST /api/update/apply` is refused with `409 apply_in_flight` before any remote lookup or spawn: two overlapping runners each run the installer and each relaunch the service, so the later relaunch reaps the earlier one and the device sits without a service for minutes, and the two processes also overwrite each other's lines in the shared append handle. A record whose timestamp cannot be parsed, or one older than the window, does not lock the lane — a killed runner must not permanently disable updates.
